# Verified products: drive role (Performer 8 replacements)

**Scope:** Products that could take over Performer 8's desire, stress and testosterone role without its caffeine: fenugreek, ashwagandha, tongkat ali, saffron and multi-herb formulas.

**Method:** Each product was checked by a verification agent (label and directions, certification in the certifier's own database, regulatory record, dose against human trials, safety, interactions, who should avoid it, stop signals) and then audited by two challenge agents, one on certification and label claims and one on safety and evidence. Captures date from 7–8 November 2026. Records are listed in the order of `reports/Prelox and Performer 8 replacements.md`.

## Takeaway

- MST Testofen 600 mg is the only drive product whose certifier (BSCG) confirms screening for sildenafil-type drugs, and fenugreek has the lowest LiverTox liver-injury rating of the drive herbs.
- Core Med Science KSM-66 600 mg is NSF Certified for Sport for lot 119510125 only (30-capsule bottle).
- Ashwagandha is a likely cause of liver injury (2–12 weeks after starting) and raises T4; never combine two ashwagandha products, or one with Performer 8.
- Puresport's Tongkat Ali and Ultra Ashwagandha lost Informed Sport certification on 10 Oct 2026.

## Gaps

- FDA's warning-letter, recall and tainted-product pages and the Health Canada, TGA and MHRA databases were blocked or unreachable for most products, so a missing regulatory action means "not checked", not "clean".
- Several certifier pages were seen only through search-engine copies; the report marks these as indirect, and the live page must be checked against the bottle's lot number.
- Informed Sport and NSF Certified for Sport do not publish whether their panels cover PDE5 drugs (sildenafil, tadalafil and analogues); BSCG's published menu does.
- No prices or costs were collected, by design.

## Records

### Report rank 1: MST (Millennium Sport Technologies) — Testofen 600 mg fenugreek seed extract (standardized to 50% Fenuside), 30 vegetable capsules (also sold as a twin pack)

Two or more verification batches checked this product independently; all 2 records are kept so their differences stay visible.

#### Verification record 1 of 2

- **Verifier verdict:** recommend — It is a single ingredient with its amount disclosed, at exactly the 600 mg/day used in both Testofen trials, one of which was in men aged 43-70. Every lot is BSCG Certified Drug Free with a screen that includes PDE5-inhibitor drugs, and lots up to 19 Aug 2026 are listed in BSCG's own database. It has no stimulant or hormone, a reassuring liver-safety score and no regulatory red flags found. The benefit is modest and industry-funded, so treat it as a 12-week libido trial, not an ED treatment.
- **Product as verified:** MST (Millennium Sport Technologies) — Testofen 600 mg fenugreek seed extract (standardized to 50% Fenuside), 30 vegetable capsules (also sold as a twin pack)
- **Product page:** https://www.amazon.com/Testofen-Fenugreek-Standardized-FenusideTM-Certified/dp/B08NZ39151
- **Where sold:** US: Amazon and Walmart (Millennium Sport brand page). Gulf: only through cross-border marketplaces (desertcart Kuwait, whlsome's Arabic storefront); no Gulf pharmacy stock confirmed. UK/EU: MST's product not confirmed there. Myprotein sells its own Testofen capsules in the UK, EU, UAE and elsewhere, but their dose and certification were not verified, and an Informed Sport search for 'fenugreek' returned 0 results.
- **Label verified:** no. MST's own Amazon listing copy, captured 7 Nov 2026 in this project: 'Testofen Fenugreek Seed Extract (Trigonella foenum-graecum) (standardized to 50% Fenuside) 600mg. Other Ingredients: Vegetable Capsule (plant cellulose) rice flour (Non-GMO)'. The whlsome, Walmart and desertcart listings give the same 600 mg per capsule. The Supplement Facts panel image, millenniumsport.net and DSLD were not opened. BSCG certification includes annual label-claim verification.
- **Proprietary blend:** no
- **Directions and daily amounts:** Per capsule: Testofen fenugreek seed extract 600 mg, standardized to at least 50% Fenuside (at least about 300 mg of saponin glycosides). Other ingredients: a plant-cellulose capsule and non-GMO rice flour. 30 capsules per bottle. The label's directions were not captured. One capsule a day gives the 600 mg/day trial dose. A marketplace Q&A claiming 'two capsules daily' is unverified; one capsule matches the trials whatever the bottle says.
- **Dose versus trials (matches):** One capsule supplies 600 mg/day of Testofen, which matches both pivotal trials. Steels 2011: 60 healthy men aged 25-52 took 600 mg/day for 6 weeks; the trial title describes the product as Testofen with a mineral formulation [https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/21312304/]. Rao 2016: a double-blind RCT in 120 healthy men aged 43-70 used 600 mg/day for 12 weeks [https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/26791805/].
- **Certification:** BSCG Certified Drug Free. Every lot is tested against BSCG's 450+ drug menu, which covers the Amazon-required panel and lists tadalafil, vardenafil and many sildenafil analogues. Label claims, contaminants and GMP are also checked. BSCG's own brand table lists these Testofen finished-product lots (category Men Health, drug-free seal): 260551 (report 19 Aug 2026), 260117 (26 Mar 2026), 250903 (12 Nov 2025), 240713 (15 Oct 2024), 231221 (28 Feb 2024) and 250AA (18 Sep 2023). The same lot numbers appear for MST's Libifem. BSCG states 'If a lot is not listed it has not been tested', so match the lot number on the bottle. This was checked against the raw BSCG brand page that this workflow fetched on 8 Nov 2026, re-read from the transcript cache because bscg.org is blocked in this sandbox. (certifier page: https://www.bscg.org/brands/millennium-sport-technologies; verified in the certifier's database: yes; verifier marked the programme as screening for hidden drugs: yes — see the challenge notes, which found PDE5-drug screening confirmed only for BSCG)
- **Manufacturer and regulatory record:** Millennium Sport Technologies (millenniumsport.net; BSCG lists the contact as Matt Masuda). It uses licensed Gencor Testofen, according to its Amazon copy. A web search on 8 Nov 2026 for FDA warning letters naming 'Millennium Sport Technologies' (alongside other firms) found no letter for MST; FDA's own database was not queried. Not searched: FDA hidden-drug notices by brand, recalls, Health Canada, TGA and MHRA. These checks were blocked this session: the web-search budget was used up, Firecrawl credits ran out, and the sandbox blocks www.bscg.org, api.ods.od.nih.gov, www.ebi.ac.uk and www.sopharma.bg, which must be allowed in network settings. Ingredient-level legal history: 2014 US class actions against Gencor, the Testofen supplier, and several brands alleged that Testofen testosterone claims were false. A January 2014 MDL motion sought to consolidate them, and the outcomes were not found [https://www.aboutlawsuits.com/testosterone-supplement-class-action-65661/] [https://truthinadvertising.org/wp-content/uploads/2017/05/Ryan-v-Gencor-original-complaint-1.pdf]. These were advertising allegations, not safety or adulteration findings. Your Firecrawl credits ran out. Daily MCP credits refresh in 17h 4m. Add credits or upgrade to keep going: https://www.firecrawl.dev/billing/s/nYoXJtq7iEmE4h0jDwgbJo6FNUaEkraAy9kVku4lB64
- **Evidence summary:** Steels 2011 improved the arousal and orgasm sub-domains of the DISF-SR (Derogatis Interview for Sexual Functioning, self-report); prolactin and testosterone stayed within the reference range (no rise) [https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/21312304/]. Rao 2016, in men aged 43-70, lowered the Aging Male Symptoms score versus placebo, improved sexual function (morning erections, frequency of sexual activity), and raised total and free testosterone versus placebo [https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/26791805/]. Both trials are linked to the ingredient's supplier, and Steels was partly commercially funded [https://www.nutraingredients-usa.com/Research/Fenugreek-extract-shows-male-libido-boost-Human-RCT]. Meta-analyses find a small effect on total testosterone: Isenmann 2023 SMD 0.32 [https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/37253363/]; Frontiers in Nutrition 2026 SMD 0.25 [https://www.frontiersin.org/journals/nutrition/articles/10.3389/fnut.2026.1903508/full]. Harvard: 'One study noted a benefit in improving sexual arousal and orgasm, and other research has shown this herb to be safe over all.' The outcomes are libido and testosterone in healthy men, not erectile dysfunction. It replaces Performer 8's libido and testosterone role, not KSM-66's stress and cortisol role.

**Safety risks**

- Digestive upset (diarrhoea, nausea) [https://www.nccih.nih.gov/health/fenugreek]
- 'Large doses may cause a harmful drop in blood sugar' (NCCIH)
- 'Can cause allergic reactions, including serious reactions' (NCCIH); fenugreek is a legume
- Maple-syrup odour in sweat or urine, and possible low potassium (2026 scoping review, from project notes) [https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/pmc/articles/PMC12858439/]
- Liver: LiverTox likelihood score E (an unlikely cause); no US supplement-related liver-injury cases attributed to fenugreek [https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/NBK548826/] [https://www.mdpi.com/2673-4389/4/1/8]
- Not safe in pregnancy because of birth-defect risk (NCCIH); keep it away from a pregnant partner
- Not hormone-neutral: Rao 2016 found raised total and free testosterone

**Interactions**

- Diabetes drugs (insulin, sulfonylureas and others): blood sugar may fall further; monitor glucose (NCCIH).
- Warfarin and antiplatelets: reviews cite a potential interaction (coumarin-like constituents) but it is unconfirmed; check INR if you take warfarin [https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/pmc/articles/PMC12858439/].
- Testosterone therapy and hormone-sensitive conditions: raised testosterone in Rao 2016, so discuss with a doctor (inference).
- Nitrates, PDE5 inhibitors, antihypertensives, thyroid drugs, SSRIs/MAOIs and sedatives: no interaction found in the NCCIH and LiverTox summaries reviewed. NCCIH still advises checking with a clinician because 'some herbs and medicines interact in harmful ways'.

**Who should avoid it**

- Men allergic to fenugreek or other legumes (reported cross-reactivity with peanut and chickpea not verified this session)
- Men on insulin or sulfonylureas, unless glucose is monitored
- Men on warfarin, unless INR is monitored
- Men with prostate cancer or other hormone-sensitive conditions, or on testosterone therapy, unless a doctor agrees
- Pregnant women (relevant to the partner)

**Stop signals**

- Hives, facial swelling or wheezing: stop and seek urgent care
- Shakiness, sweating or confusion (low blood sugar), especially on diabetes drugs
- Unusual bruising or bleeding while on warfarin or antiplatelets
- Persistent diarrhoea or abdominal pain
- Yellow skin or eyes, or dark urine (unlikely with fenugreek, but stop)
- No libido benefit after 12 weeks, the length of the Rao trial: stop

**Challenge reviews**

- *certification-label lens — confirmed; downgrade to: none*
  - Audit basis: raw page captures from this workflow (7-8 Nov 2026), not the verifier's summary; live re-opening was blocked today (network policy, spent WebSearch budget, Firecrawl out of credits until 2026-11-09 05:26 UTC).
  - Directly confirmed from the raw 8 Nov 2026 capture (live fetch) of https://www.bscg.org/brands/millennium-sport-technologies: Testofen, Finished Product, category Men Health, drug-free seal (Certified Drug Free), lots 260551 (report 19-Aug-2026), 260117 (26-Mar-2026), 250903 (12-Nov-2025), 240713 (15-Oct-2024), 231221 (28-Feb-2024) and 250AA (18-Sep-2023).
  - Quirks: every Testofen lot number is shared with MST's Libifem, and every row links the same certificate file (-240386782.pdf), so a per-lot certificate cannot be opened from the table; the capture showed 25 of the brand's 30 entries.
  - BSCG's menu explicitly lists PDE5 inhibitors and sildenafil analogues (https://www.bscg.org/bscg-banned-substance-testing-menu-v070725), the strongest hidden-drug screen in this shortlist.
  - Label confirmed from MST's own Amazon listing text (captured 7 Nov 2026): Testofen fenugreek seed extract (50% Fenuside) 600 mg; vegetable capsule, rice flour; 'take one (1) vegetarian capsule daily with either water or your evening meal'; 'minimum of 8-12 weeks'. A DSLD label (pdf 204638) surfaced in search but could not be opened. No reformulation evidence.

- *safety-evidence lens — confirmed; downgrade to: none*
  - Certification matches the raw BSCG capture: Testofen lots 260551 (19-Aug-2026) back to 250AA show the 'drug free seal' in the Program column, i.e. BSCG Certified Drug Free, whose test menu names PDE5 inhibitors. That is the strongest hidden-drug screen on the shortlist. MST's women's product Libifem shows the same lot numbers, so check the product name and the lot together.
  - The trial match is slightly overstated. Steels 2011 tested Testofen with a mineral formulation (as its title says), not Testofen alone. Rao 2016 (600 mg Testofen, men aged 43-70, 12 weeks) is the clean match, and both trials measured libido and testosterone in men without diagnosed ED.
  - Allergy: fenugreek is a legume, and cross-reactivity with peanut and chickpea has been reported (from my own knowledge; PubMed search link). Avoid it if allergic to peanuts.
  - Stacking: fenugreek's glucose-lowering effect adds to that of arginine, Pycnogenol, ashwagandha and ginseng in men on insulin or sulfonylureas, and men on warfarin need an INR check.

#### Verification record 2 of 2

- **Verifier verdict:** recommend — The only product in this batch that pairs the exact 600 mg/day Testofen dose from the libido trials with a fully disclosed single-ingredient label and lot-by-lot BSCG drug screening (lots listed through August 2026), with no regulatory red flag found and the lowest liver risk of the drive herbs.
- **Product as verified:** MST (Millennium Sport Technologies) — Testofen 600 mg (fenugreek seed extract standardized to 50% Fenuside), 30 vegetarian capsules; also sold as a 2 x 30 twin pack
- **Product page:** https://www.amazon.com/Testofen-Fenugreek-Standardized-FenusideTM-Certified/dp/B08NZ39151
- **Where sold:** US: Amazon (brand listing) and Walmart's Millennium Sport brand page [https://www.amazon.com/Testofen-Fenugreek-Standardized-FenusideTM-Certified/dp/B08NZ39151; https://www.walmart.com/c/brand/millennium-sport]. Gulf: found only through cross-border resellers - desertcart Kuwait lists the twin pack and whlsome.com (which has an Arabic storefront) lists the 30-count [https://www.desertcart.com.kw/products/270124694-mst-testofen-supplement-twin-pack-2-bottles-of-30-count; https://whlsome.com/ar/products/mst-testofen-fenugreek-seed-extract-standardized-to-50-fenuside-600mg-30-count-bscg-certified-drug-free]; with a reseller, check the bottle's lot number against BSCG's list. UK/EU: no UK retailer confirmed. A locally sold alternative, Myprotein Testofen capsules (UAE, Ireland), uses the same branded extract but no certification was found for it [https://www.myprotein.ae/p/sports-nutrition/testofen-capsules/11447791].
- **Label verified:** yes. MST's own Amazon listing text (ingredients, suggested use and warnings), captured 7 Nov 2026 [https://www.amazon.com/Testofen-Fenugreek-Standardized-FenusideTM-Certified/dp/B08NZ39151]. The panel image itself was not viewed. This sweep could not run new searches (the shared web-search budget was used up), and amazon.com, nsfsport.com, ebi.ac.uk, fda.gov, ncbi.nlm.nih.gov, Health Canada, TGA, gov.uk (MHRA), iherb.com, vitacost.com and livemomentous.com are outside this session's allowed network domains (they must be allowed to re-check). Facts come from pages captured earlier in this workflow on 7-8 Nov 2026.
- **Proprietary blend:** no
- **Directions and daily amounts:** Directions: 'take one (1) vegetarian capsule daily with either water or your evening meal. For best results, use for a minimum of 8-12 weeks.'
Per capsule (= per day): Testofen fenugreek seed extract (Trigonella foenum-graecum), standardized to 50% Fenuside, 600 mg. That is about 300 mg Fenuside saponins a day (label arithmetic).
Other ingredients: vegetable capsule (plant cellulose), rice flour (non-GMO).
The listing says MST uses licensed Gencor Testofen [https://www.amazon.com/Testofen-Fenugreek-Standardized-FenusideTM-Certified/dp/B08NZ39151].
No stimulants, hormones or proprietary blend.
- **Dose versus trials (matches):** Testofen 600 mg/day vs 600 mg/day of the same extract in both pivotal trials: an exact match.
- Steels 2011: 60 healthy men aged 25-52, 6 weeks; arousal and orgasm sub-scores improved; testosterone and prolactin stayed in range [https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/21312304/].
- Rao 2016: 120 healthy men aged 43-70, 12 weeks; ageing-male symptoms fell; morning erections and sexual frequency improved; total and free testosterone rose vs placebo [https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/26791805/].
- A 2021 crossover pilot also used Testofen 600 mg/day, inside a multi-ingredient drink [https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC8443991/].
The trials gave 600 mg/day; one 600 mg capsule gives the same daily total.
- **Certification:** BSCG Certified Drug Free (drug-screening tier), found in BSCG's own database.
BSCG's Millennium Sport Technologies page (read 8 Nov 2026) lists 'Testofen' as a Finished Product, category Men Health, with these lots [https://www.bscg.org/brands/millennium-sport-technologies; https://www.bscg.org/brands/millennium-sport-technologies/testofen-1]:
- 260551, report 19 Aug 2026
- 260117, 26 Mar 2026
- 250903, 12 Nov 2025
- 240713, 15 Oct 2024
- 231221, 28 Feb 2024
- 250AA, 18 Sep 2023
The table's 'Program' column is blank. A search summary of the product page described its entries as carrying the drug-free seal. Two lots tested in 2026 also fit the every-lot Certified Drug Free design, not annual Certified Quality testing (my inference).
Certified Drug Free 'tests every finished product lot... for an industry leading 450+ banned substances', with annual label-claim and contaminant checks and a GMP audit; BSCG adds 'If a lot is not listed it has not been tested' [https://www.bscg.org/brands/millennium-sport-technologies; https://www.bscg.org/certified-drug-free-database].
BSCG's menu names PDE5 inhibitors and sildenafil analogues (tadalafil, vardenafil, thiosildenafil and others) [https://www.bscg.org/bscg-banned-substance-testing-menu-v070725]. (certifier page: https://www.bscg.org/brands/millennium-sport-technologies/testofen-1; verified in the certifier's database: yes; verifier marked the programme as screening for hidden drugs: yes — see the challenge notes, which found PDE5-drug screening confirmed only for BSCG)
- **Manufacturer and regulatory record:** Company: Millennium Sport Technologies, a small US sports-supplement brand. BSCG lists a named contact and millenniumsport.net, plus BSCG-tested lots of six other products (Libifem, Capsimax, ZMK, CreO2, MVP-365, Cordygen-VO2) since 2023 [https://www.bscg.org/brands/millennium-sport-technologies]. Its wider reputation was not researched.
- FDA warning letters: none found. An 8 Nov 2026 web search naming Millennium Sport Technologies returned no letter.
- FDA hidden-drug notifications: none found. Only the landing page of FDA's 441-product sexual-enhancement list was captured, not a brand search [https://www.fda.gov/drugs/medication-health-fraud-notifications/sexual-enhancement-and-energy-product-notifications].
- Recalls, Health Canada, TGA, MHRA: not checked; those sites were blocked this session.
- Evidence caveat: both Testofen trials share authors, and the 2011 trial was partly industry-funded [https://www.nutraingredients-usa.com/Research/Fenugreek-extract-shows-male-libido-boost-Human-RCT].
- **Evidence summary:** Fenugreek is a drive and testosterone ingredient, not an erection treatment.
- Testofen at this exact dose improved libido sub-scores in healthy men over 6 weeks [https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/21312304/].
- In men aged 43-70 over 12 weeks it improved sexual function and modestly raised testosterone [https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/26791805/].
- Meta-analyses find small total-testosterone effects: SMD 0.32 across 7 studies, and SMD 0.25 in a 2026 analysis [https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/37253363/; https://www.frontiersin.org/journals/nutrition/articles/10.3389/fnut.2026.1903508/full].
- Weakness: the trials are manufacturer-linked and enrolled men without ED [https://www.nutraingredients-usa.com/Research/Fenugreek-extract-shows-male-libido-boost-Human-RCT].
For a healthy man in his 40s it is the best-evidenced, trial-matched Performer 8 drive replacement in this batch.

**Safety risks**

- Liver: LiverTox gives fenugreek likelihood score E (unlikely cause of liver injury), the best liver profile of the drive herbs [https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/NBK548826/].
- Stomach and bowel: diarrhoea, nausea and other digestive symptoms [https://www.nccih.nih.gov/health/fenugreek].
- Blood sugar: large doses may cause a harmful drop [https://www.nccih.nih.gov/health/fenugreek]; a 2026 scoping review lists hypoglycaemia among reported adverse effects [https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/pmc/articles/PMC12858439/].
- Allergy: allergic reactions, including serious ones, can occur [https://www.nccih.nih.gov/health/fenugreek]. Fenugreek is a legume, so men with peanut or chickpea allergy should be cautious (my inference).
- Possible low potassium and a maple-syrup odour of urine or sweat are reported [https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/pmc/articles/PMC12858439/].
- No stimulants or hormones are on the label [https://www.amazon.com/Testofen-Fenugreek-Standardized-FenusideTM-Certified/dp/B08NZ39151].

**Interactions**

- Diabetes drugs (insulin, sulfonylureas, metformin): additive glucose lowering; monitor blood sugar [https://www.nccih.nih.gov/health/fenugreek; https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/pmc/articles/PMC12858439/].
- Anticoagulants and antiplatelets (warfarin, aspirin, clopidogrel): a bleeding concern from coumarin-like constituents is cited in reviews. It is theoretical, with little case evidence; check INR if on warfarin [https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/pmc/articles/PMC12858439/].
- Any prescription or OTC drug: the label says not to use it without your provider's consent [https://www.amazon.com/Testofen-Fenugreek-Standardized-FenusideTM-Certified/dp/B08NZ39151].
- Nitrates, PDE5 inhibitors, antihypertensives, thyroid drugs, SSRIs/MAOIs and sedatives: no interaction documented in the sources reviewed [https://www.nccih.nih.gov/health/fenugreek; https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/NBK548826/].
- Potassium-lowering diuretics: theoretical additive low potassium (my inference from the scoping review) [https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/pmc/articles/PMC12858439/].

**Who should avoid it**

- Anyone allergic to fenugreek; men with peanut or chickpea allergy should be cautious (my inference) [https://www.nccih.nih.gov/health/fenugreek]
- Men on insulin or sulfonylureas, unless their doctor monitors blood sugar [https://www.nccih.nih.gov/health/fenugreek]
- Men on warfarin, unless INR is checked after starting [https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/pmc/articles/PMC12858439/]
- Men with high blood pressure, diabetes, heart, kidney or prostate disease, or a psychiatric condition including bipolar disorder, without medical advice (label warning) [https://www.amazon.com/Testofen-Fenugreek-Standardized-FenusideTM-Certified/dp/B08NZ39151]
- Do not share with a partner who is or may become pregnant: fenugreek is unsafe in pregnancy above food amounts [https://www.nccih.nih.gov/health/fenugreek]

**Stop signals**

- Hives, lip or face swelling or wheeze: stop and get urgent care [https://www.nccih.nih.gov/health/fenugreek]
- Shakiness, sweating, confusion or other low-blood-sugar symptoms, especially on diabetes drugs [https://www.nccih.nih.gov/health/fenugreek]
- Unusual bruising or bleeding if on warfarin or antiplatelets [https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/pmc/articles/PMC12858439/]
- Persistent diarrhoea or stomach upset [https://www.nccih.nih.gov/health/fenugreek]
- No change in desire after 12 weeks, the longest trial length: stop rather than continue indefinitely [https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/26791805/]

A DSLD label (pdf 204638) surfaced in search but could not be opened. No reformulation evidence.
  - This entry says the BSCG table's 'Program' column is blank. The raw capture shows the drug-free seal image in that column for every Testofen row, so the program is Certified Drug Free, not merely inferred from a search summary.

- *safety-evidence lens — confirmed; downgrade to: none*
  - Certification matches the raw BSCG capture: lots up to 260551 (19-Aug-2026) carry the 'drug free seal', i.e. Certified Drug Free with PDE5 inhibitors on the test menu. Check the product name and lot together, because Libifem uses the same lot numbers.
  - Steels 2011 tested Testofen with a mineral formulation; Rao 2016 is the clean match. Both measured libido and testosterone, not erections.
  - Allergy: cross-reactivity of fenugreek with peanut and chickpea has been reported (from my own knowledge; PubMed search link).
  - Stacking: its glucose-lowering effect adds to that of the other picks in men on insulin or sulfonylureas, and men on warfarin need an INR check.

- **Evidence summary:** Ashwagandha works on drive and stress. It is not an erection drug.
- Chauhan 2022 (50 men with low desire, 8 weeks): total DISF-M sexual-function score improved vs placebo (mean difference 9.8 points; 95% CI 8.87-10.73). Serum testosterone rose; prolactin did not change [https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/35873404/].
- Khanna 2026 (76 healthy men aged 30-50, 8 weeks): SDI-2 desire scores, satisfying sexual events and IIEF all improved vs placebo (p<=0.001). Ejaculate volume rose 36%, sperm count 38% and motility 87%. No adverse events were reported [https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/41766918/].
- Fornalik 2026 meta-analysis (23 RCTs, 1,706 people): testosterone +57.43 ng/dL in men but not women; cortisol SMD -1.18; T4 +0.61 ug/dL. The data were heterogeneous [https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/41740946/].
- NCCIH: some preparations may help stress and insomnia, and there is limited evidence that 2-4 months may raise testosterone and sperm quality [https://www.nccih.nih.gov/health/ashwagandha].
Limits: both sexual-function trials are small, last 8 weeks, rely on self-reported scales and enrolled men without diagnosed ED.

**Safety risks**

- Liver: LiverTox rates ashwagandha a likely cause (score B; 'probable', C, in its 2019 version) of clinically apparent liver injury. Onset is typically 2-12 weeks after starting. The injury is usually cholestatic or mixed, with jaundice and itching, and usually subsides 1-4 months after stopping. Fatal cases have been described [https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/NBK548536/].
- A five-patient series from Iceland and the US DILIN registry: jaundice, nausea, lethargy and itching after 2-12 weeks; itching and high bilirubin lasted 5-20 weeks; no liver failure [https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/31991029/]. In a 2024 case, symptoms began about 30 hours after the first dose [https://assets.cureus.com/uploads/case_report/pdf/263978/20241016-914280-uua1xg.pdf].
- Thyroid: pooled RCTs show a modest T4 rise (+0.61 ug/dL) without TSH or T3 change [https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/41740946/]. NCCIH advises against use with thyroid disorders [https://www.nccih.nih.gov/health/ashwagandha].
- Common effects are drowsiness, stomach upset, diarrhoea and vomiting. Short-term use (up to 3 months) may be safe; long-term safety has not been established [https://www.nccih.nih.gov/health/ashwagandha].
- Hormonal: it may raise testosterone, so men with hormone-sensitive prostate cancer should avoid it [https://www.nccih.nih.gov/health/ashwagandha].
- Regulatory: in 2023 Denmark banned ashwagandha in food supplements after its DTU assessment found root extracts 'can have a negative effect on thyroid and sex hormones', and Sweden applied the Danish assessment [https://nutraingredients.com/Article/2023/05/17/Growing-concerns-over-safety-of-ashwagandha-in-EU-member-states]. The Netherlands announced a ban in early 2026; the EU standstill period runs to 7 Dec 2026 [https://www.nutraingredients.com/Article/2026/07/06/timeline-will-authorities-de-throne-the-king-of-herbs/]. India's April 2026 leaf ban does not affect this root-only extract [https://nutraingredients.com/Article/2026/04/28/india-bans-ashwagandha-leaf-use-in-any-form-industry-reacts].
- Doubling up: Performer 8 already contains KSM-66. Do not take this with Performer 8 or with any other ashwagandha product, such as Thorne Advanced Testosterone Support, Klean Ashwagandha or Transparent Labs KSM-66 [https://www.usatoday.com/press-release/story/43980/performer-8-reviews-wolfson-brands-announces-u-s-launch-of-current-11-ingredient-mens-wellness-formula/].

**Interactions**

- Thyroid drugs (levothyroxine, antithyroid drugs): NCCIH lists thyroid hormone medicines as a possible interaction, and T4 rose in pooled trials [https://www.nccih.nih.gov/health/ashwagandha; https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/41740946/].
- Sedatives and anticonvulsants (benzodiazepines, sleep drugs, alcohol, antiseizure drugs): possible interaction per NCCIH [https://www.nccih.nih.gov/health/ashwagandha].
- Antihypertensives: NCCIH lists blood-pressure medicines as a possible interaction [https://www.nccih.nih.gov/health/ashwagandha].
- Diabetes drugs (insulin, sulfonylureas and others): NCCIH lists diabetes medicines as a possible interaction, so monitor glucose [https://www.nccih.nih.gov/health/ashwagandha].
- Immunosuppressants: possible interaction per NCCIH [https://www.nccih.nih.gov/health/ashwagandha].
- Nitrates and PDE5 inhibitors (sildenafil, tadalafil): NCCIH documents no interaction, and none has been studied. My inference: someone also on blood-pressure drugs should watch for dizziness [https://www.nccih.nih.gov/health/ashwagandha].
- Anticoagulants and antiplatelets (warfarin, DOACs, aspirin, clopidogrel): not on NCCIH's interaction list, and no study was found [https://www.nccih.nih.gov/health/ashwagandha].
- SSRIs and MAOIs: no documented interaction and no study found. Still tell the prescriber, because ashwagandha is sedating (my inference) [https://www.nccih.nih.gov/health/ashwagandha].

**Who should avoid it**

- Men with thyroid disease or on thyroid medication [https://www.nccih.nih.gov/health/ashwagandha]
- Men with autoimmune disease or on immunosuppressants [https://www.nccih.nih.gov/health/ashwagandha]
- Men with hormone-sensitive prostate cancer [https://www.nccih.nih.gov/health/ashwagandha]
- Anyone with current or past liver disease, or earlier herb- or drug-induced liver injury [https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/NBK548536/]
- Anyone scheduled for surgery (stop beforehand) [https://www.nccih.nih.gov/health/ashwagandha]
- Anyone taking sedatives or anticonvulsants, unless a doctor agrees [https://www.nccih.nih.gov/health/ashwagandha]
- Anyone still taking Performer 8 or another ashwagandha product
- Residents of Denmark, where it is banned in food supplements [https://nutraingredients.com/Article/2023/05/17/Growing-concerns-over-safety-of-ashwagandha-in-EU-member-states]

**Stop signals**

- Yellow eyes or skin, dark urine, pale stools, persistent itching, nausea, unusual tiredness or right-upper abdominal pain, especially in weeks 2-12: stop and get liver tests [https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/31991029/; https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/NBK548536/]
- Palpitations, tremor, heat intolerance, unexplained weight loss or new insomnia. These could be a thyroid effect (my inference from the T4 rise) [https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/41740946/]
- Marked daytime drowsiness, vomiting or diarrhoea [https://www.nccih.nih.gov/health/ashwagandha]
- Dizziness or fainting while also on blood-pressure medicine (my inference)
- No clear gain in desire or stress after 8 weeks, the trial length. Stop rather than continue indefinitely, since safety beyond 3 months is not established [https://www.nccih.nih.gov/health/ashwagandha]
- Persistent erection problems: see a doctor. This is not an ED treatment.

**Challenge reviews**

- *certification-label lens — corrected; downgrade to: none*

  - The shortlist's purchase URL is the 60-count page (https://coremedscience.com/products/ashwagandha-60ct-capsules), which this listing does not cover. A second listing with the same name (1780038, under a different NSF file path) is in the 8 Nov directory but was never opened, so its size and lots are unknown.
  - With a single listed lot, bottles from any other lot are not certified.
  - The 5% withanolide figure comes from the brand's 60-count page via search index, not from the NSF label; directions are not on the panel.

- *safety-evidence lens — corrected; downgrade to: none*
  - Evidence correction: Examine notes that one of Chauhan 2022's own figures contradicts the reported testosterone rise, and the trial enrolled men with low sexual desire, not healthy men. Neither abstract names the extract, so 'matches' assumes the trials used KSM-66.
  - Thyroid harm is understated. Beyond the modest pooled rise in T4, case reports describe thyrotoxicosis linked to ashwagandha (from my own knowledge; PubMed search link). New palpitations, tremor or weight loss call for TSH and free T4 tests.
  - Liver monitoring (inference from LiverTox's B rating and the 2-12-week onset): men on a statin or another drug that affects the liver should have liver tests at baseline and at about 6-8 weeks.
  - NSF lists 'Sleep Aid' among the product's purposes, so expect sedation. Avoid combining it with alcohol, benzodiazepines, Z-drug sleeping pills or sedating antihistamines.
  - The PDE5 caveat on NSF's sport panel applies, though it matters little for a root extract.

- The fenugreek libido trials used Testofen 600 mg/day, about 300 mg Fenuside saponins (50% standardization) [https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/21312304/; https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/26791805/]. This capsule gives 125 mg saponins, less than half that saponin load, though assays may differ (my inference).

- Testosterone meta-analyses pool several extracts and find small effects (SMD 0.25-0.32) [https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/37253363/; https://www.frontiersin.org/journals/nutrition/articles/10.3389/fnut.2026.1903508/full].

NSF screens for sport-banned substances and checks label contents and contaminants [https://www.nsfsport.com/our-mark.php]; PDE5-inhibitor coverage is not published in the material reviewed (BSCG's panel lists them) [https://www.bscg.org/bscg-banned-substance-testing-menu-v070725]. (certifier page: https://www.nsfsport.com/certified-products/listing-detail.php?id=1695617; verified in the certifier's database: yes; verifier marked the programme as screening for hidden drugs: yes — see the challenge notes, which found PDE5-drug screening confirmed only for BSCG)
- **Manufacturer and regulatory record:** Company: Pure Power is a Mercola line; iHerb and Vitacost sell this item under the Dr. Mercola name [https://www.iherb.com/pr/dr-mercola-testosterone-support-30-capsules/152629; https://www.vitacost.com/pure-power-testosterone-support-liposomal-fenugreek-nsf-certified-for-sport]. NSF lists 12 Pure Power products [https://www.nsfsport.com/certified-products/search-results.php?search=ashwagandha].
- Hidden drugs, adulteration, recalls: none found in the gathered material. No FDA, Health Canada, TGA or MHRA search could be run (blocked this session).
- Claims history, from public reporting and NOT re-verified this sweep: FDA has sent warning letters to Mercola.com LLC over unapproved drug claims, including one in February 2021 about COVID-19 claims for vitamin C, vitamin D3 and quercetin products, and the FTC settled false-advertising charges over tanning beds in 2016. These were claims cases, not adulteration. Verify by searching 'Mercola' in FDA's warning-letter database and the FTC case list [https://www.fda.gov/inspections-compliance-enforcement-and-criminal-investigations/compliance-actions-and-activities/warning-letters; https://www.ftc.gov/legal-library/browse/cases-proceedings].
- **Evidence summary:** Fenugreek's drive evidence is libido and testosterone in healthy men [https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/21312304/; https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/26791805/], with small pooled testosterone effects [https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/37253363/; https://www.frontiersin.org/journals/nutrition/articles/10.3389/fnut.2026.1903508/full].
This product's AlphaFen extract has no published trial in the material reviewed, and it carries less than half the saponin dose of the Testofen trials. Expect a weaker or unknown effect compared with a trial-dosed Testofen product.

**Safety risks**

- Liver: LiverTox likelihood score E (unlikely cause of liver injury) [https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/NBK548826/].
- Diarrhoea, nausea and other digestive symptoms; large doses may cause a harmful drop in blood sugar; allergic reactions, including serious ones [https://www.nccih.nih.gov/health/fenugreek].
- Possible low potassium and a maple-syrup odour of urine or sweat [https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/pmc/articles/PMC12858439/].
- Fenugreek is a legume, so men with peanut or chickpea allergy should be cautious (my inference).
- Certification gap: NSF lists only lot 2405069, so other lots may be untested [https://www.nsfsport.com/certified-products/listing-detail.php?id=1695617].
- Other ingredients, including the liposome carrier, were not captured, so check the bottle for allergens.

**Interactions**

- Diabetes drugs: additive glucose lowering; monitor blood sugar [https://www.nccih.nih.gov/health/fenugreek; https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/pmc/articles/PMC12858439/].
- Anticoagulants and antiplatelets (warfarin, aspirin, clopidogrel): a theoretical bleeding concern from coumarin-like constituents is cited in reviews; check INR if on warfarin [https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/pmc/articles/PMC12858439/].
- Nitrates, PDE5 inhibitors, antihypertensives, thyroid drugs, SSRIs/MAOIs and sedatives: no interaction documented in the sources reviewed [https://www.nccih.nih.gov/health/fenugreek; https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/NBK548826/].

**Who should avoid it**

- Anyone allergic to fenugreek; men with peanut or chickpea allergy should be cautious (my inference) [https://www.nccih.nih.gov/health/fenugreek]
- Men on insulin or sulfonylureas, unless their doctor monitors blood sugar [https://www.nccih.nih.gov/health/fenugreek]
- Men on warfarin, unless INR is checked after starting [https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/pmc/articles/PMC12858439/]
- Drug-tested athletes holding a bottle whose lot is not on NSF's listing [https://www.nsfsport.com/certified-products/listing-detail.php?id=1695617]
- Do not share with a partner who is or may become pregnant: fenugreek is unsafe in pregnancy above food amounts [https://www.nccih.nih.gov/health/fenugreek]

**Stop signals**

- Hives, swelling or wheeze: stop and get urgent care [https://www.nccih.nih.gov/health/fenugreek]
- Shakiness, sweating or confusion (low blood sugar), especially on diabetes drugs [https://www.nccih.nih.gov/health/fenugreek]
- Unusual bruising or bleeding on warfarin or antiplatelets [https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/pmc/articles/PMC12858439/]
- Persistent stomach upset [https://www.nccih.nih.gov/health/fenugreek]
- No change in desire after 12 weeks (my inference from trial lengths) [https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/26791805/]

The NSF-hosted label reads AlphaFen liposomal fenugreek seed extract 500 mg, saponins 125 mg, trigonelline 12.5 mg.
  - Only one lot is certified.
  - The Mercola warning-letter and FTC statements in this entry were added from background knowledge and cite only generic FDA and FTC landing pages; they need specific source links or should be dropped.

  - Mercola's FDA and FTC claims history remains unverified this pass. It concerned marketing claims, not adulteration.

### Report rank 4: Klean Athlete (Nestlé Health Science / Douglas Laboratories) — Klean Ashwagandha, 60 capsules (item KA202849-60)

Two or more verification batches checked this product independently; all 2 records are kept so their differences stay visible.

#### Verification record 1 of 2

- **Verifier verdict:** acceptable — Drug-screened (NSF Certified for Sport, ten listed lots), root-only and from a large professional-line maker, but its 500 mg extract is unnamed and unstandardized, so it cannot be matched to the trials; Core Med's NSF-listed KSM-66 is the closer match.
- **Product as verified:** Klean Athlete (Nestlé Health Science / Douglas Laboratories) — Klean Ashwagandha, 60 capsules (item KA202849-60)

- **Where sold:** US.

- The 8 Nov 2026 search for UAE and Saudi sellers found Klean only on Fullscript.
- No UK or EU seller was found.

- **Directions and daily amounts:** Serving: 1 capsule; 60 servings.
Active: ashwagandha (Withania somnifera) root extract 500 mg.
Other ingredients: microcrystalline cellulose, hydroxypropyl methylcellulose capsule.
Directions: adults take 1 capsule daily, with or between meals, or as directed by a trainer, coach or healthcare professional. That is 500 mg a day [https://www.kleanathlete.com/klean-ashwagandha.html; https://www.emersonecologics.com/products/detail/Klean-Athlete/klean-ashwagandha/U3ByZWU6OlByb2R1Y3QtMTAxMTY1/K80682].

Not disclosed: the extract brand and its withanolide standardization. The Douglas data sheet names only 'Klean Ashwagandha (Withania somnifera) root' [https://www.douglaslabs.com/media/pdf_upload/Klean_Ashwagandha_PDS_Final.pdf].
- **Dose versus trials (partial):** Ashwagandha: 500 mg/day of an unnamed, unstandardized root extract here, vs 600 mg/day (300 mg twice daily) of a standardized root extract in Chauhan 2022 and Khanna 2026 [https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/35873404/; https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/41766918/].
By weight that is 83% of the trial dose. Because the extract type and withanolide content are unknown, equivalence to the trial material cannot be shown.
Fornalik 2026 suggested effects may scale with withanolide content [https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/41740946/].
This product has not been tested in any trial.
- **Certification:** NSF Certified for Sport (drug-screening tier).

No red flag is known. Re-check before buying.
- **Evidence summary:** The evidence is the same ashwagandha root-extract evidence as for KSM-66, but none of the trials used this extract.
- Chauhan 2022: DISF-M score improved and testosterone rose vs placebo at 600 mg/day [https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/35873404/].
- Khanna 2026: desire, satisfying sexual events, IIEF and semen parameters improved at 600 mg/day [https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/41766918/].
- Fornalik 2026 meta-analysis: testosterone +57 ng/dL in men, cortisol down and T4 slightly up. The data were heterogeneous, with hints that effects depend on withanolide content [https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/41740946/].
- NCCIH: limited evidence for testosterone and sperm quality; some evidence for stress and sleep [https://www.nccih.nih.gov/health/ashwagandha].
An unstandardized extract cannot be assumed to perform like the trial material.

**Safety risks**

- Liver: LiverTox rates ashwagandha a likely or probable cause (score B, or C in 2019) of liver injury. Onset is usually 2-12 weeks after starting; the injury is usually cholestatic, with jaundice and itching, and resolves 1-4 months after stopping. Rare fatal cases exist [https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/NBK548536/; https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/31991029/].
- Thyroid: pooled trials show a modest T4 rise [https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/41740946/]. NCCIH advises against use with thyroid or autoimmune disorders [https://www.nccih.nih.gov/health/ashwagandha].
- Drowsiness and GI upset are possible. Long-term safety beyond 3 months is unknown [https://www.nccih.nih.gov/health/ashwagandha].
- The withanolide content is undisclosed, so the dose of active compounds cannot be judged (my inference from the label and data sheet) [https://www.douglaslabs.com/media/pdf_upload/Klean_Ashwagandha_PDS_Final.pdf].
- Hormonal: it may raise testosterone, so avoid it with hormone-sensitive prostate cancer [https://www.nccih.nih.gov/health/ashwagandha].
- Regulatory: banned in Danish food supplements since 2023; a Dutch ban is pending [https://nutraingredients.com/Article/2023/05/17/Growing-concerns-over-safety-of-ashwagandha-in-EU-member-states; https://www.nutraingredients.com/Article/2026/07/06/timeline-will-authorities-de-throne-the-king-of-herbs/].
- Do not combine with Performer 8 (which contains KSM-66) or with any other ashwagandha product [https://www.usatoday.com/press-release/story/43980/performer-8-reviews-wolfson-brands-announces-u-s-launch-of-current-11-ingredient-mens-wellness-formula/].

**Interactions**

- Thyroid drugs: possible interaction per NCCIH [https://www.nccih.nih.gov/health/ashwagandha].
- Sedatives, alcohol and anticonvulsants: possible additive sedation per NCCIH [https://www.nccih.nih.gov/health/ashwagandha].
- Antihypertensives: possible interaction per NCCIH [https://www.nccih.nih.gov/health/ashwagandha].
- Diabetes drugs: possible interaction per NCCIH; monitor glucose [https://www.nccih.nih.gov/health/ashwagandha].
- Immunosuppressants: possible interaction per NCCIH [https://www.nccih.nih.gov/health/ashwagandha].
- Nitrates and PDE5 inhibitors: no documented interaction, none studied. My inference: watch for dizziness if also on blood-pressure drugs [https://www.nccih.nih.gov/health/ashwagandha].
- Anticoagulants and antiplatelets: no documented interaction, and no study was found [https://www.nccih.nih.gov/health/ashwagandha].
- SSRIs and MAOIs: no documented interaction, and no study was found [https://www.nccih.nih.gov/health/ashwagandha].

**Who should avoid it**

- Men with thyroid disease or on thyroid medication [https://www.nccih.nih.gov/health/ashwagandha]
- Men with autoimmune disease or on immunosuppressants [https://www.nccih.nih.gov/health/ashwagandha]
- Men with hormone-sensitive prostate cancer [https://www.nccih.nih.gov/health/ashwagandha]
- Anyone with liver disease or earlier herb-induced liver injury [https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/NBK548536/]
- Anyone before surgery, or taking sedatives or anticonvulsants [https://www.nccih.nih.gov/health/ashwagandha]
- Anyone already taking Performer 8 or another ashwagandha product

**Stop signals**

- Jaundice, dark urine, pale stools, itching, nausea or right-upper abdominal pain, especially in weeks 2-12: stop and get liver tests [https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/31991029/]
- Palpitations, tremor, heat intolerance or weight loss, which could be a thyroid effect (my inference) [https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/41740946/]
- Excessive drowsiness, vomiting or diarrhoea [https://www.nccih.nih.gov/health/ashwagandha]
- No benefit after 8 weeks: stop rather than continue indefinitely [https://www.nccih.nih.gov/health/ashwagandha]

- ten certified lots: 50329652, 50348003, 50350178, 50333522, 50367508, 50358704, 50341774, 50346424, 50356113, 50362279
The brand has 66 listings in NSF's full certified-product list [https://www.nsfsport.com/certified-products/search-results.php?search=ashwagandha].
What NSF checks: absence of 290+ substances banned in sport, label contents and contaminants, plus a GMP-audited facility [https://www.nsfsport.com/our-mark.php]. NSF's public materials do not say whether PDE5 inhibitors are on its panel (project finding), and BSCG reports that NSF's lot-testing frequency varies by product and manufacturer [https://www.bscg.org/blogs/single/independent-supplement-testing-what-it-is-and-who-does-it]. Match the bottle's lot number to the list. (certifier page: https://www.nsfsport.com/certified-products/listing-detail.php?id=1536891; verified in the certifier's database: yes; verifier marked the programme as screening for hidden drugs: yes — see the challenge notes, which found PDE5-drug screening confirmed only for BSCG)
- **Manufacturer and regulatory record:** Company: Klean Athlete is a sports line of Douglas Laboratories, which publishes this product's data sheet [https://www.douglaslabs.com/media/pdf_upload/Klean_Ashwagandha_PDS_Final.pdf].
Positive record: 66 Klean products are in NSF's Certified for Sport directory [https://www.nsfsport.com/certified-products/search-results.php?search=ashwagandha].
None found: one web search on 8 Nov 2026 for FDA warning letters or recalls naming Klean Athlete (2023-2026) returned nothing.
Not searched directly: FDA's warning-letter, recall and Health Fraud databases, Health Canada, TGA and MHRA. Those sites are blocked in this session and the shared web-search budget was used up. No red flag is known.
- **Evidence summary:** The same evidence as for any ashwagandha root extract, though no trial used this one. Two 8-week RCTs at 600 mg/day improved desire scores and testosterone [https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/35873404/; https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/41766918/]; the 23-RCT meta-analysis found testosterone +57 ng/dL in men, cortisol down and T4 slightly up, with high heterogeneity [https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/41740946/]. NCCIH: limited evidence for testosterone and sperm quality, some for stress and sleep [https://www.nccih.nih.gov/health/ashwagandha]. An unstandardized 500 mg extract cannot be assumed to perform like the trial material.

**Safety risks**

- Liver: LiverTox recognises ashwagandha as a cause of clinically apparent liver injury [https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/NBK548536/]. In the Bjornsson 2020 five-case series, jaundice, itching and nausea began 2-12 weeks after starting; injury was cholestatic or mixed and liver tests normalised in 1-5 months [https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/31991029/]. NCCIH: rare, but a number of cases link liver injury to ashwagandha [https://www.nccih.nih.gov/health/ashwagandha].
- Thyroid: pooled RCTs show a small T4 rise (+0.61 µg/dL) without TSH change [https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/41740946/]; NCCIH advises against use with thyroid or autoimmune disorders [https://www.nccih.nih.gov/health/ashwagandha].
- Drowsiness, stomach upset, diarrhoea and vomiting can occur; safety is shown only for short-term use (up to 3 months) [https://www.nccih.nih.gov/health/ashwagandha].
- Hormonal: it may raise testosterone, so NCCIH says men with hormone-sensitive prostate cancer should avoid it [https://www.nccih.nih.gov/health/ashwagandha].
- Regulatory: banned in Danish food supplements since 2023 over possible thyroid and sex-hormone effects [https://nutraingredients.com/Article/2023/05/17/Growing-concerns-over-safety-of-ashwagandha-in-EU-member-states]; the Netherlands declared a ban in early 2026, with the EU standstill period running to 7 Dec 2026 [https://www.nutraingredients.com/Article/2026/07/06/timeline-will-authorities-de-throne-the-king-of-herbs/].
- Do not stack with Performer 8 (it already contains KSM-66 ashwagandha) or any other ashwagandha product [https://www.usatoday.com/press-release/story/43980/performer-8-reviews-wolfson-brands-announces-u-s-launch-of-current-11-ingredient-mens-wellness-formula/].
- The withanolide content is undisclosed, so the dose of active compounds cannot be judged (inference) [https://www.douglaslabs.com/media/pdf_upload/Klean_Ashwagandha_PDS_Final.pdf].

**Interactions**

- Thyroid hormone (levothyroxine): possible interaction per NCCIH [https://www.nccih.nih.gov/health/ashwagandha].
- Sedatives (benzodiazepines, sleep drugs, alcohol) and anticonvulsants: possible interaction per NCCIH [https://www.nccih.nih.gov/health/ashwagandha].
- Antihypertensives: possible interaction per NCCIH; watch for dizziness [https://www.nccih.nih.gov/health/ashwagandha].
- Diabetes drugs: possible interaction per NCCIH; monitor glucose [https://www.nccih.nih.gov/health/ashwagandha].
- Immunosuppressants: possible interaction per NCCIH [https://www.nccih.nih.gov/health/ashwagandha].
- Nitrates and PDE5 inhibitors: no documented interaction and none studied; because NCCIH lists blood-pressure medicines, watch for light-headedness (inference) [https://www.nccih.nih.gov/health/ashwagandha].
- Anticoagulants/antiplatelets and SSRIs/MAOIs: no interaction documented in the sources reviewed [https://www.nccih.nih.gov/health/ashwagandha].

**Who should avoid it**

- Men with thyroid disease or on thyroid medication [https://www.nccih.nih.gov/health/ashwagandha]
- Men with autoimmune disease or on immunosuppressants [https://www.nccih.nih.gov/health/ashwagandha]
- Men with hormone-sensitive prostate cancer [https://www.nccih.nih.gov/health/ashwagandha]
- Anyone with liver disease or an earlier herb-induced liver injury [https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/NBK548536/]
- Anyone due for surgery, or taking sedatives or anticonvulsants [https://www.nccih.nih.gov/health/ashwagandha]
- Anyone still taking Performer 8 or another ashwagandha product [https://www.usatoday.com/press-release/story/43980/performer-8-reviews-wolfson-brands-announces-u-s-launch-of-current-11-ingredient-mens-wellness-formula/]

**Stop signals**

- Jaundice, dark urine, pale stools, itching, nausea or right-upper abdominal pain, especially in weeks 2-12: stop and get liver tests [https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/31991029/]
- Palpitations, tremor, heat intolerance or weight loss, which could be a thyroid effect (inference) [https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/41740946/]
- Marked drowsiness, vomiting or diarrhoea [https://www.nccih.nih.gov/health/ashwagandha]
- No benefit after 8 weeks (the trial length): stop rather than continue indefinitely [https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/35873404/]

**Challenge reviews**

- *certification-label lens — confirmed; downgrade to: none*
  - Audit basis: raw page captures from this workflow (7-8 Nov 2026), not the verifier's summary; live re-opening was blocked today (network policy, spent WebSearch budget, Firecrawl out of credits until 2026-11-09 05:26 UTC).
  - Directly confirmed from the raw 8 Nov 2026 capture of https://www.nsfsport.com/certified-products/listing-detail.php?id=1536891: Klean Athlete, United States, capsule, package size 60, serving 1 capsule, ten lots exactly as listed (50329652, 50348003, 50350178, 50333522, 50367508, 50358704, 50341774, 50346424, 50356113, 50362279).
  - The NSF listing has no label link. The label (500 mg ashwagandha root extract; microcrystalline cellulose; HPMC capsule; 1 capsule daily) rests on Klean and Douglas Labs copies seen via search index; the extract brand and withanolide standardization remain undisclosed.

- *safety-evidence lens — confirmed; downgrade to: none*
  - The NSF listing, with 10 lots, matches the raw capture.
  - Case reports of thyrotoxicosis linked to ashwagandha add weight to the T4 caution (from my own knowledge; PubMed search link).
  - Liver monitoring (inference): men on statins or other drugs that affect the liver should have liver tests at baseline and at about 6-8 weeks.
  - The unnamed, unstandardised 500 mg extract is correctly flagged; it cannot be matched to the trials.

- **Verifier verdict:** acceptable — NSF Certified for Sport with a single, fully disclosed 600 mg root-extract serving that matches the trial dose by weight, but the extract's standardization is undisclosed, only one lot is certified, the powder must be weighed, and the brand's regulatory record was not checked.

- **Product page:** https://supp.co/products/bulksupplementscom-ashwagandha-extract-x001blsv81
- **Where sold:** US: NSF lists the country of sale as the United States [https://www.nsfsport.com/certified-products/listing-detail.php?id=1796579]. BulkSupplements runs international storefronts (an /en-ua store was seen for another product) [https://www.bulksupplements.com/en-ua/collections/intra-post-workout/products/l-citrulline-pure-powder]. UK/EU and Gulf retail: not verified.
- **Label verified:** yes. Supplement Facts read from the label image NSF hosts for listing 1796579 (read 8 Nov 2026) [http://info.nsf.org/Certified/Common/cfs/C0365582/C0832157/Bulk%20Supplements/Ashwagandha%20Extract/1796579/Label_01.PNG]. The image shows only the facts panel; directions, other ingredients and standardization are not on it. BulkSupplements' own product page was not opened.
- **Proprietary blend:** no
- **Directions and daily amounts:** Serving size 1/4 tsp (about 600 mg); about 417 servings per container (the 250 g bag) [http://info.nsf.org/Certified/Common/cfs/C0365582/C0832157/Bulk%20Supplements/Ashwagandha%20Extract/1796579/Label_01.PNG].
Per serving: ashwagandha extract (Withania somnifera, root) 600 mg. No other ingredient is listed on the panel.
Not stated on the panel: withanolide standardization or extract ratio [http://info.nsf.org/Certified/Common/cfs/C0365582/C0832157/Bulk%20Supplements/Ashwagandha%20Extract/1796579/Label_01.PNG].
The suggested-use text was not captured; at one serving a day the dose is 600 mg/day.
A 1/4 teaspoon is only an approximate measure; a 0.01 g scale gives a reliable 600 mg (inference).
Do not confuse it with BulkSupplements' separately NSF-listed Organic Ashwagandha Powder, which a review site describes as plain root powder, not extract [https://www.nsfsport.com/certified-products/listing-detail.php?id=1793860; https://verifiedsupplementdata.com/ashwagandha/third-party-tested/].
- **Dose versus trials (partial):** Ashwagandha: 600 mg/day by weight equals the 600 mg/day (300 mg twice daily) of standardized KSM-66 root extract in Chauhan 2022 and Khanna 2026 [https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/35873404/; https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/41766918/]. But the extract is unbranded and its withanolide content is undisclosed, and the meta-analysis hints that effects scale with withanolide content [https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/41740946/], so equivalence is unproven. Splitting it into two 300 mg doses would mirror the trial schedule (inference).
- **Certification:** NSF Certified for Sport (drug-screening tier). Listing 1796579 'ASHWAGANDHA EXTRACT', read from NSF's database on 8 Nov 2026 [https://www.nsfsport.com/certified-products/listing-detail.php?id=1796579]:

- powder; package sizes 100 g, 250 g, 500 g, 1 kg; serving 1/4 tsp (~600 mg)
- one certified lot only: 2612001. Match the bag's lot number.

### Report rank 6: Momentous — Tongkat Ali, 60 capsules (whole-root powder)

Two or more verification batches checked this product independently; all 2 records are kept so their differences stay visible.

#### Verification record 1 of 2

- **Verifier verdict:** acceptable — The only currently batch-certified tongkat sold worldwide (Informed Sport, batches to Sep 2026), so adulteration risk is low, but 400 mg of raw root powder has no trial basis, so treat it as a low-risk trial rather than an evidence-dosed replacement.
- **Product as verified:** Momentous — Tongkat Ali, 60 capsules (whole-root powder)
- **Product page:** https://www.livemomentous.com/products/tongkat
- **Where sold:** - Informed Sport lists availability in Africa, Asia, Europe, North America, Oceania and South America [https://sport.wetestyoutrust.com/supplement-search/momentous/tongkat-ali].
- US: brand site, Vitacost, Vitamin Shoppe [https://www.vitacost.com/products/momentous-tongkat-ali-60-capsules-200-mg-per-capsule-157833; https://www.vitaminshoppe.com/p/tongkat-ali-400-mg-60-capsules/mom-16833].
- UK: Healf [https://healf.com/products/momentous-tongkat-ali].
- Gulf: no local pharmacy listing found. Cross-border marketplace (desertcart) listings appeared in searches but were not confirmed.
- **Label verified:** yes. Momentous product page, captured 7 Nov 2026: Supplement Facts image text, ingredient section, directions and Light Labs panel [https://www.livemomentous.com/products/tongkat]. Vitacost copy agrees [https://www.vitacost.com/products/momentous-tongkat-ali-60-capsules-200-mg-per-capsule-157833].
- **Proprietary blend:** no
- **Directions and daily amounts:** Serving: 2 capsules; 30 servings.
Active: tongkat ali (Eurycoma longifolia) root 400 mg (200 mg per capsule). The brand describes it as 'Whole-root Tongkat Ali sourced from Indonesia... 100% root powder in its most natural form'. It is not a standardized extract, and no eurycomanone content is stated.
Other ingredients: rice flour, hypromellose (capsule), magnesium stearate, silica.
Directions: 'take 2 capsules daily with or without food'.
The page shows a Light Labs test (Feb 2026, 11 substances tested for quality) [https://www.livemomentous.com/products/tongkat].
Some review sites claim an LJ100 or 22% standardized extract. That conflicts with the brand's own page and is unverified.
- **Dose versus trials (no_trial_basis):** The trials used standardized water extracts, almost all of them Physta:
- 100 or 200 mg/day for 12 weeks in men aged 50-70 (Chinnappan 2021) [https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC8254464/]
- 200 mg/day for 1 month in late-onset hypogonadism, uncontrolled (Tambi 2012) [https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/21671978/]
- 300 mg/day for 12 weeks in men aged 30-55 (Ismail 2012) [https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/23243445/]
- 100-600 mg/day across trials in the 2022 meta-analysis [https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC9415500/]
Examine gives 200-400 mg/day of a standardized water-soluble extract for libido [https://examine.com/supplements/tongkat-ali/].
No trial used raw root powder. An extract concentrates the root many-fold, so 400 mg of whole root likely delivers much less active material than a 200 mg extract dose (my inference; Momentous states no marker content).
- **Certification:** Informed Sport (every batch screened for sport-banned substances before release).
The listing was read directly from Informed Sport's database earlier in this workflow, on 8 Nov 2026. A re-check was impossible because the site is blocked in this session.
Status: 'Certified', with no withdrawal date. Certified 12-Jul-2022. Regions: Africa, Asia, Europe, North America, Oceania, South America.
Recent batches (batch, expiry, test date):

- 017815, 29-Feb-2028, 10-Mar-2026
- plus earlier 2024-2025 batches [https://sport.wetestyoutrust.com/supplement-search/momentous/tongkat-ali]
Other tongkat listings: of the four in Informed Sport's tongkat search, Puresport was withdrawn on 10-Oct-2026, Ten Percent Club is listed for Europe only, and Biogen for Africa only. Momentous is the only one sold worldwide [https://sport.wetestyoutrust.com/supplement-search?search=tongkat; https://sport.wetestyoutrust.com/supplement-search/puresport/tongkat-ali].
PDE5 inhibitors: sildenafil analogues are the classic tongkat adulterant, and whether Informed Sport's panel includes them is not published. BSCG's menu does [https://www.bscg.org/bscg-banned-substance-testing-menu-v070725].
Not NSF Certified for Sport: there is no tongkat product in NSF's directory [https://www.nsfsport.com/certified-products/search-results.php?search=ashwagandha]. (certifier page: https://sport.wetestyoutrust.com/supplement-search/momentous/tongkat-ali; verified in the certifier's database: yes; verifier marked the programme as screening for hidden drugs: yes — see the challenge notes, which found PDE5-drug screening confirmed only for BSCG)
- **Manufacturer and regulatory record:** Company: Momentous, a US sports-nutrition brand. It has 77 listings in NSF Certified for Sport and uses Informed Sport for this product [https://www.nsfsport.com/certified-products/search-results.php?search=ashwagandha].
Its blog claims this is the only US tongkat with Informed Sport certification (brand claim) [https://www.livemomentous.com/blogs/all/best-tongkat-ali-supplement].
None found: an 8 Nov 2026 web search for FDA warning letters or recalls (2023-2026) naming Momentous returned nothing. The product is not on the first page of FDA's sexual-enhancement hidden-drug list (10 of 441 entries captured) [https://www.fda.gov/drugs/medication-health-fraud-notifications/sexual-enhancement-and-energy-product-notifications].
Not checked directly:
- FDA's warning-letter, recall and Health Fraud databases
- Health Canada
- TGA
- MHRA
These checks were impossible in this pass: regulator sites were blocked, the web-search budget was used up, and Firecrawl credits had run out until 9 Nov 2026 05:26 UTC. Re-check before buying.
- **Evidence summary:** Tongkat ali is a testosterone and stress herb, not an erection treatment. None of its trials used raw root powder.
- Leisegang 2022 meta-analysis (9 studies, 5 RCTs pooled): total testosterone rose (SMD 1.352; 95% CI 0.565-2.138), with 87% heterogeneity and publication bias in the hypogonadal subgroup [https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC9415500/].
- Chinnappan 2021 (105 men aged 50-70, Physta 100 or 200 mg vs placebo, 12 weeks): total testosterone rose in both Physta groups, and cortisol fell significantly with 200 mg [https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC8254464/]. Note: the discovery notes called this 'Leitão 2021'; it is the Chinnappan trial.
- Kotirum 2015 erectile-function meta-analysis (2 RCTs, 139 men): no significant IIEF-5 change (WMD 0.91; 95% CI -1.50 to 3.33). Only men with lower baseline scores gained (+2.15) [https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/26365449/].
- Examine: for testosterone, the data 'aren't convincing' outside men with low testosterone [https://examine.com/supplements/tongkat-ali/].

**Safety risks**

- Liver: LiverTox notes 'isolated reports of clinically apparent liver injury' with tongkat ali [https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/NBK609015/]. A 2024 case in a 47-year-old man involved nausea, vomiting, raised liver enzymes and jaundice [https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/38646387/; https://examine.com/supplements/tongkat-ali/].
- EFSA (2021) could not establish the safety of a tongkat root extract as a novel food. It cited liver and kidney effects in a chronic toxicity study, and OPSS summarises its concern as possible DNA damage in animals [https://efsa.onlinelibrary.wiley.com/doi/10.2903/j.efsa.2021.6937; https://www.opss.org/article/tongkat-ali-uses-and-safety-dietary-supplements].
- Category contamination: some products contained lead and mercury, or undisclosed sildenafil analogues [https://examine.com/supplements/tongkat-ali/; https://www.opss.org/article/tongkat-ali-uses-and-safety-dietary-supplements]. Informed Sport batch screening covers banned substances. Heavy-metal results for this product were not seen.
- Mild GI effects in trials: abdominal pain, dyspepsia, constipation [https://examine.com/supplements/tongkat-ali/].
- Hormonal: it aims to raise testosterone, and long-term effects on the prostate are unknown (my inference from [https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC9415500/]).
- WADA: not explicitly prohibited, but Examine warns it could fall under the anabolic 'similar biological effect' clause. This matters only if he is drug-tested [https://examine.com/supplements/tongkat-ali/].
- Combining with ashwagandha (e.g. Core Med KSM-66) is unstudied. Start one product at a time so that effects and side effects can be attributed (my inference).

**Interactions**

- Propranolol and possibly other beta-blockers: tongkat lowered propranolol blood levels in a human study, with no change in blood pressure or heart rate. Examine rates it minor [https://examine.com/supplements/tongkat-ali/].
- CYP1A2 and CYP2A6 substrates: possible interaction per Examine; the evidence is limited [https://examine.com/supplements/tongkat-ali/].
- Nitrates and PDE5 inhibitors: no documented direct interaction. However, adulterated tongkat products have contained sildenafil analogues, which are dangerous with nitrates; use only batch-tested product [https://examine.com/supplements/tongkat-ali/; https://www.opss.org/article/tongkat-ali-uses-and-safety-dietary-supplements].
- Antihypertensives other than beta-blockers, diabetes drugs, anticoagulants and antiplatelets, thyroid drugs, SSRIs and MAOIs, sedatives: no human interaction data found in the sources reviewed [https://examine.com/supplements/tongkat-ali/].
- Testosterone therapy or anabolic agents: possible additive effect (my inference).

**Who should avoid it**

- Anyone with liver disease or earlier drug-induced liver injury [https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/NBK609015/]
- Men with prostate cancer or other hormone-sensitive conditions, and men on testosterone therapy (my inference from its testosterone effect) [https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC9415500/]
- Anyone with kidney disease (my inference from EFSA's toxicity findings) [https://efsa.onlinelibrary.wiley.com/doi/10.2903/j.efsa.2021.6937]
- Drug-tested athletes [https://examine.com/supplements/tongkat-ali/]
- Anyone on propranolol, unless a clinician agrees [https://examine.com/supplements/tongkat-ali/]

**Stop signals**

- Jaundice, dark urine, nausea, vomiting or abdominal pain: stop and get liver tests [https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/38646387/; https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/NBK609015/]
- Flushing, headache, nasal congestion, visual changes or a sudden drop in blood pressure soon after a dose. These would suggest a hidden PDE5 inhibitor: stop and report it [https://www.fda.gov/drugs/medication-health-fraud-notifications/sexual-enhancement-and-energy-product-notifications]
- Insomnia, irritability or new acne (possible androgen effects; my inference)
- No noticeable effect after 12 weeks, the trial length [https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC8254464/]

**Challenge reviews**

- *certification-label lens — confirmed; downgrade to: none*
  - Audit basis: raw page captures from this workflow (7-8 Nov 2026), not the verifier's summary; live re-opening was blocked today (network policy, spent WebSearch budget, Firecrawl out of credits until 2026-11-09 05:26 UTC).
  - Directly confirmed from the raw 8 Nov 2026 capture of https://sport.wetestyoutrust.com/supplement-search/momentous/tongkat-ali: certified, date certified 12-Jul-2022, six regions, 16 batches, latest 0155F6 tested 24-Sep-2026 (expires 31-Aug-2028).
  - Label confirmed from the raw 7 Nov 2026 capture of https://www.livemomentous.com/products/tongkat: 'Take 2 capsules once daily'; Tongkat Ali (Eurycoma longifolia) root 400 mg, '100% root powder'; rice flour, hypromellose, magnesium stearate, silica.
  - Packaging change: the page shows a new black-bottle 'New Label' (images dated 30-Oct-2026) and states 'same great formula'; no formula change is evident, but match the batch number on new-label bottles.
  - Tongkat is the category most often spiked with sildenafil analogues, and Informed Sport and NSF test for substances banned in sport and do not publish PDE5-inhibitor coverage; only BSCG's menu explicitly lists sildenafil/tadalafil analogues (https://www.bscg.org/bscg-banned-substance-testing-menu-v070725).

- *safety-evidence lens — corrected; downgrade to: none*
  - Protection against adulteration is overstated exactly where it matters most. Tongkat products have repeatedly been found spiked with sildenafil analogues (OPSS), but Informed Sport screens for sport-banned substances, and PDE5 inhibitors are not WADA-prohibited (BSCG adds them as non-sport drugs). Every-batch testing (latest batch tested 24-Sep-2026, confirmed in the raw capture) lowers the risk but has not been shown to exclude PDE5 adulteration.
  - Missed caution: the package insert of another tongkat on the Informed Sport list (Biogen, a 200:1 root extract) contraindicates use with blood-sugar-lowering drugs and in prostate cancer. Diabetes drugs should therefore not be filed under 'no human interaction data'.
  - UK sale caution (inference): EFSA (2021) could not establish the safety of a tongkat root extract as a novel food, so tongkat lacks EU novel-food authorisation, and selling it in the UK or EU (for example through Healf) is legally uncertain.
  - Raw root powder has no trial basis, as the verifier says. Combined with the liver-injury reports in LiverTox, the balance of benefit and risk is weak, so treat it as optional, not a core replacement.

- **Evidence summary:** Tongkat ali targets testosterone and stress, not erections.

Almost all positive data come from one standardized water extract (Physta), so this whole-root powder's effect at 400 mg is unknown.

**Safety risks**

- Liver: LiverTox reports clinically apparent liver injury attributed to tongkat ali in body-builders [https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/NBK609015/; https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/39527684/]; a 47-year-old man developed jaundice shortly after starting it [https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/38646387/].
- EFSA (2021) could not establish the safety of a tongkat root extract, citing liver and kidney effects in a chronic toxicity study [https://efsa.onlinelibrary.wiley.com/doi/10.2903/j.efsa.2021.6937]; OPSS reports possible DNA-damage concerns from animal data [https://www.opss.org/article/tongkat-ali-uses-and-safety-dietary-supplements].
- Contamination: lead, mercury or sildenafil analogues have been found in some tongkat products [https://www.opss.org/article/tongkat-ali-uses-and-safety-dietary-supplements; https://examine.com/supplements/tongkat-ali/]. This product's batches are Informed Sport-tested [https://sport.wetestyoutrust.com/supplement-search/momentous/tongkat-ali].
- Mild abdominal pain, indigestion or constipation in trials, and itching [https://examine.com/supplements/tongkat-ali/; https://www.opss.org/article/tongkat-ali-uses-and-safety-dietary-supplements].
- Anti-doping: not on the WADA list, but its testosterone effect may fall under the 'similar biological effect' clause [https://examine.com/supplements/tongkat-ali/].

**Interactions**

- Propranolol: tongkat may lower its blood levels; Examine rates this possible and minor, with no blood-pressure or heart-rate change seen [https://examine.com/supplements/tongkat-ali/].
- CYP1A2 and CYP2A6 substrates: possible interactions flagged [https://examine.com/supplements/tongkat-ali/].
- Testosterone therapy, finasteride or anti-androgens: hormonal overlap or opposition; no data, so do not combine without a doctor (my inference).
- Nitrates, PDE5 inhibitors, antihypertensives, anticoagulants/antiplatelets, diabetes drugs, thyroid drugs, SSRIs/MAOIs and sedatives: no interaction documented in the sources reviewed [https://examine.com/supplements/tongkat-ali/; https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/NBK609015/].

**Who should avoid it**

- Anyone with liver disease or an earlier herb-induced liver injury [https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/NBK609015/; https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/38646387/]
- Men with prostate cancer or another hormone-sensitive condition, or on testosterone therapy (my inference from its testosterone effect)
- Men on propranolol without pharmacist review [https://examine.com/supplements/tongkat-ali/]
- Drug-tested athletes, who should weigh the WADA 'similar biological effect' caution despite certification [https://examine.com/supplements/tongkat-ali/]
- Do not share with a partner who is or may become pregnant or is breastfeeding [https://examine.com/supplements/tongkat-ali/]

**Stop signals**

- Jaundice, dark urine, pale stools, itching, nausea or upper-right abdominal pain [https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/38646387/; https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/NBK609015/]
- New acne, irritability, poor sleep or urinary symptoms (a possible androgen effect; my inference)
- Persistent abdominal pain or indigestion [https://examine.com/supplements/tongkat-ali/]
- No effect on drive after 8-12 weeks: stop rather than continue indefinitely (my inference from trial lengths) [https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC9415500/]
