### Report rank 7: Momentous — Ashwagandha (NooGandha), 60 capsules, 300 mg per capsule

Two or more verification batches checked this product independently; all 2 records are kept so their differences stay visible.

#### Verification record 1 of 2

- **Verifier verdict:** acceptable — Drug-screened (NSF Certified for Sport, all lots) and fully disclosed, but NooGandha is a root-and-leaf extract marketed for focus with no libido or testosterone trial, so it is a low-priority fallback rather than an evidence-dosed Performer 8 replacement.
- **Product as verified:** Momentous — Ashwagandha (NooGandha), 60 capsules, 300 mg per capsule
- **Product page:** https://www.livemomentous.com/products/ashwagandha
- **Where sold:** US: NSF lists the country of sale as the United States; sold by Momentous and Vitacost. Canada: Momentous en-CA storefront. Cross-border marketplace listings (desertcart in Sri Lanka, New Zealand, Morocco and Vietnam) appeared in searches but were not confirmed. UK/EU and Gulf retail: not found.
- **Label verified:** no. Momentous product page and Vitacost listing, seen only through search summaries. The NSF listing confirms the serving size (1 capsule) and pack size (60 capsules).
- **Proprietary blend:** no
- **Directions and daily amounts:** Serving: 1 capsule; 60 servings (NSF: package 60 capsules, serving 1 capsule).
Per capsule: NooGandha ashwagandha extract (root and leaf), described as liposomal, 300 mg.
- Standardized to 4% withanolides on the Momentous page and Vitacost, but 3.5% on other listings; that is about 10.5-12 mg withanolides.
- Other ingredients: rice flour, hypromellose capsule, calcium laurate, silica.
The brand suggests 300-600 mg a day (1-2 capsules): 300 mg/day at 1 capsule, 600 mg/day at 2.
- **Dose versus trials (no_trial_basis):** No trial of NooGandha on libido, testosterone or sexual function was found in the gathered sources. NSF lists the product's purpose as 'Brain Health' and 'Focus' [https://www.nsfsport.com/certified-products/listing-detail.php?id=1604971].
The male sexual-function trials used a root extract at 300 mg twice daily (600 mg/day) for 8 weeks [https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/35873404/; https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/41766918/]. Two capsules match that milligram total, but a root-and-leaf liposomal extract is a different preparation, so those results cannot be assumed to transfer (my inference).
- **Certification:** NSF Certified for Sport (drug-screening tier), verified in NSF's own database.
Listing 1604971, 'Ashwagandha', read in this workflow (NSF page cached 7 Nov 2026) [https://www.nsfsport.com/certified-products/listing-detail.php?id=1604971]:
- brand Momentous; country of sale United States
- product type Botanical/Vegetarian; purpose Brain Health, Focus
- lot # 'All'; capsule; unflavored; 60 capsules; serving 1 capsule
It also appears in the 2,625-listing NSF directory captured 8 Nov 2026, as one of 77 Momentous listings [https://www.nsfsport.com/certified-products/search-results.php?search=ashwagandha].
NSF certifies products to be free of substances banned by major sports organizations [https://www.nsf.org/consumer-resources/articles/certified-for-sport-program]. The NSF materials reviewed in this project do not say whether sildenafil-type drugs are on its panel; BSCG's menu does list them [https://www.bscg.org/bscg-banned-substance-testing-menu-v070725]. (certifier page: https://www.nsfsport.com/certified-products/listing-detail.php?id=1604971; verified in the certifier's database: yes; verifier marked the programme as screening for hidden drugs: yes — see the challenge notes, which found PDE5-drug screening confirmed only for BSCG)
- **Manufacturer and regulatory record:** Company: Momentous, a US sports-nutrition brand that certifies most of its range: 77 NSF Certified for Sport listings, and Informed Sport for its tongkat [https://www.nsfsport.com/certified-products/search-results.php?search=ashwagandha; https://sport.wetestyoutrust.com/supplement-search/momentous/tongkat-ali].
- FDA warning letters and recalls: none found in an 8 Nov 2026 web search covering 2023-2026.
- FDA hidden-drug list: no entry seen. Only the first page of the 441 sexual-enhancement notifications was captured [https://www.fda.gov/drugs/medication-health-fraud-notifications/sexual-enhancement-and-energy-product-notifications].
- Not checked directly: FDA's databases, Health Canada, TGA and MHRA. Regulator sites were unreachable and the search budget was used up, so re-check before buying.
- Ingredient rules: the extract includes leaf. India's Ministry of Ayush (15 Apr 2026) and FSSAI (16 Apr 2026) moved Ayush products and health supplements to root-only ashwagandha [https://nutraingredients.com/Article/2026/04/28/india-bans-ashwagandha-leaf-use-in-any-form-industry-reacts; https://www.supplysidesj.com/supplement-regulations/ayush-bans-ashwagandha-leaf-extracts]. That does not make US sale illegal. Denmark bans ashwagandha supplements, and a Dutch ban is pending [https://nutraingredients.com/Article/2023/05/17/Growing-concerns-over-safety-of-ashwagandha-in-EU-member-states; https://www.nutraingredients.com/Article/2026/07/06/timeline-will-authorities-de-throne-the-king-of-herbs/].
- **Evidence summary:** Ashwagandha's drive evidence comes from root extracts at 600 mg/day:
- better sexual-function scores and modest testosterone rises in two 8-week RCTs [https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/35873404/; https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/41766918/]
- testosterone +57 ng/dL in a 23-trial meta-analysis [https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/41740946/]
This product's NooGandha extract is marketed for focus and cognition, and no sexual-function trial of it was found. Its effect on drive is unknown.

**Safety risks**

- Liver: LiverTox likelihood score B or C. Injury appears 2-12 weeks after starting, usually cholestatic with jaundice and itching [https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/NBK548536/; https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/31991029/].
- Leaf content: regulators in India now restrict ashwagandha to root only [https://nutraingredients.com/Article/2026/04/28/india-bans-ashwagandha-leaf-use-in-any-form-industry-reacts]. The root-only trial data do not cover this extract's safety profile (my inference).
- Thyroid: T4 rose modestly in pooled RCTs; NCCIH advises against use with thyroid disorders [https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/41740946/; https://www.nccih.nih.gov/health/ashwagandha].
- Drowsiness, stomach upset, diarrhoea and vomiting can occur; safety beyond 3 months is unknown [https://www.nccih.nih.gov/health/ashwagandha].
- Do not stack with Performer 8 or another ashwagandha product.

**Interactions**

- Diabetes drugs and blood-pressure drugs (additive lowering), immunosuppressants, sedatives, anticonvulsants and thyroid hormone: possible interactions per NCCIH [https://www.nccih.nih.gov/health/ashwagandha].
- Nitrates and PDE5 inhibitors: no documented interaction. Watch for dizziness if also on blood-pressure drugs (my inference).
- Anticoagulants and antiplatelets, SSRIs and MAOIs: no documented interaction in the sources reviewed [https://www.nccih.nih.gov/health/ashwagandha].

**Who should avoid it**

- Men with thyroid disease or on thyroid medication [https://www.nccih.nih.gov/health/ashwagandha]
- Men with autoimmune disease or on immunosuppressants [https://www.nccih.nih.gov/health/ashwagandha]
- Men with hormone-sensitive prostate cancer [https://www.nccih.nih.gov/health/ashwagandha]
- Anyone with liver disease or a past herb-induced liver injury [https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/NBK548536/]
- Anyone due for surgery, or taking sedatives or anticonvulsants [https://www.nccih.nih.gov/health/ashwagandha]
- Anyone still taking Performer 8 or another ashwagandha product

**Stop signals**

- Jaundice, dark urine, itching, nausea or upper-right abdominal pain: stop and get liver tests [https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/31991029/]
- Palpitations, tremor or heat intolerance (a possible thyroid effect; my inference) [https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/41740946/]
- Excessive drowsiness or ongoing GI upset [https://www.nccih.nih.gov/health/ashwagandha]
- No change in drive after 8-12 weeks (my inference; no trial of this extract exists)

### Report rank 8: Transparent Labs — KSM-66 Ashwagandha, 60 capsules

Two or more verification batches checked this product independently; all 2 records are kept so their differences stay visible.

#### Verification record 1 of 2

- **Verifier verdict:** acceptable — The right extract at the trial dose from a brand that certifies much of its range, but the label was not seen first-hand and its Informed Choice status (a weaker, non-batch programme) could not be confirmed, so it ranks below the NSF-listed Core Med KSM-66.
- **Product as verified:** Transparent Labs — KSM-66 Ashwagandha, 60 capsules
- **Product page:** https://www.transparentlabs.com/products/ksm-66-ashwagandha
- **Where sold:** - US: brand site, Vitamin Shoppe, Amazon, The Feed [https://www.transparentlabs.com/products/ksm-66-ashwagandha; https://www.vitaminshoppe.com/p/ksm-66-ashwagandha-600-mg-60-capsules/tpl-17909; https://thefeed.com/products/transparent-labs-ksm-66-ashwagandha].
- UK: Healf, Total Shape [https://healf.com/products/transparent-labs-ksm-66-ashwagandha-600mg; https://totalshape.com/products/transparent-labs-ksm-66-ashwagandha].
- Gulf: not confirmed.
Lookalike '.it.com' sites that copy the brand appeared in searches. Buy only from the brand or a major retailer.
- **Label verified:** no. Retailer and brand copy seen through search-engine summaries (Vitamin Shoppe, The Feed, a retailer listing, and the transparentlabs.com description, 7-8 Nov 2026). The manufacturer's Supplement Facts panel was not opened.
- **Proprietary blend:** no
- **Directions and daily amounts:** Serving: 1 capsule; 60 servings.
Active: KSM-66 ashwagandha root extract 600 mg, standardized to 5% withanolides, about 30 mg [https://thefeed.com/products/transparent-labs-ksm-66-ashwagandha; https://www.transparentlabs.com/products/ksm-66-ashwagandha].
Other ingredients: rice flour, hypromellose capsule, vegetable magnesium stearate, silica.
Directions: 1 capsule with a glass of water 1-2 times daily, i.e. 600-1,200 mg a day. For healthy adults 18 and over [https://www.vitaminshoppe.com/p/ksm-66-ashwagandha-600-mg-60-capsules/tpl-17909].
Take 1 capsule a day to match the trials.
- **Dose versus trials (matches):** KSM-66: 1 capsule gives 600 mg/day, the total of the 300 mg twice-daily regimen in Chauhan 2022 and Khanna 2026 [https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/35873404/; https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/41766918/].
The label's upper option, 2 capsules (1,200 mg/day), is double the trial dose. No sexual-function trial supports it (my inference).
The trials split the dose; here 1 capsule is taken once.
- **Certification:** Informed Choice (claimed). Informed Choice tests for banned substances through periodic blind retail sampling, a weaker scheme than every-batch programmes, and it is not treated as a hidden-drug screen in this ranking.
Current status: UNCONFIRMED.
- The product appeared in Informed Choice's own 'ashwagandha' search results captured 8 Nov 2026 [https://choice.wetestyoutrust.com/supplement-search?search=ashwagandha].
- Verified Supplement Data reported it on the Informed Choice list on 7 Oct 2026 [https://verifiedsupplementdata.com/ashwagandha/third-party-tested/].
- The listing page itself, with status and batches, could not be opened [https://choice.wetestyoutrust.com/supplement-search/transparent-labs/ksm-66-ashwagandha].
- These searches also return withdrawn products. Puresport's Ultra Ashwagandha KSM-66 was withdrawn on 10 Oct 2026 yet still appears in search [https://sport.wetestyoutrust.com/supplement-search/puresport/ultra-ashwagandha-ksm-66]. Transparent Labs' own L-Citrulline was 'voluntarily withdrawn' from Informed Choice [https://choice.wetestyoutrust.com/supplement-search/transparent-labs/l-citrulline].
Not NSF Certified for Sport: absent from NSF's 2,625-listing directory captured 8 Nov 2026, although 55 other Transparent Labs products (whey, Creatine HMB, Hydrate) are listed [https://www.nsfsport.com/certified-products/search-results.php?search=ashwagandha].
The KSM-66 ingredient itself is BSCG Certified Drug Free; that covers the ingredient, not this bottle [https://www.newhope.com/industry-insights/ksm-66-ashwagandha-now-bscg-certified-drug-free]. (certifier page: https://choice.wetestyoutrust.com/supplement-search/transparent-labs/ksm-66-ashwagandha; verified in the certifier's database: no; verifier marked the programme as screening for hidden drugs: no — see the challenge notes, which found PDE5-drug screening confirmed only for BSCG)
- **Manufacturer and regulatory record:** Company: Transparent Labs, a US sports-nutrition brand that uses certification for part of its range.
- 55 NSF Certified for Sport listings [https://www.nsfsport.com/certified-products/search-results.php?search=ashwagandha].
- Several Informed Choice listings, e.g. Vitality, certified 14 Aug 2023, with batches tested through Oct 2025 [https://choice.wetestyoutrust.com/supplement-search/transparent-labs/vitality].
None found: an 8 Nov 2026 web search for FDA warning letters naming Transparent Labs returned none.
Not checked directly:
- FDA's warning-letter, recall and Health Fraud databases
- Health Canada
- TGA
- MHRA
These checks were impossible in this pass: regulator sites were blocked, the web-search budget was used up, and Firecrawl credits had run out until 9 Nov 2026 05:26 UTC. Re-check before buying.
- **Evidence summary:** Same KSM-66 root-extract evidence as Core Med.
- Chauhan 2022: DISF-M score improved and serum testosterone rose vs placebo in 50 men with low desire [https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/35873404/].
- Khanna 2026: desire, sexual events, IIEF and semen parameters improved in 76 healthy men [https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/41766918/].
- Fornalik 2026 meta-analysis: testosterone +57 ng/dL in men, cortisol down [https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/41740946/].
- NCCIH: limited evidence for testosterone and sperm quality [https://www.nccih.nih.gov/health/ashwagandha].
The trials are short and small, use self-reported scales, and did not enrol men with ED.

**Safety risks**

- Liver: LiverTox likelihood B or C, onset 2-12 weeks, usually cholestatic with jaundice and itching [https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/NBK548536/; https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/31991029/].
- Thyroid: T4 rises modestly. Avoid with thyroid or autoimmune disease [https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/41740946/; https://www.nccih.nih.gov/health/ashwagandha].
- Taking 2 capsules (1,200 mg/day), which the label allows, doubles the trial dose with no added evidence (my inference).
- Drowsiness and GI upset; long-term safety beyond 3 months is unknown [https://www.nccih.nih.gov/health/ashwagandha].
- Avoid with hormone-sensitive prostate cancer [https://www.nccih.nih.gov/health/ashwagandha].
- Counterfeit risk: lookalike Transparent Labs storefronts on '.it.com' domains appeared in searches. Buy from the brand or a major retailer.
- Banned in Danish food supplements; a Dutch ban is pending [https://nutraingredients.com/Article/2023/05/17/Growing-concerns-over-safety-of-ashwagandha-in-EU-member-states; https://www.nutraingredients.com/Article/2026/07/06/timeline-will-authorities-de-throne-the-king-of-herbs/].
- Do not combine with Performer 8 or any other ashwagandha product.

**Interactions**

- Thyroid drugs, sedatives and anticonvulsants, immunosuppressants, diabetes drugs and antihypertensives: possible interactions per NCCIH [https://www.nccih.nih.gov/health/ashwagandha].
- Nitrates and PDE5 inhibitors: no documented interaction and none studied. Watch for dizziness if also on blood-pressure drugs (my inference) [https://www.nccih.nih.gov/health/ashwagandha].
- Anticoagulants and antiplatelets: no documented interaction and no study found [https://www.nccih.nih.gov/health/ashwagandha].
- SSRIs and MAOIs: no documented interaction and no study found [https://www.nccih.nih.gov/health/ashwagandha].

**Who should avoid it**

- Men with thyroid disease or on thyroid medication [https://www.nccih.nih.gov/health/ashwagandha]
- Men with autoimmune disease or on immunosuppressants [https://www.nccih.nih.gov/health/ashwagandha]
- Men with hormone-sensitive prostate cancer [https://www.nccih.nih.gov/health/ashwagandha]
- Anyone with liver disease or earlier herb-induced liver injury [https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/NBK548536/]
- Anyone before surgery, or taking sedatives or anticonvulsants [https://www.nccih.nih.gov/health/ashwagandha]
- Anyone still taking Performer 8 or another ashwagandha product

**Stop signals**

- Jaundice, dark urine, itching, nausea or right-upper abdominal pain: stop and get liver tests [https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/31991029/]
- Palpitations, tremor, heat intolerance (possible thyroid effect; my inference) [https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/41740946/]
- Excessive drowsiness or GI upset [https://www.nccih.nih.gov/health/ashwagandha]
- No benefit after 8 weeks: stop [https://www.nccih.nih.gov/health/ashwagandha]

- **Verifier verdict:** acceptable — The right root-only KSM-66 extract at the 600 mg trial dose with no caffeine, but its label was seen only in retailer copy and its Informed Choice listing (a sampling programme, not batch drug-screening) could not be confirmed as current, so it ranks below a batch-certified KSM-66.

Denmark bans ashwagandha supplements [https://nutraingredients.com/Article/2023/05/17/Growing-concerns-over-safety-of-ashwagandha-in-EU-member-states].

Retailer copy only (Vitamin Shoppe, Reach.dog, The Feed); Transparent Labs' own Supplement Facts panel was not seen [https://www.vitaminshoppe.com/p/ksm-66-ashwagandha-600-mg-60-capsules/tpl-17909; https://reach.dog/shop/transparent-labs/products/ksm-66-ashwagandha; https://thefeed.com/products/transparent-labs-ksm-66-ashwagandha]. Facts come from pages captured earlier in this workflow on 7-8 Nov 2026.
- **Proprietary blend:** no
- **Directions and daily amounts:** Directions (retailer copy): 'Take 1 capsule with a glass of water 1-2 times daily' [https://www.vitaminshoppe.com/p/ksm-66-ashwagandha-600-mg-60-capsules/tpl-17909; https://reach.dog/shop/transparent-labs/products/ksm-66-ashwagandha].
Per capsule: KSM-66 organic ashwagandha root extract 600 mg, standardized to 5% withanolides (about 30 mg) [https://thefeed.com/products/transparent-labs-ksm-66-ashwagandha; https://reach.dog/shop/transparent-labs/products/ksm-66-ashwagandha].
Other ingredients: rice flour, hypromellose capsule, vegetable magnesium stearate, silica.
Daily amount: 600 mg at 1 capsule; 1,200 mg at the 2-capsule maximum.
For comparison, Performer 8's directed 3 capsules give about 562 mg KSM-66 plus 150 mg caffeine (from the 4-capsule formula in the task brief); this product has no caffeine.
- **Dose versus trials (matches):** KSM-66 600 mg/day at 1 capsule vs the root extract at 300 mg twice daily (600 mg/day) for 8 weeks in both male sexual-function RCTs: a match.
- Chauhan 2022: 50 men with low desire; DISF-M improved. The testosterone rise is disputed by Examine [https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/35873404/; https://examine.com/summaries/study/9D6av1/].
- Khanna 2026: 76 healthy men aged 30-50; SDI-2 and IIEF improved [https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/41766918/].
A critique notes no correction for multiple comparisons in the KSM-66 trial [https://naturalhealthresearch.org/ashwagandha-root-extract-ksm-66-and-male-sexual-health-evidence-from-an-8-week-randomized-controlled-trial/].
The label's 2-capsule option (1,200 mg/day) is double the trial dose with no added evidence, so take 1 capsule.
- **Certification:** Informed Choice (identity/purity tier per this brief). It is a banned-substance programme, but uses regular blind retail sampling, so not every batch is tested [https://sport.wetestyoutrust.com/about/sport-vs-choice].
- The product appears in Informed Choice's own search results (captured 8 Nov 2026) with a listing URL [https://choice.wetestyoutrust.com/supplement-search?search=ashwagandha; https://choice.wetestyoutrust.com/supplement-search/transparent-labs/ksm-66-ashwagandha].

Current certification is therefore unconfirmed. Transparent Labs' L-Citrulline and Vegan Mass Gainer were voluntarily withdrawn from Informed Choice [https://choice.wetestyoutrust.com/supplement-search/transparent-labs/l-citrulline; https://choice.wetestyoutrust.com/supplement-search/transparent-labs/vegan-mass-gainer]. (certifier page: https://choice.wetestyoutrust.com/supplement-search/transparent-labs/ksm-66-ashwagandha; verified in the certifier's database: no; verifier marked the programme as screening for hidden drugs: no — see the challenge notes, which found PDE5-drug screening confirmed only for BSCG)

Vitality shows batches tested to Oct 2025 [https://choice.wetestyoutrust.com/supplement-search/transparent-labs/vitality]; two products were withdrawn [https://choice.wetestyoutrust.com/supplement-search/transparent-labs/l-citrulline; https://choice.wetestyoutrust.com/supplement-search/transparent-labs/vegan-mass-gainer].
- FDA warning letters: none found in an 8 Nov 2026 web search naming Transparent Labs.

- Not checked directly: FDA databases, recalls, Health Canada, TGA, MHRA (blocked this session).

- **Evidence summary:** This is the extract and daily dose used in the male trials:
- improved sexual-function scores over 8 weeks in low-desire and healthy men [https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/35873404/; https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/41766918/]
- testosterone +57 ng/dL and lower cortisol in a 23-RCT meta-analysis, with high heterogeneity [https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/41740946/]
The evidence is small, short and partly industry-linked [https://naturalhealthresearch.org/ashwagandha-root-extract-ksm-66-and-male-sexual-health-evidence-from-an-8-week-randomized-controlled-trial/]. It targets desire and stress, not erections. It is the cleanest like-for-like swap for Performer 8's main drive ingredient, minus the caffeine.

**Safety risks**

- Liver: LiverTox rates ashwagandha a likely or probable cause of liver injury, with onset 2-12 weeks after starting, usually cholestatic; fatal cases are described [https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/NBK548536/; https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/31991029/; https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC10531359/].
- Thyroid: T4 rose modestly in pooled RCTs [https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/41740946/]; NCCIH advises against use with thyroid disorders [https://www.nccih.nih.gov/health/ashwagandha].
- Drowsiness, stomach upset, diarrhoea and vomiting; long-term safety beyond about 3 months is unknown [https://www.nccih.nih.gov/health/ashwagandha].
- Taking 2 capsules (1,200 mg/day) doubles exposure beyond the studied dose (my inference).
- Regulatory: Denmark bans ashwagandha in supplements over thyroid and sex-hormone concerns [https://nutraingredients.com/Article/2023/05/17/Growing-concerns-over-safety-of-ashwagandha-in-EU-member-states].

**Interactions**

- Diabetes drugs and blood-pressure drugs: possible additive lowering [https://www.nccih.nih.gov/health/ashwagandha].
- Immunosuppressants, sedatives (benzodiazepines, sleep aids, alcohol), anticonvulsants and thyroid hormone: possible interactions [https://www.nccih.nih.gov/health/ashwagandha].
- Nitrates and PDE5 inhibitors: no documented interaction. Watch for dizziness if also on blood-pressure drugs (my inference).
- Anticoagulants/antiplatelets and SSRIs/MAOIs: no documented interaction in the sources reviewed [https://www.nccih.nih.gov/health/ashwagandha].

**Who should avoid it**

- Men with thyroid disease or on levothyroxine [https://www.nccih.nih.gov/health/ashwagandha]
- Men with autoimmune disease or on immunosuppressants [https://www.nccih.nih.gov/health/ashwagandha]
- Men with hormone-sensitive prostate cancer [https://www.nccih.nih.gov/health/ashwagandha]
- Anyone with liver disease or an earlier herb-induced liver injury [https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/NBK548536/]
- Anyone due for surgery, or taking sedatives or anticonvulsants [https://www.nccih.nih.gov/health/ashwagandha]
- Anyone still taking Performer 8 (double ashwagandha)
- Do not share with a partner who is or may become pregnant or is breastfeeding [https://www.nccih.nih.gov/health/ashwagandha]

**Stop signals**

- Jaundice, dark urine, pale stools, itching, nausea or upper-right abdominal pain: stop and get liver tests [https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/31991029/; https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/NBK548536/]
- Palpitations, tremor, heat intolerance or weight loss (a possible thyroid effect; my inference) [https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/41740946/]
- Excessive drowsiness or ongoing stomach upset [https://www.nccih.nih.gov/health/ashwagandha]
- No change in desire after 8 weeks (the trial length): stop, and do not run courses longer than about 3 months without review [https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/35873404/; https://www.nccih.nih.gov/health/ashwagandha]

Withdrawn products stay in these searches (Informed Sport's ashwagandha search still shows Puresport Ultra Ashwagandha KSM-66, withdrawn 10-Oct-2026).

### Report rank 9: Nutriburst — Ashwagandha KSM-66 gummies, 60

- **Verifier verdict:** acceptable — Root-only KSM-66 from a brand that appears in Informed Sport's database, but the label gives 300 mg/day, half the 600 mg/day used in the libido and testosterone trials, and neither the full gummy label nor current certification could be confirmed, so it is only a UK/Ireland fallback.
- **Product as verified:** Nutriburst — Ashwagandha KSM-66 gummies, 60
- **Product page:** https://www.hollandandbarrett.com/shop/product/nutriburst-ashwagandha-ksm-66-60-gummies-6100013797
- **Where sold:** UK and Ireland: Holland & Barrett, Boots (UK and Ireland), Rowlands Pharmacy, Chemco Pharmacy (a 50-gummy pack) and Beauty Bay. Czech Republic: Dr.Max. US and Gulf: not found. A 'nutriburst.vercel.app' storefront in search results looks unofficial, so buy from the large pharmacy chains (my inference).
- **Label verified:** no. Retailer listings only (Holland & Barrett and Dr.Max text, seen through search summaries). The Supplement Facts panel itself was not read.
- **Proprietary blend:** no
- **Directions and daily amounts:** Serving: 2 gummies daily (retailer directions). Per 2 gummies: KSM-66 ashwagandha root extract 300 mg.
- Holland & Barrett lists 'Ashwagandha Ksm66 Withania Somnifera Extract ... 300mg'.
- Dr.Max (Czech) says '300 mg extraktu z kořene ashwagandhy KSM-66 v denní dávce' (300 mg of KSM-66 root extract per daily dose).
- That is about 150 mg per gummy (my inference from the 2-gummy serving).
- Sugar or sweeteners, gelling agents and flavours were not captured.
A 60-gummy pack lasts 30 days.
- **Dose versus trials (underdosed):** KSM-66 at 300 mg/day vs 600 mg/day (300 mg twice daily for 8 weeks) in both male sexual-function RCTs:
- Chauhan 2022: 50 men with low sexual desire [https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/35873404/].
- Khanna 2026: 76 healthy men aged 30-50 [https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/41766918/].
The label dose is half the trial dose. Reaching 600 mg takes 4 gummies, double the label. The 2026 hormone meta-analysis saw a possible dose-response by withanolide content, so the lower dose may do less [https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/41740946/].
- **Certification:** Informed Sport (claimed); current status UNCONFIRMED.
- The product ('Ashwagandha KSM-66', formulation 'Chew') appears in Informed Sport's own search results, scraped live in this workflow on 7-8 Nov 2026 [https://sport.wetestyoutrust.com/supplement-search?search=ashwagandha].
- A search-engine summary of its listing shows 'Date Certified: 23-Dec-2022' [https://sport.wetestyoutrust.com/supplement-search/nutriburst/ashwagandha-ksm-66]. The listing page, with its status and batch list, was not opened.
- These searches also return withdrawn products. Puresport's Ultra Ashwagandha KSM-66 was withdrawn on 10 Oct 2026 but still appears [https://sport.wetestyoutrust.com/supplement-search/puresport/ultra-ashwagandha-ksm-66].
- If the certification is current, Informed Sport tests every batch before release for more than 285 substances banned in sport [https://sport.wetestyoutrust.com/about/sport-vs-choice]. Whether that panel includes PDE5 inhibitors was not checked; BSCG's menu does list them [https://www.bscg.org/bscg-banned-substance-testing-menu-v070725].
- Not in NSF's 2,625-listing Certified for Sport directory captured 8 Nov 2026 [https://www.nsfsport.com/certified-products/search-results.php?search=ashwagandha].
Check the batch number on the pack against the Informed Sport listing before buying. (certifier page: https://sport.wetestyoutrust.com/supplement-search/nutriburst/ashwagandha-ksm-66; verified in the certifier's database: no; verifier marked the programme as screening for hidden drugs: yes — see the challenge notes, which found PDE5-drug screening confirmed only for BSCG)
- **Manufacturer and regulatory record:** Company: Nutriburst, a UK gummy-supplement brand stocked by the main UK pharmacy chains. The manufacturer and facility were not identified.
- None found, but no brand-specific search of FDA, UK FSA/MHRA, Health Canada or TGA records was possible in this pass: regulator sites were unreachable, the web-search budget was used up and Firecrawl credits had run out until 9 Nov 2026. Re-check before buying.
- Ingredient rules in its markets: Denmark banned ashwagandha in food supplements in 2023, and the Netherlands has declared a ban (EU standstill period to 7 Dec 2026) [https://nutraingredients.com/Article/2023/05/17/Growing-concerns-over-safety-of-ashwagandha-in-EU-member-states; https://www.nutraingredients.com/Article/2026/07/06/timeline-will-authorities-de-throne-the-king-of-herbs/].
- KSM-66 is a root extract, so India's April 2026 move to root-only ashwagandha does not affect it [https://nutraingredients.com/Article/2026/04/28/india-bans-ashwagandha-leaf-use-in-any-form-industry-reacts].
- **Evidence summary:** KSM-66 ashwagandha has the best drive evidence of Performer 8's ingredients, but only at 600 mg/day.
- Chauhan 2022: DISF-M sexual-function scores and serum testosterone rose vs placebo in 50 men with low desire [https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/35873404/].
- Khanna 2026: desire, satisfying sexual events and overall IIEF improved in 76 healthy men, with no adverse events [https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/41766918/].
- A 23-RCT meta-analysis found testosterone +57 ng/dL in men and lower cortisol, with heterogeneity [https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/41740946/].
NCCIH calls the testosterone evidence limited [https://www.nccih.nih.gov/health/ashwagandha]. No trial at 300 mg/day for sexual outcomes was found in the gathered sources.

**Safety risks**

- Liver: LiverTox likelihood score B or C. Injury appears 2-12 weeks after starting, usually cholestatic with jaundice and itching [https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/NBK548536/; https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/31991029/].
- Thyroid: T4 rose modestly (+0.61 µg/dL) in pooled RCTs, and NCCIH advises against use with thyroid disorders [https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/41740946/; https://www.nccih.nih.gov/health/ashwagandha].
- Drowsiness, stomach upset, diarrhoea and vomiting can occur; safety beyond 3 months is unknown [https://www.nccih.nih.gov/health/ashwagandha].
- Gummy format: sugar or sugar-alcohol content was not captured. Check it if diabetic or prone to GI upset (my inference).
- Do not stack with Performer 8 or another ashwagandha product. Doubling the gummies to reach the trial dose would also double the sugar or sweetener (my inference).

**Interactions**

- Diabetes drugs and blood-pressure drugs (additive lowering), immunosuppressants, sedatives, anticonvulsants and thyroid hormone: possible interactions per NCCIH [https://www.nccih.nih.gov/health/ashwagandha].
- Nitrates and PDE5 inhibitors: no documented interaction. Watch for dizziness if also on blood-pressure drugs (my inference).
- Anticoagulants and antiplatelets, SSRIs and MAOIs: no documented interaction in the sources reviewed [https://www.nccih.nih.gov/health/ashwagandha].

**Who should avoid it**

- Men with thyroid disease or on thyroid medication [https://www.nccih.nih.gov/health/ashwagandha]
- Men with autoimmune disease or on immunosuppressants [https://www.nccih.nih.gov/health/ashwagandha]
- Men with hormone-sensitive prostate cancer [https://www.nccih.nih.gov/health/ashwagandha]
- Anyone with liver disease or a past herb-induced liver injury [https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/NBK548536/]
- Anyone due for surgery, or taking sedatives or anticonvulsants [https://www.nccih.nih.gov/health/ashwagandha]
- Anyone still taking Performer 8 or another ashwagandha product

**Stop signals**

- Jaundice, dark urine, itching, nausea or upper-right abdominal pain: stop and get liver tests [https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/31991029/]
- Palpitations, tremor or heat intolerance (a possible thyroid effect; my inference) [https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/41740946/]
- Excessive drowsiness or ongoing GI upset [https://www.nccih.nih.gov/health/ashwagandha]
- No benefit after 8-12 weeks; the trials ran 8 weeks [https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/35873404/]

  - Audit basis: raw page captures from this workflow (7-8 Nov 2026), not the verifier's summary; live re-opening was blocked today (network policy, spent WebSearch budget, Firecrawl out of credits until 2026-11-09 05:26 UTC).

  - 300 mg/day is half the trial dose, and doubling to 4 gummies doubles the sugar or sweetener load.
  - The thyroid case reports and the statin liver-monitoring advice (inference) apply. Denmark bans ashwagandha supplements, and the Netherlands has notified a ban.

### Report rank 10: Puresport (UK) — Tongkat Ali, 60 capsules

- **Verifier verdict:** acceptable — Fully disclosed at 400 mg/day, inside the studied range, and batch 7503 was Informed Sport-tested, but the certification was withdrawn on 10 Oct 2026, the extract is not a trial extract, and tongkat ali carries liver and EFSA safety cautions, so buy only batch 7503 or ask Puresport for current batch test results.
- **Product as verified:** Puresport (UK) — Tongkat Ali, 60 capsules
- **Product page:** https://puresport.co/products/tongkat-ali
- **Where sold:** UK/EU: Puresport's own shops [https://puresport.co/products/tongkat-ali; https://shop.puresport.co/products/tongkat-ali], Puresport's Amazon UK store (linked from Informed Sport) [https://www.amazon.co.uk/stores/Puresport/Homepage/page/BFE65AD5-FFEE-4A89-8CB3-F97D4D8B2E44] and XMiles, with Italian and French storefronts [https://xmiles.co.uk/collections/all/products/tongkat-ali; https://xmiles.co.uk/en-it/collections/puresport/products/tongkat-ali]. Informed Sport also links a US-locale Puresport collection [https://puresport.co/en-us/collections/all]. While certified, Informed Sport listed Europe only [https://sport.wetestyoutrust.com/supplement-search/puresport/tongkat-ali]. Gulf: not verified.
- **Label verified:** no. Puresport's own product pages (puresport.co and shop.puresport.co), quoted through search-engine summaries on 8 Nov 2026; the two agree but neither was opened directly [https://puresport.co/products/tongkat-ali; https://shop.puresport.co/products/tongkat-ali]. Other ingredients were not captured.
- **Proprietary blend:** no
- **Directions and daily amounts:** 60 capsules; take 2 capsules in the morning with food (= 30 days).
Per 2 capsules:
- Tongkat ali (Eurycoma longifolia) extract: 400 mg of a 40:1 extract (labelled as 16,000 mg root equivalent), providing 8 mg eurycomanone (2%)
- Piperine (Piper nigrum): 5 mg, from 5.26 mg of a 60:1 black-pepper extract
[https://puresport.co/products/tongkat-ali; https://shop.puresport.co/products/tongkat-ali]
Fully itemised; no stimulant or hormone listed.
- **Dose versus trials (partial):** - Tongkat ali: 400 mg/day vs the Physta water extract at 100-600 mg/day in testosterone trials (Tambi 2012: 200 mg; Ismail 2012: 300 mg; Henkel 2014: 400 mg) and Examine's 200-400 mg/day for libido [https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC9415500/; https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/21671978/; https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/23243445/; https://examine.com/supplements/tongkat-ali/]. The amount is in range, but this unnamed 40:1 extract is neither Physta nor LJ100, so equivalence is unproven.
- Erectile function: the meta-analysis of 2 RCTs found no significant IIEF-5 gain (+0.91) [https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/26365449/].
- Piperine 5 mg is a bioavailability enhancer, not an active for libido.
- **Certification:** Informed Sport (drug-screening tier), now WITHDRAWN. Read from Informed Sport's own database on 8 Nov 2026 (live page): 'This product is no longer certified. Please contact the brand for further information.' Date certified 17-Aug-2023; date withdrawn 10-Oct-2026; region Europe [https://sport.wetestyoutrust.com/supplement-search/puresport/tongkat-ali].
- The page notes that batch 7503 (expiry 31-Oct-2027) was tested on 10-Dec-2025 but is not listed 'due to a technical issue'; otherwise 'No batches are currently associated with this product' [https://sport.wetestyoutrust.com/supplement-search/puresport/tongkat-ali].
- So a bottle from batch 7503 was drug-screened; later batches were not (inference).
- The discovery claim of current certification is out of date.
- While certified, Informed Sport tested every batch for 285+ banned substances [https://sport.wetestyoutrust.com/about/sport-vs-choice]. (certifier page: https://sport.wetestyoutrust.com/supplement-search/puresport/tongkat-ali; verified in the certifier's database: no; verifier marked the programme as screening for hidden drugs: no — see the challenge notes, which found PDE5-drug screening confirmed only for BSCG)
- **Manufacturer and regulatory record:** Company: Puresport, a UK sports-nutrition brand. It held Informed Sport certification for this product from Aug 2023 until 10 Oct 2026, and calls it the first European tongkat ali certified by Informed Sport (brand claim) [https://sport.wetestyoutrust.com/supplement-search/puresport/tongkat-ali; https://puresport.co/products/tongkat-ali].
None found, but not searched: no FDA, MHRA, UK FSA, TGA or Health Canada search was run for Puresport in this workflow, and the project notes hold no record. Those sites are blocked in this session and the shared web-search budget was used up.
- **Evidence summary:** Tongkat ali targets Performer 8's testosterone and stress role, not erections. A 2022 meta-analysis found a testosterone rise (SMD 1.35), with high heterogeneity (87%) and publication bias in the hypogonadal subgroup [https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC9415500/]. Examine: 'the data aren't convincing' [https://examine.com/supplements/tongkat-ali/]. The ED meta-analysis was null [https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/26365449/]. For a healthy man in his 40s, expect at most a modest hormone or stress effect.

**Safety risks**

- Liver: LiverTox reports clinically apparent liver injury attributed to tongkat ali in body-builders [https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/NBK609015/; https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/39527684/]; a 47-year-old man developed jaundice and raised enzymes shortly after starting it [https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/38646387/].
- EFSA (2021) could not establish the safety of a tongkat root extract; it cited liver and kidney effects in a chronic toxicity study, and OPSS reports EFSA's concern about possible DNA damage in animals [https://efsa.onlinelibrary.wiley.com/doi/10.2903/j.efsa.2021.6937; https://www.opss.org/article/tongkat-ali-uses-and-safety-dietary-supplements].
- Category contamination: some tongkat products have contained lead, mercury or sildenafil analogues [https://examine.com/supplements/tongkat-ali/; https://www.opss.org/article/tongkat-ali-uses-and-safety-dietary-supplements]; this product's batch screening has lapsed (except batch 7503).
- Mild GI effects (abdominal pain, indigestion, constipation) in trials [https://examine.com/supplements/tongkat-ali/].
- Anti-doping: not on the WADA list, but its testosterone effect may fall under the 'similar biological effect' clause [https://examine.com/supplements/tongkat-ali/].
- Piperine's effect on the absorption of prescription drugs was not assessed this session.

**Interactions**

- Propranolol: tongkat may lower its levels; judged minor [https://examine.com/supplements/tongkat-ali/].
- CYP1A2 and CYP2A6 substrates: flagged by Examine (details not public) [https://examine.com/supplements/tongkat-ali/].
- Piperine: may alter how other drugs are absorbed; not verified this session, so ask a pharmacist if on any prescription drug.
- Testosterone therapy, finasteride or anti-androgens: hormonal overlap or opposition; no data, so do not combine (inference).
- PDE5 inhibitors, nitrates, antihypertensives, anticoagulants/antiplatelets, diabetes drugs, thyroid drugs, SSRIs/MAOIs and sedatives: no interaction documented in the sources reviewed [https://examine.com/supplements/tongkat-ali/].

**Who should avoid it**

- Anyone with liver disease or an earlier herb-induced liver injury [https://examine.com/supplements/tongkat-ali/; https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/NBK609015/]
- Men with prostate cancer or another hormone-sensitive condition, or on testosterone therapy (inference from its testosterone effect)
- Drug-tested athletes: tongkat may fall under WADA's 'similar biological effect' clause, and only batch 7503 of this product was tested [https://examine.com/supplements/tongkat-ali/; https://sport.wetestyoutrust.com/supplement-search/puresport/tongkat-ali]
- Men on propranolol or other drugs with a narrow safety margin, without pharmacist review [https://examine.com/supplements/tongkat-ali/]

**Stop signals**

- Jaundice, dark urine, pale stools, itching, nausea or right-upper abdominal pain [https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/38646387/; https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/NBK609015/]
- New acne, irritability, poor sleep or urinary symptoms (possible androgen effect, inference)
- Persistent abdominal pain or indigestion [https://examine.com/supplements/tongkat-ali/]
- No noticeable effect on drive after 8-12 weeks (the usual trial length): stop rather than continue indefinitely

**Challenge reviews**

- *certification-label lens — confirmed; downgrade to: acceptable*
  - Audit basis: raw page captures from this workflow (7-8 Nov 2026), not the verifier's summary; live re-opening was blocked today (network policy, spent WebSearch budget, Firecrawl out of credits until 2026-11-09 05:26 UTC).
  - Withdrawal directly confirmed from the raw 8 Nov 2026 capture of https://sport.wetestyoutrust.com/supplement-search/puresport/tongkat-ali: 'This product is no longer certified'; withdrawn 10-Oct-2026; certified 17-Aug-2023; Europe only. Batch 7503 (tested 10-Dec-2025, expires 31-Oct-2027) is described in the text, yet the table says 'No batches are currently associated with this product'. The 'Certified Product' heading on the page is template text.
  - Puresport's Ultra Ashwagandha KSM-66 was withdrawn the same day (https://sport.wetestyoutrust.com/supplement-search/puresport/ultra-ashwagandha-ksm-66).
  - Label (400 mg of a 40:1 extract with 8 mg eurycomanone, plus 5 mg piperine, per 2 capsules) comes from Puresport's pages via search index only.

  - Missed caution: the tongkat package insert's contraindications (blood-sugar-lowering drugs, prostate cancer).
  - UK/EU caution (inference): after EFSA's 2021 opinion there is no EU novel-food authorisation, so a UK brand selling into the EU sits in a regulatory grey zone.
  - PDE5 caveat: even batch 7503's Informed Sport screen may not have covered PDE5 inhibitors, which are not WADA-prohibited.

### Report rank 11: NutraBio — Tongkat Ali (LJ100) 200 mg, 60 vegetable capsules

- **Verifier verdict:** acceptable — The right kind of standardized extract at the studied 200 mg/day, fully disclosed and with no warning letter found, but uncertified and without a public lot COA in a category known for sildenafil spiking and heavy metals; get a lot COA (heavy metals plus a PDE5 screen) before use, and rank it below the certified options.
- **Product as verified:** NutraBio — Tongkat Ali (LJ100) 200 mg, 60 vegetable capsules
- **Product page:** https://nutrabio.com/products/tongkat-ali-lj100-200mg
- **Where sold:** - US: NutraBio, Vitacost [https://nutrabio.com/products/tongkat-ali-lj100-200mg; https://www.vitacost.com/products/nutrabio-tongkat-ali-60-capsules-200-mg-per-capsule-123311].
- New Zealand: supplements.co.nz [https://www.supplements.co.nz/a/s/products/nutrabio-tongkat-ali-200mg-60-caps].
- Saudi Arabia: Nahdi stocks NutraBio's Unbound (tongkat plus fadogia), not this product [https://www.nahdionline.com/en-sa/nutrabio-unbound-tongkat-ali-fadogia-agrestis-120-capsules/pdp/103493670].
- UK/EU: none found.
- **Label verified:** no. NutraBio product page plus Vitacost and RangeMe listings, seen through search-engine summaries on 8 Nov 2026 [https://nutrabio.com/products/tongkat-ali-lj100-200mg; https://www.rangeme.com/brands/nutrabio/products/tongkat-ali-lj100-200mg-60-vegetable-capsule]. The full Supplement Facts panel (excipients, warnings) was not seen.
- **Proprietary blend:** no
- **Directions and daily amounts:** Serving: 1 capsule; 60 per bottle.
Active: tongkat ali (Eurycoma longifolia) root extract LJ100, 200 mg, standardized to 40% glycosaponins and 22% eurypeptides.
Directions: 1 capsule daily as needed, i.e. 200 mg a day [https://nutrabio.com/products/tongkat-ali-lj100-200mg; https://www.vitacost.com/products/nutrabio-tongkat-ali-60-capsules-200-mg-per-capsule-123311].
Excipients and warnings were not captured.
- **Dose versus trials (partial):** 200 mg/day of a standardized water extract matches the most-studied dose:
- Physta 200 mg/day for 12 weeks in men aged 50-70 (Chinnappan 2021) [https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC8254464/]
- Physta 200 mg/day for 1 month in late-onset hypogonadism (Tambi 2012) [https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/21671978/]
- Examine's libido range is 200-400 mg/day [https://examine.com/supplements/tongkat-ali/]
But nearly all trials used Physta, not LJ100 [https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC9415500/]. No LJ100-specific controlled trial was found in the gathered sources; a claim that 200 mg LJ100 raised testosterone in ageing men within 2 weeks comes from an unverified review. Equivalence between the two extracts is assumed, not shown.
- **Certification:** None found.
- NSF Certified for Sport: NSF's 2,625-listing directory captured 8 Nov 2026 contains no tongkat product and no NutraBio product [https://www.nsfsport.com/certified-products/search-results.php?search=ashwagandha].
- Informed Sport: not among the tongkat results (Puresport, withdrawn; Biogen; Ten Percent Club; Momentous) [https://sport.wetestyoutrust.com/supplement-search?search=tongkat].
- Informed Choice: not among the tongkat results, which show only VH Nutrition [https://choice.wetestyoutrust.com/supplement-search?search=tongkat].
- BSCG, USP and ConsumerLab were not checked this session.
NutraBio says its products are 'verified through rigorous third-party testing'. That does not count, and no public lot certificate of analysis was found.
Ingredient-level positives, both supplier claims: HP Ingredients' LJ100 has GRAS status and an Intertek safety confirmation [https://supplysidesj.com/supplement-regulations/hp-ingredients-lj100-receives-gras-status; https://www.nutraceuticalsworld.com/live_from_shows/hp-ingredients-lj-100-receives-safety-confirmation-by-intertek/]. (certifier page: none; verified in the certifier's database: no; verifier marked the programme as screening for hidden drugs: no — see the challenge notes, which found PDE5-drug screening confirmed only for BSCG)
- **Manufacturer and regulatory record:** Company: NutraBio, a US brand that markets full label disclosure. Facility details were not verified this session.
None found: an 8 Nov 2026 web search for FDA warning letters naming NutraBio returned none; only Double Wood's 2020 letter over COVID-19 claims surfaced. The product is not on the first page of FDA's sexual-enhancement hidden-drug list (10 of 441 entries captured) [https://www.fda.gov/drugs/medication-health-fraud-notifications/sexual-enhancement-and-energy-product-notifications].
Not checked directly:
- FDA's warning-letter, recall and Health Fraud databases
- Health Canada
- TGA
- MHRA
These checks were impossible in this pass: regulator sites were blocked, the web-search budget was used up, and Firecrawl credits had run out until 9 Nov 2026 05:26 UTC. Re-check before buying.
- **Evidence summary:** Same tongkat evidence base as Momentous.
- Testosterone rose in pooled RCTs, but the data are heterogeneous and show publication bias [https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC9415500/].
- Physta 200 mg/day lowered cortisol and raised total testosterone over 12 weeks in men aged 50-70 [https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC8254464/].
- Erectile-function meta-analysis: null (WMD 0.91 IIEF-5 points) [https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/26365449/].
LJ100's own controlled evidence is thinner than Physta's. Expect a possible hormone and stress effect, not better erections.

**Safety risks**

- Liver: isolated reports of clinically apparent liver injury, including a 47-year-old man with jaundice [https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/NBK609015/; https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/38646387/].
- EFSA (2021) could not establish safety of a tongkat root extract; it cited liver and kidney toxicity findings and possible genotoxicity [https://efsa.onlinelibrary.wiley.com/doi/10.2903/j.efsa.2021.6937; https://www.opss.org/article/tongkat-ali-uses-and-safety-dietary-supplements].
- No batch drug screening, in a category where products have contained sildenafil analogues, lead and mercury. OPSS: 'Without laboratory testing there is no way to know the actual ingredients (or their amounts) in a product' [https://www.opss.org/article/tongkat-ali-uses-and-safety-dietary-supplements; https://examine.com/supplements/tongkat-ali/].
- Mild GI effects [https://examine.com/supplements/tongkat-ali/].
- Hormonal effects on the prostate are unknown over the long term (my inference).
- Combining with ashwagandha is unstudied; start one product at a time (my inference).

**Interactions**

- Propranolol: blood levels may fall; Examine rates this minor [https://examine.com/supplements/tongkat-ali/].
- CYP1A2 and CYP2A6 substrates: possible interaction; the evidence is limited [https://examine.com/supplements/tongkat-ali/].
- Nitrates and PDE5 inhibitors: no documented direct interaction. An unscreened tongkat product could hide a sildenafil analogue, which is dangerous with nitrates. Men on nitrates should not use an unscreened tongkat [https://www.opss.org/article/tongkat-ali-uses-and-safety-dietary-supplements].
- Other antihypertensives, diabetes drugs, anticoagulants and antiplatelets, thyroid drugs, SSRIs and MAOIs, sedatives: no human interaction data found [https://examine.com/supplements/tongkat-ali/].
- Testosterone therapy: possible additive effect (my inference).

**Who should avoid it**

- Men taking nitrates, because an unscreened tongkat could contain a PDE5 drug [https://www.opss.org/article/tongkat-ali-uses-and-safety-dietary-supplements]
- Anyone with liver disease or earlier drug-induced liver injury [https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/NBK609015/]
- Men with prostate cancer or other hormone-sensitive conditions, and men on testosterone therapy (my inference)
- Anyone with kidney disease (my inference from EFSA's findings) [https://efsa.onlinelibrary.wiley.com/doi/10.2903/j.efsa.2021.6937]
- Drug-tested athletes [https://examine.com/supplements/tongkat-ali/]

**Stop signals**

- Jaundice, dark urine, nausea, vomiting or abdominal pain: stop and get liver tests [https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/38646387/]
- Flushing, headache, nasal congestion, visual changes or dizziness soon after a dose, which suggest a hidden PDE5 inhibitor: stop and report it [https://www.fda.gov/drugs/medication-health-fraud-notifications/sexual-enhancement-and-energy-product-notifications]
- Insomnia, irritability or acne (my inference)
- No effect after 12 weeks [https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC8254464/]

**Challenge reviews**

- *certification-label lens — confirmed; downgrade to: none*
  - Audit basis: raw page captures from this workflow (7-8 Nov 2026), not the verifier's summary; live re-opening was blocked today (network policy, spent WebSearch budget, Firecrawl out of credits until 2026-11-09 05:26 UTC).
  - No certification: no NutraBio product is in the NSF directory of 8 Nov 2026, and neither the Informed Sport tongkat search (Puresport, Biogen, Ten Percent Club, Momentous) nor the Informed Choice one (VH Nutrition only) lists it.
  - The 'every batch third-party tested' wording appears only for NutraBio's Unbound product; no public lot certificate was found for this LJ100 product.

- *safety-evidence lens — corrected; downgrade to: none*
  - Missed caution: a tongkat package insert (Biogen) contraindicates use with blood-sugar-lowering drugs and in prostate cancer, so add diabetes drugs to the interactions.
  - It has no certification and no public certificate of analysis in a category known for sildenafil spiking, as the verifier flagged. Any lot certificate should name PDE5 inhibitors and their analogues, not just heavy metals.
  - LJ100's own controlled evidence is thin, because most trials used Physta, as the verifier says.

### Report rank 12: Double Wood Supplements — LJ100 Tongkat Ali Extract (sold as 'LJ-100 Tongkat Ali Supplement'), 120 capsules, 100 mg per capsule

- **Verifier verdict:** acceptable — The right type of extract with a disclosed 100 mg dose and a public per-lot COA, but it has no drug-screening certification in a category known for sildenafil spiking, the company had a 2020 FDA warning letter over drug claims for another product, and the label dose sits at the bottom of the studied range.
- **Product as verified:** Double Wood Supplements — LJ100 Tongkat Ali Extract (sold as 'LJ-100 Tongkat Ali Supplement'), 120 capsules, 100 mg per capsule
- **Product page:** https://doublewoodsupplements.com/products/lj100-tongkat-ali-extract
- **Where sold:** US: brand site and resellers such as Hebron Nutrition. The US page links an 'International' storefront (doublewoodsupplements.store), so delivery outside the US looks possible, but UK/EU and Gulf delivery of this item was not confirmed. The US page showed the item as backordered when read on 7 Nov 2026.
- **Label verified:** yes. Double Wood product page, read live on 7 Nov 2026: directions, FAQ and the on-page certificate of analysis (lot AILUH). The Supplement Facts image itself was not transcribed. https://doublewoodsupplements.com/products/lj100-tongkat-ali-extract
- **Proprietary blend:** no
- **Directions and daily amounts:** Serving: 1 capsule. Brand directions: 'Take 1 capsule daily, preferably in the morning or early afternoon, with or without food.' The FAQ says: 'Take 1 capsule (100mg) daily.'
Per day at the label dose: LJ100 Eurycoma longifolia root extract 100 mg (HP Ingredients' patented standardized extract).
- The brand COA for lot AILUH reports 100.8 mg per serving against a spec of 'not less than 100 mg'.
- A reseller listing gives the standardization as 40% glycosaponins and 22% eurypeptides; this was not seen on the panel.
- Excipients were not captured.
- A search-engine snippet of the same page mentioned 2-3 capsules (200-300 mg) a day, but the live page read on 7 Nov 2026 says 1 capsule, so the snippet looks out of date.
Single ingredient; no blend.
- **Dose versus trials (partial):** LJ100 at the label dose gives 100 mg/day. The human trials used standardized water extracts, nearly all Physta, a related Malaysian extract. LJ100 equivalence is assumed, not shown [https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC9415500/].
- Chinnappan 2021: 105 men aged 50-70 took Physta 100 mg or 200 mg/day for 12 weeks. Total testosterone rose in both groups; cortisol fell only with 200 mg [https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC8254464/].
- Tambi 2012: 200 mg/day for 1 month in late-onset hypogonadism, uncontrolled [https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/21671978/].
- Examine gives 100-600 mg/day for testosterone and 200-400 mg/day for libido [https://examine.com/supplements/tongkat-ali/].
So the label dose equals the lowest dose that raised testosterone. Two capsules (200 mg) would reach the libido and cortisol dose but go beyond the label directions.
The erectile-function meta-analysis was null: IIEF-5 WMD 0.91 (95% CI -1.50 to 3.33) [https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/26365449/].
The brand's own page says the Hamzah 2003 study it cites did not use LJ100 [https://doublewoodsupplements.com/products/lj100-tongkat-ali-extract].
- **Certification:** None. No listing was found in any certifier database:
- NSF Certified for Sport: no Double Wood product and no tongkat product among the 2,625 listings captured from nsfsport.com on 8 Nov 2026 [https://www.nsfsport.com/certified-products/search-results.php?search=ashwagandha].
- Informed Sport: its 'tongkat' search lists only Puresport (withdrawn), Biogen, Ten Percent Club and Momentous [https://sport.wetestyoutrust.com/supplement-search?search=tongkat].
- Informed Choice: its 'tongkat' search lists only VH Nutrition [https://choice.wetestyoutrust.com/supplement-search?search=tongkat].
- BSCG, USP and ConsumerLab: their databases could not be searched in this pass.
Weaker but real positive: Double Wood publishes a per-lot certificate of analysis from 'ISO/IEC 17025 accredited labs' covering identity, potency, heavy metals by ICP-MS and a microbial panel. Lot AILUH results:
- LJ100 100.8 mg/serving
- lead 0.184 ppm (spec 1.3 ppm or less)
- cadmium below 0.072 ppm; mercury below 0.017 ppm; arsenic 0.105 ppm
Buyers can look up the COA for their bottle's lot number on the product page [https://doublewoodsupplements.com/products/lj100-tongkat-ali-extract]. No drug (PDE5) screen is shown on the COA.
Ingredient-level supplier claims: LJ100 has self-affirmed GRAS status and an Intertek safety review [https://www.supplysidesj.com/supplement-regulations/hp-ingredients-lj100-receives-gras-status; https://www.nutraceuticalsworld.com/live_from_shows/hp-ingredients-lj-100-receives-safety-confirmation-by-intertek/]. (certifier page: none; verified in the certifier's database: no; verifier marked the programme as screening for hidden drugs: no — see the challenge notes, which found PDE5-drug screening confirmed only for BSCG)
- **Manufacturer and regulatory record:** Company: Double Wood Supplements, a US direct-to-consumer brand with US and international storefronts.
- FDA warning letter: Double Wood LLC, ref. 593766, dated 23 Jul 2020. FDA reviewed the firm's websites in June 2020, found orders being taken for a product called Dycetin, and said the website claims showed it was intended for use as a drug. This was a claims (unapproved drug/misbranding) case about a different product, not adulteration or a hidden drug. The specific claims were not captured. The link between Double Wood LLC and this brand rests on the name match [https://www.fda.gov/inspections-compliance-enforcement-and-criminal-investigations/warning-letters/double-wood-llc-593766-07232020; https://www.globalkeysolutions.net/records/warning_letter/double-wood-llc/2b03ef31-71a1-4913-b593-0b0ee339b493].
- FDA hidden-drug list: no Double Wood entry was seen. Only the first page of FDA's 441 sexual-enhancement notifications was captured, so absence is not confirmed [https://www.fda.gov/drugs/medication-health-fraud-notifications/sexual-enhancement-and-energy-product-notifications].
- Not checked directly: FDA recall database, Health Canada, TGA and MHRA. In this pass regulator sites were unreachable, the web-search budget was used up and Firecrawl credits had run out until 9 Nov 2026, so re-check before buying.
- **Evidence summary:** Tongkat ali is a testosterone and stress ingredient, not an erection ingredient.
- Pooled RCTs show a rise in total testosterone (SMD 1.35), but with high heterogeneity and publication bias [https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC9415500/].
- In men aged 50-70, Physta 100-200 mg/day raised testosterone over 12 weeks, and 200 mg lowered cortisol [https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC8254464/].
- The erectile-function meta-analysis (2 RCTs, 139 men) was not significant [https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/26365449/].
No LJ100-specific controlled trial was found in the gathered sources. Expect at most a modest hormone, mood or drive effect after 8-12 weeks.

**Safety risks**

- Liver: LiverTox reports clinically apparent liver injury attributed to tongkat ali in bodybuilders. A 47-year-old man developed jaundice soon after starting it and recovered after stopping [https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/NBK609015/; https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/38646387/].
- EFSA (2021) said tongkat root extract may cause DNA damage in animals and that its overall safety is not established; a chronic animal study showed liver and kidney effects [https://efsa.onlinelibrary.wiley.com/doi/10.2903/j.efsa.2021.6937; https://www.opss.org/article/tongkat-ali-uses-and-safety-dietary-supplements].
- Category risk: tongkat products have been found with lead, mercury and undisclosed sildenafil analogues. This product's COA covers heavy metals, but no drug screen is shown [https://examine.com/supplements/tongkat-ali/; https://www.opss.org/article/tongkat-ali-uses-and-safety-dietary-supplements].
- Mild GI upset and itching have been reported. The brand notes mild digestive discomfort, mainly on an empty stomach [https://www.opss.org/article/tongkat-ali-uses-and-safety-dietary-supplements; https://doublewoodsupplements.com/products/lj100-tongkat-ali-extract].
- Hormone-active; long-term prostate effects are unknown (my inference).
- No stimulant or hormone is listed.

**Interactions**

- Propranolol: tongkat may lower propranolol blood levels; Examine rates this minor because blood pressure and heart rate did not change [https://examine.com/supplements/tongkat-ali/].
- CYP1A2 and CYP2A6 substrates: possible interaction; the evidence is limited [https://examine.com/supplements/tongkat-ali/].
- Nitrates and PDE5 inhibitors: no documented direct interaction. The danger is a product contaminated with a sildenafil analogue, which a lack of drug screening cannot exclude [https://www.opss.org/article/tongkat-ali-uses-and-safety-dietary-supplements].
- Antihypertensives, anticoagulants and antiplatelets, diabetes drugs, thyroid drugs, SSRIs and MAOIs, and sedatives: no human interaction data found [https://examine.com/supplements/tongkat-ali/].
- Testosterone therapy: possible additive effect (my inference).

**Who should avoid it**

- Men taking nitrates, because an unscreened tongkat product could hide a PDE5 drug [https://www.opss.org/article/tongkat-ali-uses-and-safety-dietary-supplements]
- Anyone with liver disease or a past herb-induced liver injury [https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/NBK609015/]
- Men with prostate cancer or another hormone-sensitive condition, or on testosterone therapy (my inference)
- Anyone with kidney disease (my inference from EFSA's animal findings) [https://efsa.onlinelibrary.wiley.com/doi/10.2903/j.efsa.2021.6937]
- Drug-tested athletes [https://examine.com/supplements/tongkat-ali/]

**Stop signals**

- Jaundice, dark urine, itching, nausea or upper-right abdominal pain: stop and get liver tests [https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/38646387/]
- Flushing, headache, nasal congestion, visual changes or dizziness soon after a dose (typical PDE5-drug effects) suggest a hidden drug: stop and report it (my inference from FDA's hidden-drug warnings) [https://www.fda.gov/drugs/medication-health-fraud-notifications/sexual-enhancement-and-energy-product-notifications]
- Insomnia, irritability or acne (my inference)
- No noticeable effect after 12 weeks, the length of the main trial [https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC8254464/]

**Challenge reviews**

- *certification-label lens — confirmed; downgrade to: none*
  - Audit basis: raw page captures from this workflow (7-8 Nov 2026), not the verifier's summary; live re-opening was blocked today (network policy, spent WebSearch budget, Firecrawl out of credits until 2026-11-09 05:26 UTC).

  - Directions on the live page are 'Take 1 capsule daily' and the copy mentions 'a smaller serving'; the serving-size image was replaced on 7-Oct-2026, so older bottles or listings may say 2-3 capsules. Follow the bottle in hand.

- *safety-evidence lens — corrected; downgrade to: none*
  - Missed caution: a tongkat package insert (Biogen) contraindicates use with blood-sugar-lowering drugs and in prostate cancer.
  - The certificate of analysis covers identity, potency, heavy metals and microbes, but not PDE5 drugs, as the verifier flagged.
  - The 2020 FDA warning letter to 'Double Wood LLC' concerned COVID-related drug claims for a different product (Dycetin), and the link to this brand rests only on the name.

### Report rank 13: Natural Factors — Stress-Relax Pure Saffron Extract (affron) 28 mg, 30 vegetarian capsules (a 60-count is also sold)

- **Verifier verdict:** acceptable — A disclosed single-ingredient 28 mg affron capsule close to the 30 mg/day of the one positive male trial, but that trial was in fluoxetine-related sexual problems and did not improve desire, the largest male ED trial was negative, and the finished product is uncertified, so it fits only men whose low drive is linked to SSRI use, with their prescriber's agreement.
- **Product as verified:** Natural Factors — Stress-Relax Pure Saffron Extract (affron) 28 mg, 30 vegetarian capsules (a 60-count is also sold)
- **Product page:** https://naturalfactors.com/products/stress-relax-affron-saffron-extract
- **Where sold:** Canada: Natural Factors' Canadian French-language page and retailers such as Vitasave and Natural Vibe. US: naturalfactors.com. iHerb international storefronts list it (Sri Lanka and Pakistan pages seen); iHerb also runs Gulf storefronts, so Gulf delivery is likely but was not confirmed for this item. In the EU, affron 28 mg capsules are sold under other brands (e.g., Vitakruid in the Netherlands).
- **Label verified:** no. Product titles on iHerb ('28 mg per Capsule', 30 vegetarian capsules) and the Natural Factors product name. The panel, directions and excipients were not read.
- **Proprietary blend:** no
- **Directions and daily amounts:** Serving: 1 vegetarian capsule. Per capsule: saffron (Crocus sativus) stigma extract (affron) 28 mg. Directions and other ingredients were not captured; at 1 capsule a day the intake is 28 mg. Single ingredient.
- **Dose versus trials (partial):** affron 28 mg/day compared with:
- Modabbernia 2012: saffron 15 mg twice daily (30 mg/day) for 4 weeks in 36 men with fluoxetine-related sexual impairment. Erectile function and intercourse satisfaction improved vs placebo; desire and orgasm did not [https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/22552758/].
- Safarinejad 2010: 30 mg twice daily (60 mg/day) for 12 weeks in 346 men with ED. No IIEF domain improved [https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/20520621/].
- 30 mg/day is the usual dose in depression trials [https://publish.csiro.au/hc/Fulltext/HC22067].
- affron's own trials used 28 mg/day for mood (a brand-cited 2017 RCT in 128 adults) [https://outworknutrition.com/blogs/learn/affron%C2%AE-natural-support-for-sleep-mood-and-emotional-well-being].
The amount is close to the positive SSRI trial, but the preparation differs, and no affron trial on male sexual function was found.
- **Certification:** None for the finished product.
- Informed Sport: a 'saffron' search returned no results (scraped 7-8 Nov 2026) [https://sport.wetestyoutrust.com/supplement-search?search=saffron].
- NSF Certified for Sport: no saffron product and no Natural Factors product among the 2,625 listings captured 8 Nov 2026 [https://www.nsfsport.com/certified-products/search-results.php?search=ashwagandha].
- Informed Choice, BSCG, USP and ConsumerLab: not searched for this product in this pass.
Ingredient level only: the affron raw material holds 'Informed Ingredient' certification, which covers the ingredient, not this capsule [https://ingredient.wetestyoutrust.com/news/affron-awarded-new-mood-support-patent]. A listing says Natural Factors DNA-authenticates the extract (brand claim). (certifier page: https://sport.wetestyoutrust.com/supplement-search?search=saffron; verified in the certifier's database: no; verifier marked the programme as screening for hidden drugs: no — see the challenge notes, which found PDE5-drug screening confirmed only for BSCG)
- **Manufacturer and regulatory record:** Company: Natural Factors, a Canadian supplement maker selling in Canada and the US.
- One combined web search on 8 Nov 2026 for 2024-2026 recalls and warnings (covering Korea Ginseng Corp., Natural Factors and Sopharma) returned nothing on Natural Factors. The brand did not surface in the results at all, so this is weak evidence.
- Not checked directly: FDA's warning-letter, recall and hidden-drug databases; Health Canada recalls and the product's Canadian licence (NPN); TGA; MHRA. In this pass regulator sites were unreachable, the web-search budget was used up and Firecrawl credits had run out, so re-check before buying.
- **Evidence summary:** Mixed and population-specific.
- Ho 2025 meta-analysis (14 RCTs, 1,227 men with ED): saffron and ginseng drove the erectile-function benefit [https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/41696741/].
- Borrelli 2018 rated saffron results 'mixed' [https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/29633089/].
- The largest male trial (346 men, vs sildenafil) was negative [https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/20520621/].
- The clearest positive is in fluoxetine-related sexual dysfunction, for erectile function rather than desire [https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/22552758/].
There is no good evidence for raising libido in healthy men, and affron's own trials are in mood.

**Safety risks**

- Generally well tolerated at about 30 mg/day. In the two male trials, side effects did not differ from comparator or placebo [https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/20520621/; https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/22552758/].
- Toxic only at gram doses: 5 g/day or more is toxic and has uterine-stimulant effects; up to 1.5 g/day is thought safe and 10-20 g may be fatal. 28 mg is far below these levels [https://publish.csiro.au/hc/Fulltext/HC22067; https://www.drugs.com/npp/saffron.html].
- Bleeding: caution is advised with antiplatelet or anticoagulant drugs [https://publish.csiro.au/hc/Fulltext/HC22067].
- Reported effects at 30 mg/day include dry mouth, nausea, dizziness, appetite change and anxiety, at rates similar to antidepressant comparators [https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC5339650/].
- Long-term data are limited: most trials are small and short, typically about 6 weeks at 30 mg/day [https://publish.csiro.au/hc/Fulltext/HC22067].

**Interactions**

- Anticoagulants and antiplatelets (e.g., warfarin, aspirin, clopidogrel): caution, possible added bleeding risk [https://publish.csiro.au/hc/Fulltext/HC22067].
- SSRIs: saffron was studied as an add-on to fluoxetine for 4 weeks without excess adverse events [https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/22552758/]. MAOIs: no data found.
- Nitrates, PDE5 inhibitors, antihypertensives, diabetes drugs, thyroid drugs and sedatives: no documented interaction found in the sources reviewed.

**Who should avoid it**

- Men on anticoagulants or antiplatelets, unless their doctor agrees [https://publish.csiro.au/hc/Fulltext/HC22067]
- Men on antidepressants, unless their prescriber agrees (my inference) [https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/22552758/]
- Men seeking a libido boost with no SSRI-related cause: the trials do not support it [https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/20520621/; https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/22552758/]

**Stop signals**

- Unusual bruising, bleeding gums or nosebleeds, especially on blood thinners [https://publish.csiro.au/hc/Fulltext/HC22067]
- Nausea, dizziness, anxiety or a change in mood [https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC5339650/]
- No change in erections or drive after 4-6 weeks; the positive trial ran 4 weeks [https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/22552758/]
