### Report rank 14: Thorne — Advanced Testosterone Support, 60 capsules

- **Verifier verdict:** acceptable — NSF-certified, fully itemised and from a top-tier maker with a trial-matched shilajit dose, but the combination is untested, the 150 mg luteolin has no human data, the ashwagandha includes leaf, and the label says not to use it while trying to conceive.
- **Product as verified:** Thorne — Advanced Testosterone Support, 60 capsules
- **Product page:** https://www.thorne.com/products/dp/advanced-testosterone-support
- **Where sold:** - US: NSF lists the country of sale as the United States [https://www.nsfsport.com/certified-products/listing-detail.php?id=1822465]. Sold by Thorne, Fullscript, Emerson Ecologics, Vitacost, iHerb's US site and the Mayo Clinic store [https://fullscript.com/catalog/products/advanced-testosterone-support; https://www.vitacost.com/products/thorne-advanced-testosterone-support-60-capsules-147640; https://www.iherb.com/pr/thorne-advanced-testosterone-support-60-capsules/147640; https://store.mayoclinic.com/thorne-advanced-testosterone-support.html].
- UK: Healf [https://healf.com/products/thorne-advanced-testosterone-support].
- EU: Smeets & Graas, Netherlands [https://www.smeetsengraas.nl/en/thorne/advanced-testosterone-support]. The announced Dutch ashwagandha ban and Denmark's ban could restrict EU sale [https://www.nutraingredients.com/Article/2026/07/06/timeline-will-authorities-de-throne-the-king-of-herbs/].
- Gulf: the 8 Nov 2026 UAE/Saudi search found no listing. iHerb delivery to the Gulf was not confirmed.
- **Label verified:** yes. - Supplement Facts image hosted by NSF for listing 1822465, transcribed earlier in this workflow on 8 Nov 2026 [http://info.nsf.org/Certified/Common/cfs/4H980/C0385636/Thorne/Advanced%20Testosterone%20Support/1822465/Label_01.PNG].
- Directions and warning from Thorne-sourced copies on Fullscript and Emerson Ecologics [https://fullscript.com/catalog/products/advanced-testosterone-support; https://emersonecologics.com/products/detail/Thorne/advanced-testosterone-support/U3ByZWU6OlByb2R1Y3QtMTA3NzQ1/TH0465].
- Branded-ingredient names (PrimaVie, Shoden) from Thorne's product and men's-health pages and retailer copy, seen through search summaries [https://www.thorne.com/products/dp/advanced-testosterone-support; https://thorne.com/mens-health].
- **Proprietary blend:** no
- **Directions and daily amounts:** Serving: 2 capsules (the daily dose); 30 servings.
Per 2 capsules [NSF-hosted label: http://info.nsf.org/Certified/Common/cfs/4H980/C0385636/Thorne/Advanced%20Testosterone%20Support/1822465/Label_01.PNG]:
- zinc (as zinc bisglycinate chelate) 15 mg (136% DV)
- shilajit extract 500 mg
- luteolin phytosome 150 mg (Japanese sophora, Styphnolobium japonicum, bud extract with sunflower phospholipid)
- ashwagandha extract (root, leaf) 120 mg
Other ingredients: hypromellose capsule, microcrystalline cellulose, calcium laurate, leucine.
Thorne names the shilajit PrimaVie and the ashwagandha Shoden, standardized to 35% withanolide glycosides, which is about 42 mg [https://thorne.com/mens-health; https://www.thorne.com/products/dp/advanced-testosterone-support].
Directions: 'Take 2 capsules daily or as recommended by your health-care practitioner.'
Warning: 'If pregnant, nursing, or trying to conceive, do not use this product.' [https://fullscript.com/catalog/products/advanced-testosterone-support]
Disclosure gaps:
- The amount of luteolin inside the 150 mg phytosome is not stated.
- Some retailers list a different luteolin source (Chrysanthemum leaf), so check the bottle.
- **Dose versus trials (partial):** - Shilajit, 500 mg/day: matches Pandit 2016, which gave purified shilajit (PrimaVie) 250 mg twice daily for 90 days to healthy men aged 45-55. Total testosterone, free testosterone and DHEAS rose vs placebo, total testosterone by about 20% [https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/26395129/; https://www.nutraingredients.com/Article/2015/10/06/Shilajit-supplements-may-boost-testosterone-for-healthy-men-RCT/].
- Ashwagandha, 120 mg/day Shoden (about 42 mg withanolide glycosides): Lopresti 2019 gave Shoden supplying 21 mg withanolide glycosides a day to overweight men aged 40-70. Testosterone rose 14.7% and DHEA-S 18% more than on placebo, with no between-group difference in fatigue, vigour or sexual well-being [https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC6438434]. A second 2019 Lopresti trial used 240 mg/day in stressed adults [https://core.ac.uk/download/231906895.pdf]. So 120 mg is within the studied Shoden range, but it is a different extract from the 600 mg/day sexual-function trials.
- Luteolin phytosome, 150 mg/day: no published human trial or safety study at any dose. The only human data found are unpublished manufacturer 'data on file' for other luteolin forms at 100 or 500 mg [https://www.lifeextension.com/magazine/2024/6/restore-youthful-free-testosterone].
- Zinc, 15 mg/day: a nutritional amount, below the adult upper limit of 40 mg [https://ods.od.nih.gov/factsheets/Zinc-Consumer/].
No trial of the finished formula was found.
- **Certification:** NSF Certified for Sport (drug-screening tier).
The listing was read directly from NSF's database earlier in this workflow, on 8 Nov 2026. A re-check was impossible because the site is blocked in this session.
Listing 1822465, 'Thorne Advanced Testosterone Support':
- country of sale: United States; product type: Cardiovascular Health/Sexual Wellness; gluten-free claim
- lot: 416894; 60 capsules; serving: 2 capsules
- NSF-hosted label attached [https://www.nsfsport.com/certified-products/listing-detail.php?id=1822465]
NSF's dietary listing for Thorne also shows the product [https://info.nsf.org/Certified/Dietary/Listings.asp?CompanyName=Thorne]. Thorne has 69 listings in the NSF Certified for Sport directory captured 8 Nov 2026 [https://www.nsfsport.com/certified-products/search-results.php?search=ashwagandha].
What NSF tests: each lot against sport prohibited lists, plus content [https://www.nsf.org/consumer-resources/articles/certified-for-sport-program].
PDE5 inhibitors: whether they are on NSF's panel is not published [https://www.bscg.org/bscg-banned-substance-testing-menu-v070725].
Check that the bottle carries lot 416894 or a lot NSF adds later. (certifier page: https://www.nsfsport.com/certified-products/listing-detail.php?id=1822465; verified in the certifier's database: yes; verifier marked the programme as screening for hidden drugs: yes — see the challenge notes, which found PDE5-drug screening confirmed only for BSCG)
- **Manufacturer and regulatory record:** Company: Thorne (Thorne HealthTech), a large US practitioner-channel maker. Its SEC filings carry only generic recall and enforcement risk language and say some products are made by third-party manufacturers [https://www.sec.gov/Archives/edgar/data/1844280/000095017023011358/thrn-20221231.htm].
Found, unverified: one third-party blog says Thorne once agreed to a voluntary recall after selling a prescription-drug ingredient as a supplement. It gives no date, product or FDA source [https://scienceinsights.org/is-thorne-fda-approved-or-just-fda-regulated/].
None found for this product: an 8 Nov 2026 web search for FDA warning letters or recalls (2023-2026) naming Thorne returned only SEC filings. The product is not on the first page of FDA's sexual-enhancement hidden-drug list (10 of 441 entries captured) [https://www.fda.gov/drugs/medication-health-fraud-notifications/sexual-enhancement-and-energy-product-notifications].
Not checked directly:
- FDA's warning-letter, recall and Health Fraud databases
- Health Canada
- TGA
- MHRA
These checks were impossible in this pass: regulator sites were blocked, the web-search budget was used up, and Firecrawl credits had run out until 9 Nov 2026 05:26 UTC. Re-check before buying.
- **Evidence summary:** No trial has tested this formula. Its case is borrowed from single-ingredient studies of hormone levels, not of libido or erections.
- PrimaVie shilajit raised total and free testosterone in one 90-day RCT in men aged 45-55 [https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/26395129/].
- Shoden ashwagandha raised testosterone (+14.7%) and DHEA-S (+18%) over 8 weeks in men aged 40-70, but did not beat placebo on sexual well-being or vitality [https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC6438434].
- Luteolin's testosterone and aromatase claims rest on preclinical work and unpublished company data [https://www.lifeextension.com/magazine/2024/6/restore-youthful-free-testosterone].
Expect at most a modest hormonal effect. Its fit to Performer 8's drive role is indirect.

**Safety risks**

- The ashwagandha liver-injury signal applies: LiverTox likelihood B or C, onset 2-12 weeks, usually cholestatic [https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/NBK548536/; https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/31991029/].
- Ashwagandha leaf: the extract includes leaf. In April 2026 India's Ministry of Ayush and FSSAI moved to root-only ashwagandha in Ayush products and health supplements [https://nutraingredients.com/Article/2026/04/28/india-bans-ashwagandha-leaf-use-in-any-form-industry-reacts; https://www.supplysidesj.com/supplement-regulations/ayush-bans-ashwagandha-leaf-extracts]. This does not make the US product illegal, but leaf is the plant part regulators have restricted.
- Shilajit as a raw material can carry heavy metals [https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/38393486/]. A purified branded extract and NSF lot testing reduce this risk but do not eliminate it (my inference).
- Luteolin 150 mg: no human safety data found. The aromatase claims are preclinical [https://www.lifeextension.com/magazine/2024/6/restore-youthful-free-testosterone].
- Hormone-active: shilajit and ashwagandha raise testosterone and DHEA-S [https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/26395129/; https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC6438434]. NCCIH advises avoiding ashwagandha with hormone-sensitive prostate cancer [https://www.nccih.nih.gov/health/ashwagandha].
- The label says not to use it while trying to conceive [https://fullscript.com/catalog/products/advanced-testosterone-support]. For a recently married man who may be planning children this is a direct conflict, even though ashwagandha trials in men reported better semen parameters [https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/41766918/].
- Zinc: 15 mg/day is below the 40 mg adult upper limit. Count zinc from other products, such as Performer 8's 10 mg, to stay under 40 mg [https://ods.od.nih.gov/factsheets/Zinc-Consumer/; https://www.usatoday.com/press-release/story/43980/performer-8-reviews-wolfson-brands-announces-u-s-launch-of-current-11-ingredient-mens-wellness-formula/].
- It contains ashwagandha, so do not combine it with Performer 8, Core Med KSM-66, Klean Ashwagandha or Transparent Labs KSM-66.

**Interactions**

- Ashwagandha component: thyroid hormone medicines, sedatives, anticonvulsants, immunosuppressants, diabetes drugs and blood-pressure drugs [https://www.nccih.nih.gov/health/ashwagandha].
- Zinc: quinolone and tetracycline antibiotics (take the antibiotic 2 h before or 4-6 h after zinc) and penicillamine (1 h apart). Thiazide diuretics increase zinc loss [https://ods.od.nih.gov/factsheets/Zinc-Consumer/].
- Testosterone therapy or other androgen-raising supplements: possible additive hormonal effect (my inference from [https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/26395129/]).
- Shilajit and luteolin: no human interaction studies were found, which is a gap.
- Nitrates, PDE5 inhibitors, anticoagulants and antiplatelets, SSRIs and MAOIs: no documented interaction for any component in the sources reviewed, and none studied [https://www.nccih.nih.gov/health/ashwagandha; https://ods.od.nih.gov/factsheets/Zinc-Consumer/].

**Who should avoid it**

- Men trying to conceive, per the label warning [https://fullscript.com/catalog/products/advanced-testosterone-support]
- Men with thyroid or autoimmune disease, or who are about to have surgery [https://www.nccih.nih.gov/health/ashwagandha]
- Users of sedatives, anticonvulsants or immunosuppressants [https://www.nccih.nih.gov/health/ashwagandha]
- Men with hormone-sensitive prostate cancer, or on testosterone therapy (the latter is my inference) [https://www.nccih.nih.gov/health/ashwagandha]
- Anyone with liver disease or earlier herb-induced liver injury [https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/NBK548536/]
- Anyone already taking Performer 8 or another ashwagandha product
- Residents of Denmark [https://nutraingredients.com/Article/2023/05/17/Growing-concerns-over-safety-of-ashwagandha-in-EU-member-states]

**Stop signals**

- Jaundice, dark urine, itching, nausea or right-upper abdominal pain: stop and get liver tests [https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/31991029/]
- Palpitations, tremor or heat intolerance (possible thyroid effect; my inference) [https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/41740946/]
- New acne, breast tenderness or irritability, or a rising PSA or haematocrit if tested (possible androgen effects; my inference)
- Drowsiness or GI upset [https://www.nccih.nih.gov/health/ashwagandha]
- Stop if the couple starts trying to conceive (label warning) [https://fullscript.com/catalog/products/advanced-testosterone-support]
- No benefit after 90 days, the PrimaVie trial length [https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/26395129/]

**Challenge reviews**

- *certification-label lens — confirmed; downgrade to: none*
  - Audit basis: raw page captures from this workflow (7-8 Nov 2026), not the verifier's summary; live re-opening was blocked today (network policy, spent WebSearch budget, Firecrawl out of credits until 2026-11-09 05:26 UTC).
  - Directly confirmed from the raw 8 Nov 2026 capture of https://www.nsfsport.com/certified-products/listing-detail.php?id=1822465: United States, capsule, 60, serving 2 capsules, single lot 416894. The NSF-hosted label matches the shortlist: zinc 15 mg; shilajit extract 500 mg; luteolin phytosome 150 mg (Japanese sophora bud extract with sunflower phospholipid); ashwagandha extract (root, leaf) 120 mg.
  - Only lot 416894 is certified; other lots are not covered.
  - Some retailers name Chrysanthemum leaf as the luteolin source, which points to a different label version; buy the version that matches the NSF label.
  - Retailer copies (Fullscript, Emerson Ecologics) carry 'If pregnant, nursing, or trying to conceive, do not use this product.'

- *safety-evidence lens — confirmed; downgrade to: none*
  - The conception warning should decide the matter. The label says not to use it while trying to conceive, so for a man who may be trying to conceive it is excluded, not merely a caution.
  - The thyroid case reports and the statin liver-monitoring advice (inference) apply to its ashwagandha component, and the 150 mg of luteolin has no human safety data.
  - The NSF listing matches the raw capture, but covers lot 416894 only. Buy only that lot or later lots that NSF lists.

### Report rank 15: Transparent Labs — Vitality (Natural Hormone & Testosterone Support), 60 capsules

- **Verifier verdict:** acceptable — The closest like-for-like swap for Performer 8's drive role (KSM-66 at the 600 mg trial dose, no caffeine) from a brand with an Informed Choice listing, but its tongkat and shilajit are below trial doses, its label versions conflict, it combines two liver-signal herbs, and its weaker sampling-based certification could not be re-confirmed, so a single NSF-listed KSM-66 is the cleaner choice.
- **Product as verified:** Transparent Labs — Vitality (Natural Hormone & Testosterone Support), 60 capsules
- **Product page:** https://www.transparentlabs.com/products/testosterone-booster
- **Where sold:** US: Transparent Labs, Vitamin Shoppe, Amazon, The Feed. The Informed Choice listing (seen via the search index) gives availability in Africa, Asia, Europe, North America, Oceania and South America. UK and Gulf retailers for Vitality were not confirmed. Lookalike 'Transparent Labs' storefronts on '.it.com' domains (transparentlabssupplements.it.com, transparentlabssupplementsus.it.com) appear in search results, so buy only from the brand or a major retailer.
- **Label verified:** no. Retailer and review pages seen through search summaries (The Feed, PricePlow, Vitamin Shoppe, Man of Many, Garage Gym Reviews). The brand page could not be opened.
- **Proprietary blend:** no
- **Directions and daily amounts:** Serving: 2 capsules; 30 servings per 60-capsule bottle.
Most consistent panel across sources, per 2 capsules:
- KSM-66 ashwagandha root extract 600 mg
- PrimaVie purified shilajit 250 mg
- LJ100 Eurycoma longifolia (tongkat ali) root extract 100 mg
- zinc (as L-OptiZinc, zinc L-methionine) 15 mg
- BioPerine black pepper extract 5 mg (one page says 10 mg)
Some listings also show boron 5 mg, quercetin 250 mg and DIM 125 mg, which look like other formula versions.
An older PricePlow panel said 'take 2 capsules (1 serving) 1-2 times daily with food'. At 2 servings, every amount doubles (KSM-66 1,200 mg). Current directions were not confirmed; check the bottle's panel.
- **Dose versus trials (partial):** - KSM-66 600 mg/day: matches the 300 mg twice-daily regimen of Chauhan 2022 and Khanna 2026 (8 weeks) [https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/35873404/; https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/41766918/].
- PrimaVie shilajit 250 mg/day: half the dose in Pandit 2016, which gave 250 mg twice daily for 90 days to healthy men aged 45-55. Total testosterone rose about 20%, and free testosterone and DHEAS also rose [https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/26395129/; https://www.nutraingredients.com/Article/2015/10/06/Shilajit-supplements-may-boost-testosterone-for-healthy-men-RCT/].
- LJ100 100 mg/day: the lowest dose tested. Physta 100 mg/day raised testosterone in men aged 50-70; cortisol fell only at 200 mg [https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC8254464/]. Examine's libido range is 200-400 mg/day [https://examine.com/supplements/tongkat-ali/].
- Zinc 15 mg/day: a nutritional amount, below the 40 mg/day adult upper limit [https://ods.od.nih.gov/factsheets/Zinc-Consumer/].
- BioPerine: an absorption enhancer with no outcome trial in this context.
No trial of the finished formula was found.
- **Certification:** Informed Choice (claimed; identity/purity tier in this ranking). Current status UNCONFIRMED.
- The Informed Choice listing for Vitality was seen only through a search-engine summary of the certifier's page: certified 14 Aug 2023; capsule; category Recovery Formula; goal Wellness; unflavoured batches tested Oct 2024 to 20 Oct 2025; worldwide regions [https://choice.wetestyoutrust.com/supplement-search/transparent-labs/vitality]. The page could not be opened live, so current status and any newer batches are unconfirmed.
- Informed Choice monitors retail product through regular blind testing (at least 12 lots a year), so not every batch is tested [https://sport.wetestyoutrust.com/about/sport-vs-choice]. It is therefore not treated as a hidden-drug screen here.
- Transparent Labs has voluntarily withdrawn other products from the programme, e.g. L-Citrulline [https://choice.wetestyoutrust.com/supplement-search/transparent-labs/l-citrulline].
- Not NSF Certified for Sport: absent from NSF's 2,625-listing directory captured 8 Nov 2026, although 55 other Transparent Labs products (whey, Creatine HMB, Hydrate) are listed [https://www.nsfsport.com/certified-products/search-results.php?search=ashwagandha].
- Ingredient-level credentials (KSM-66 raw material BSCG Certified Drug Free; LJ100 self-affirmed GRAS) do not certify this bottle [https://www.newhope.com/industry-insights/ksm-66-ashwagandha-now-bscg-certified-drug-free; https://www.supplysidesj.com/supplement-regulations/hp-ingredients-lj100-receives-gras-status]. (certifier page: https://choice.wetestyoutrust.com/supplement-search/transparent-labs/vitality; verified in the certifier's database: no; verifier marked the programme as screening for hidden drugs: no — see the challenge notes, which found PDE5-drug screening confirmed only for BSCG)
- **Manufacturer and regulatory record:** Company: Transparent Labs, a US sports-nutrition brand that certifies much of its range: 55 NSF Certified for Sport listings and several Informed Choice listings [https://www.nsfsport.com/certified-products/search-results.php?search=ashwagandha; https://choice.wetestyoutrust.com/supplement-search/transparent-labs/vitality].
- FDA warning letters: none naming Transparent Labs in an 8 Nov 2026 web search (the same search did surface Double Wood's 2020 letter).
- FDA hidden-drug list: no entry seen. Only the first page of the 441 notifications was captured [https://www.fda.gov/drugs/medication-health-fraud-notifications/sexual-enhancement-and-energy-product-notifications].
- Counterfeit risk: lookalike storefronts on '.it.com' domains appear in search results.
- Not checked directly: FDA recall database, Health Canada, TGA and MHRA. Regulator sites were unreachable and the search budget was used up, so re-check before buying.
- **Evidence summary:** Only the ashwagandha part is trial-dosed.
- KSM-66 600 mg/day improved sexual-function scores and modestly raised testosterone in two 8-week RCTs [https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/35873404/; https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/41766918/]; pooled data show testosterone +57 ng/dL [https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/41740946/].
- PrimaVie raised testosterone about 20%, but at twice this product's dose [https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/26395129/].
- Tongkat ali at 100 mg is at the bottom of its tested range, and it affects hormones and stress, not erections [https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC8254464/; https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/26365449/].
There is no vascular (blood-flow) ingredient, and no evidence that the combination beats KSM-66 alone.

**Safety risks**

- Two liver-signal herbs in one product: ashwagandha (LiverTox B or C; onset 2-12 weeks; cholestatic) and tongkat ali (LiverTox case reports) [https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/NBK548536/; https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/31991029/; https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/NBK609015/; https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/38646387/].
- Tongkat ali: EFSA (2021) flagged possible DNA damage in animals and could not establish its safety [https://efsa.onlinelibrary.wiley.com/doi/10.2903/j.efsa.2021.6937; https://www.opss.org/article/tongkat-ali-uses-and-safety-dietary-supplements].
- Shilajit as a raw material can carry heavy metals. PrimaVie is a purified branded extract, but no heavy-metal results for this product were seen [https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/38393486/].
- Hormone-active: ashwagandha, shilajit and tongkat raise testosterone or DHEAS; NCCIH advises avoiding ashwagandha with hormone-sensitive prostate cancer [https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/26395129/; https://www.nccih.nih.gov/health/ashwagandha].
- Thyroid: ashwagandha modestly raises T4 [https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/41740946/].
- BioPerine (piperine) is added to raise absorption. Its effect on prescription-drug levels was not verified in this pass, so ask a pharmacist if you take narrow-margin drugs (my inference).
- Versions listing DIM, quercetin or boron add ingredients whose hormonal effects were not reviewed here; confirm which formula you have.
- Zinc: count all sources to stay under 40 mg/day; Performer 8 alone adds 10 mg [https://ods.od.nih.gov/factsheets/Zinc-Consumer/; https://www.usatoday.com/press-release/story/43980/performer-8-reviews-wolfson-brands-announces-u-s-launch-of-current-11-ingredient-mens-wellness-formula/].
- Do not combine with Performer 8, a KSM-66 single or Thorne Advanced Testosterone Support (duplicate ashwagandha).

**Interactions**

- Ashwagandha: diabetes drugs, blood-pressure drugs, immunosuppressants, sedatives, anticonvulsants and thyroid hormone [https://www.nccih.nih.gov/health/ashwagandha].
- Tongkat ali: propranolol (minor) and CYP1A2/CYP2A6 substrates [https://examine.com/supplements/tongkat-ali/].
- Zinc: quinolone and tetracycline antibiotics (take the antibiotic 2 h before or 4-6 h after) and penicillamine; thiazide diuretics increase zinc loss [https://ods.od.nih.gov/factsheets/Zinc-Consumer/].
- Piperine: may raise absorption of drugs taken at the same time (not verified; my inference).
- Nitrates and PDE5 inhibitors: no documented interaction for any listed ingredient. Watch for dizziness if also on blood-pressure drugs (my inference) [https://www.nccih.nih.gov/health/ashwagandha].
- Anticoagulants and antiplatelets, SSRIs and MAOIs: no documented interaction found in the sources reviewed.
- Testosterone therapy: possible additive effect (my inference).

**Who should avoid it**

- Men with thyroid disease or on thyroid medication [https://www.nccih.nih.gov/health/ashwagandha]
- Men with autoimmune disease or on immunosuppressants [https://www.nccih.nih.gov/health/ashwagandha]
- Men with hormone-sensitive prostate cancer or on testosterone therapy [https://www.nccih.nih.gov/health/ashwagandha]
- Anyone with liver disease or a past herb-induced liver injury [https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/NBK548536/; https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/NBK609015/]
- Anyone with kidney disease (my inference from EFSA's animal findings on tongkat) [https://efsa.onlinelibrary.wiley.com/doi/10.2903/j.efsa.2021.6937]
- Anyone due for surgery, or taking sedatives or anticonvulsants [https://www.nccih.nih.gov/health/ashwagandha]
- Anyone still taking Performer 8 or another ashwagandha product

**Stop signals**

- Jaundice, dark urine, itching, nausea or upper-right abdominal pain: stop and get liver tests [https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/31991029/; https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/38646387/]
- Palpitations, tremor or heat intolerance (a possible thyroid effect; my inference) [https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/41740946/]
- Insomnia, irritability or acne (possible androgen effect; my inference)
- Excessive drowsiness or ongoing GI upset [https://www.nccih.nih.gov/health/ashwagandha]
- No benefit after 8-12 weeks [https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/35873404/]

**Challenge reviews**

- *certification-label lens — corrected; downgrade to: acceptable*
  - Audit basis: raw page captures from this workflow (7-8 Nov 2026), not the verifier's summary; live re-opening was blocked today (network policy, spent WebSearch budget, Firecrawl out of credits until 2026-11-09 05:26 UTC).
  - The Informed Choice listing was seen only via a search summary (certified 14-Aug-2023; unflavoured batches tested Oct 2024 to 20-Oct-2025); it was never opened, no 2026 batch appears in that snapshot, and Informed Choice tests retail samples (at least 12 lots a year) rather than every batch.
  - Label versions conflict (some copies add boron 5 mg, quercetin 250 mg and DIM 125 mg; BioPerine is given as 5 mg or 10 mg; an older third-party panel says 2 capsules 1-2 times daily), so it is unknown which formula the certified batches had.
  - The brand page was unreadable; lookalike '.it.com' stores use the brand name.

- *safety-evidence lens — confirmed; downgrade to: none*
  - Informed Choice tests by retail sampling, not every batch, and Vitality's status could not be re-read live; the latest batch shown is from October 2025.
  - Tongkat component: Biogen's tongkat insert contraindicates blood-sugar-lowering drugs and prostate cancer, which agrees with the diabetes caution already listed for ashwagandha.
  - The thyroid case reports and the statin liver-monitoring advice apply, with two herbs that carry liver signals.
  - Label versions conflict (some list DIM, quercetin and boron), so check the formula on the bottle in hand.

### Report rank 16: Biogen (South Africa) — Tongkat Ali 400 mg, 30 capsules (Informed Sport listing name 'Tongkat Ali Testo+')

- **Verifier verdict:** acceptable — Every batch is Informed Sport-tested (13 batches up to 19 Aug 2026) and the single 400 mg ingredient is disclosed at a dose inside the trial range, but the extract is unnamed and unstandardized ('200:1'; trials used Physta at 0.8-1.5% eurycomanone), the label was seen only through an insert excerpt, it is sold only in South Africa, and tongkat ali's evidence covers only testosterone and stress, so it is acceptable rather than recommended as a Performer 8 drive replacement.
- **Product as verified:** Biogen (South Africa) — Tongkat Ali 400 mg, 30 capsules (Informed Sport listing name 'Tongkat Ali Testo+')
- **Product page:** https://biogen.co.za/shop/tongkat-ali-400mg-30-caps/
- **Where sold:** South Africa only: biogen.co.za and Dis-Chem (https://www.dischem.co.za/biogen-tongkat-ali-30-s-108). Informed Sport lists its regional availability as 'Africa' (https://sport.wetestyoutrust.com/supplement-search/biogen/tongkat-ali-testo). No US, UK/EU or Gulf/Middle East retailer appeared in this session's captured searches.
- **Label verified:** no. Biogen package insert 412108 (https://biogen.co.za/wp-content/uploads/412108-tongkat-ali-400mg-package-insert.pdf). It was read only through a search-engine excerpt captured in this session on 8 Nov 2026, plus snippets of the Biogen and Dis-Chem product pages (https://biogen.co.za/shop/tongkat-ali-30s; https://www.dischem.co.za/biogen-tongkat-ali-30-s-108). The insert PDF, the Biogen site and the label image on the Informed Sport listing ('Biogen_Edge_Tongat-Ali_30s_Label_Black.png') could not be opened today: biogen.co.za and sport.wetestyoutrust.com are blocked from this sandbox and Firecrawl credits ran out. The full panel, the excipients and any eurycomanone figure are therefore unconfirmed.
- **Proprietary blend:** no
- **Directions and daily amounts:** From the insert excerpt: take 1 capsule daily for 4 weeks, then stop for 2 weeks, and repeat. Each capsule holds 400 mg of Eurycoma longifolia (tongkat ali) root extract, stated as '200:1'. The excerpt gives no eurycomanone percentage or other marker (https://biogen.co.za/wp-content/uploads/412108-tongkat-ali-400mg-package-insert.pdf). There are no other active ingredients: Informed Sport calls it a 'single source ingredient', and search snippets give '400mg of eurycoma longifolia jack (tongkat ali) extract per capsule' (https://sport.wetestyoutrust.com/supplement-search/biogen/tongkat-ali-testo; https://biogen.co.za/shop/tongkat-ali-30s). Daily amount at the directed dose: 400 mg of extract. The pack holds 30 capsules. A 60-capsule pack also exists (https://biogen.co.za/shop/tongkat-ali-400mg-60-caps/), but Informed Sport does not list it separately.
- **Dose versus trials (partial):** This product gives 400 mg/day of an unnamed '200:1' root extract, with no stated eurycomanone content, used in 4-week courses (https://biogen.co.za/wp-content/uploads/412108-tongkat-ali-400mg-package-insert.pdf).

What the trials used: almost all used Physta, a freeze-dried water extract standardized to 0.8-1.5% eurycomanone (Leisegang 2022, https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC9415500/). Physta is defined by four markers under Malaysian Standard MS2409:2011 (https://www.ingredientsnetwork.com/47/resourcefile/17/09/46/physta-leaflet-july-2026-share.pdf). Doses and results:
- 100 or 200 mg/day for 12 weeks in low-testosterone men aged 50-70: testosterone rose (Chinnappan 2021, https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC8254464/).
- 200 mg/day for 1 month, uncontrolled, in late-onset hypogonadism (Tambi 2012, https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/21671978/).
- 300 mg/day for 12 weeks in healthy men aged 30-55: no testosterone difference vs placebo (Ismail 2012, https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/23243445/; George 2013, per the Leisegang table).
- 400 mg/day for 5 weeks, uncontrolled, in cyclists aged 57-72 (Henkel 2014, per the Leisegang table).
- 600 mg/day for 2 weeks (Chan 2021, per the Leisegang table).
- 200 mg/day of a 'dry extract' for up to 6 months in androgen-deficient men aged 40-59 (Leitao 2021, https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/33541567/).
Examine gives 100-600 mg/day for testosterone and 200-400 mg/day for sexual health, as a standardized water-soluble extract (https://examine.com/supplements/tongkat-ali/).

Why the match is only partial:
- The milligram dose is inside the studied range and equals Henkel's 400 mg.
- The extract is neither Physta nor LJ100 and states no marker. Biogen's own site tells buyers to look for explicit eurycomanone percentages (https://biogen.co.za/shop/tongkat-ali-400mg-60-caps/, search excerpt), and a label that gives only a '200:1' ratio is flagged as a weak quality signal (https://verifiedsupplementdata.com/tongkat-ali/third-party-tested/).
- The 4-week course is shorter than the 12-week trials.
So it is not proven to be equivalent to the trial material.
- **Certification:** Informed Sport (LGC). Every batch is tested before release, using ISO 17025 methods, for more than 285 substances banned in sport and 'impurities', and products are re-tested blind from retail (https://sport.wetestyoutrust.com/about/sport-vs-choice).

This session captured the listing from the certifier's own database on 8 Nov 2026 (I could not reopen it live today):
- Status: Certified, certified 19-Feb-2024, region Africa.
- Purchase link: the 30-capsule pack.
- 13 batches: 26F127 (tested 19-Aug-2026), 26F062, 26C180, 26C062, 26B017, 25R015, 25R016, 25K066, 25H007, 25C112, 25A046, 25A045, 24P042 (tested 31-Dec-2024). The newest expiry is 30-Jun-2028.
- No withdrawal date.
Informed Sport does not publish whether sildenafil-type PDE5 inhibitors are on its panel. Check the batch number on your pack against this list. (certifier page: https://sport.wetestyoutrust.com/supplement-search/biogen/tongkat-ali-testo; verified in the certifier's database: yes; verifier marked the programme as screening for hidden drugs: yes — see the challenge notes, which found PDE5-drug screening confirmed only for BSCG)
- **Manufacturer and regulatory record:** Company: Biogen is a South African sports-nutrition brand, unrelated to the US biotech Biogen Inc. It sells through biogen.co.za and through Dis-Chem, which lists this product as 'Biogen Pro Series' (https://www.dischem.co.za/biogen-tongkat-ali-30-s-108). Dis-Chem's Living Fit site publishes Biogen product news, including 'Biogen Tongkat Ali Testo+ gets Informed Sport stamp' (https://dischemlivingfit.co.za/biogen-product-news/biogen-tongkat-ali-testo-gets-informed-sport-stamp/). The legal owner and manufacturing site were not confirmed.

Certification history: Biogen has had other Informed Sport listings, such as Premium Pre-Workout (https://sport.wetestyoutrust.com/supplement-search/biogen/premium-pre-workout). This product has 13 batches tested from Dec 2024 to Aug 2026 with no withdrawal.

Regulator status: it is an unregistered complementary medicine. The label says it 'has not been evaluated by SAHPRA for its quality, safety or intended use' (insert excerpt, https://biogen.co.za/wp-content/uploads/412108-tongkat-ali-400mg-package-insert.pdf).

Adverse record: none found, but the search was incomplete. The FDA health-fraud list pages captured earlier in this session hold only the page header and intro, not the product database, so they neither confirm nor rule out a Biogen entry (https://www.fda.gov/drugs/medication-health-fraud-notifications/sexual-enhancement-and-energy-product-notifications). Dedicated searches for 'Biogen' could not be run today in FDA warning letters and import alerts, the FDA Health Fraud database, or Health Canada, TGA, MHRA and SAHPRA recall and alert lists. The reasons: this turn's shared WebSearch budget (200 calls) is used up, Firecrawl credits are exhausted until 2026-11-09 05:26 UTC, and fda.gov, biogen.co.za and sport.wetestyoutrust.com are blocked from this sandbox. These searches should be re-run in a follow-up message.
- **Evidence summary:** The evidence covers only the drive role.
- Testosterone: Leisegang 2022 (9 studies; 5 RCTs, n=232) pooled SMD 1.35 (95% CI 0.57-2.14). Heterogeneity was 87%, the hypogonadal subgroup showed publication bias, and most trials used one industry extract (https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC9415500/). Gains were seen mainly in men with low testosterone. Two 12-week RCTs of 300 mg/day in healthy men found no difference vs placebo (same source).
- Stress: cortisol fell in the 200 mg/day arm of Chinnappan 2021 (https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC8254464/).
- Erections: the Kotirum 2015 meta-analysis (2 RCTs, 139 men) found IIEF-5 +0.91 (95% CI -1.50 to 3.33), which is not significant (https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/26365449/). Tongkat ali cannot take over Prelox's vascular role.
- Overall: Examine says the testosterone data 'aren't convincing' (https://examine.com/supplements/tongkat-ali/).
No trial used Biogen's extract. For a healthy man in his 40s with normal testosterone, the likely benefit is small and uncertain.

**Safety risks**

- Rare liver injury: LiverTox describes isolated cases of clinically apparent liver injury, reported in body builders (https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/NBK609015/). The index case was a 47-year-old man with nausea, vomiting, jaundice and raised liver enzymes after starting tongkat ali, who improved after stopping (Kaliounji 2024, https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/38646387/; https://examine.com/supplements/tongkat-ali/).
- EFSA (2021) could not establish that tongkat ali root extract is safe as a novel food. Animal tests showed possible DNA damage, and a chronic toxicity study showed liver and kidney effects (https://efsa.onlinelibrary.wiley.com/doi/10.2903/j.efsa.2021.6937; https://www.opss.org/article/tongkat-ali-uses-and-safety-dietary-supplements).
- Contamination across the category: tongkat products have been found with lead and mercury and with undisclosed sildenafil analogues (https://examine.com/supplements/tongkat-ali/; https://www.opss.org/article/tongkat-ali-uses-and-safety-dietary-supplements). Informed Sport's every-batch testing lowers this risk for listed batches. However, no heavy-metal results, eurycomanone assay or PDE5-inhibitor coverage are published for this product (https://sport.wetestyoutrust.com/about/sport-vs-choice).
- Hormonal: Biogen's insert contraindicates use with prostate cancer and warns of possible prostate problems in elderly men (https://biogen.co.za/wp-content/uploads/412108-tongkat-ali-400mg-package-insert.pdf).
- Blood sugar: the insert contraindicates use with hypoglycaemic (glucose-lowering) drugs (https://biogen.co.za/wp-content/uploads/412108-tongkat-ali-400mg-package-insert.pdf).
- Mild side effects in trials: stomach and gut symptoms (abdominal pain, indigestion, constipation) and itching (https://examine.com/supplements/tongkat-ali/; https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC9415500/).
- Long-term use is untested: trials lasted 2 weeks to 6 months (https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC9415500/). OPSS says its safety in humans 'hasn't been fully established' (https://www.opss.org/article/tongkat-ali-uses-and-safety-dietary-supplements).
- No regulator has reviewed it: it is an unregistered complementary medicine 'not evaluated by SAHPRA for its quality, safety or intended use' (insert excerpt, https://biogen.co.za/wp-content/uploads/412108-tongkat-ali-400mg-package-insert.pdf).
- The label was only partly verified: the full insert and the label image could not be opened here, so the excipients and the extract's standardization are unconfirmed.
- Anti-doping: tongkat ali is not on the WADA list but could fall under its 'similar biological effect' clause (https://examine.com/supplements/tongkat-ali/). This matters only for drug-tested athletes.

**Interactions**

- Diabetes medicines (insulin, sulfonylureas and other glucose-lowering drugs): the Biogen insert contraindicates them because of added risk of low blood sugar (https://biogen.co.za/wp-content/uploads/412108-tongkat-ali-400mg-package-insert.pdf).
- Propranolol: tongkat ali may lower propranolol blood levels. Examine rates this as possible and minor, because blood pressure and heart rate did not change (https://examine.com/supplements/tongkat-ali/).
- Drugs broken down by the liver enzymes CYP1A2 or CYP2A6: Examine lists these as potential interactions; the details are not public (https://examine.com/supplements/tongkat-ali/).
- Hormone therapy for prostate cancer, and other hormone treatments: avoid. The insert contraindicates prostate cancer, and the product is meant to raise testosterone (https://biogen.co.za/wp-content/uploads/412108-tongkat-ali-400mg-package-insert.pdf; https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC9415500/).
- Nitrates and PDE5 inhibitors (sildenafil, tadalafil): no direct interaction is documented for tongkat ali itself. The danger is undeclared sildenafil analogues reported in some tongkat products (https://www.opss.org/article/tongkat-ali-uses-and-safety-dietary-supplements). With nitrates, such drugs 'may lower blood pressure to dangerous levels' (https://www.fda.gov/drugs/medication-health-fraud/public-notification-extenze-nutritional-supplement-contains-hidden-drug-ingredient). Use only packs whose batch number is on the Informed Sport list.
- Other blood-pressure drugs, anticoagulants and antiplatelets, thyroid drugs, SSRIs and MAOIs, and sedatives: no interactions are documented in Examine, LiverTox or OPSS (https://examine.com/supplements/tongkat-ali/; https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/NBK609015/). The data are thin, so tell your prescriber you take it.
- Other supplements that can affect the liver, notably KSM-66 ashwagandha in a Performer 8-style drive stack: both carry liver-injury signals (https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/NBK548536/; https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/NBK609015/). Start them weeks apart so a reaction can be traced to one of them.

**Who should avoid it**

- Men with prostate cancer or other hormone-sensitive conditions, and older men with prostate problems (https://biogen.co.za/wp-content/uploads/412108-tongkat-ali-400mg-package-insert.pdf).
- Anyone taking insulin or other glucose-lowering drugs (same insert).
- People with liver disease or a past drug- or herb-induced liver injury (https://examine.com/supplements/tongkat-ali/; https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/NBK609015/).
- People with kidney disease, as a precaution, given EFSA's kidney findings in chronic toxicity testing (https://efsa.onlinelibrary.wiley.com/doi/10.2903/j.efsa.2021.6937).
- Pregnant or breastfeeding women (https://examine.com/supplements/tongkat-ali/).
- Drug-tested athletes, unless the pack's batch number is on the Informed Sport listing (https://sport.wetestyoutrust.com/supplement-search/biogen/tongkat-ali-testo).
- Men who want better erections (Prelox's vascular role): tongkat ali did not significantly improve IIEF-5 scores (https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/26365449/).

**Stop signals**

- Yellow skin or eyes, dark urine, pale stools, persistent itching, nausea or vomiting, pain under the right ribs, or unusual tiredness: stop and get liver tests (https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/NBK609015/; https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/38646387/).
- Shakiness, sweating, confusion or other signs of low blood sugar, especially if you take diabetes drugs (https://biogen.co.za/wp-content/uploads/412108-tongkat-ali-400mg-package-insert.pdf).
- New urinary symptoms (weak stream, needing to go more often or at night) or prostate discomfort (the same insert warns about prostate problems).
- Persistent stomach upset or an itchy rash (https://examine.com/supplements/tongkat-ali/).
- Dizziness or fainting, chest pain, vision changes or a sudden drop in blood pressure. Tongkat ali should not cause these, and they could mean an undeclared PDE5-type drug: stop and get medical care (https://www.opss.org/article/tongkat-ali-uses-and-safety-dietary-supplements).
- The pack's batch number is not on the Informed Sport batch list: do not start that pack (https://sport.wetestyoutrust.com/supplement-search/biogen/tongkat-ali-testo).
- No clear change in libido or stress after two label courses (8 weeks of use; the trials ran 4-12 weeks): stop (https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC9415500/).

- https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/39527684/
- https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/38646387/
- https://efsa.onlinelibrary.wiley.com/doi/10.2903/j.efsa.2021.6937
- https://www.opss.org/article/tongkat-ali-uses-and-safety-dietary-supplements
- https://verifiedsupplementdata.com/tongkat-ali/third-party-tested/
- https://www.fda.gov/drugs/medication-health-fraud/public-notification-extenze-nutritional-supplement-contains-hidden-drug-ingredient

### Avoid list: NOW Foods — TestoJack 200 Extra Strength, 60 or 120 veg capsules

- **Verifier verdict:** avoid — It stacks four hormone-active herbs (tongkat ali, maca, Epimedium, Tribulus) with liver, kidney, heart and mood case reports, and it has an unconfirmed Tribulus amount, an unnamed tongkat extract, no human trial for Epimedium and no certification, so single-ingredient certified options are safer and better evidenced.
- **Product as verified:** NOW Foods — TestoJack 200 Extra Strength, 60 or 120 veg capsules
- **Product page:** https://nowfoods.com/products/supplements/testojack-200-veg-capsules
- **Where sold:** US: NOW, Wegmans, Target, Walmart, Vitacost, Swanson and Fullscript. Saudi Arabia: Nahdi pharmacy (120 capsules). New Zealand: NetPharmacy. NOW's regular-strength TestoJack 100 is sold in Lithuania (muscleshop.lt). UK and other Gulf states: not confirmed.
- **Label verified:** no. Retailer listings seen through search summaries (NetPharmacy NZ, Hello Pharmacist's label-database page, Same Day Supplements, Nahdi). NOW's own page could not be opened.
- **Proprietary blend:** no
- **Directions and daily amounts:** Serving: 2 veg capsules. Directions: '2 capsules 1 to 3 times daily with food, as needed, or as recommended by your healthcare practitioner.'
Per 2 capsules:
- tongkat ali (Eurycoma longifolia) 200 mg, four times the 50 mg in regular TestoJack. No captured listing names the extract or its ratio.
- organic maca root 6:1 concentrate 500 mg
- Epimedium extract complex (horny goat weed, aerial parts, min. 3% icariin) 300 mg, i.e. at least 9 mg icariin. One label-database page says 250 mg.
- Tribulus terrestris: listed as an ingredient, but no amount was found in any source.
Other ingredients: hypromellose, maltodextrin, ascorbyl palmitate, stearic acid, silicon dioxide.
Daily range at 1-3 servings: tongkat 200-600 mg; maca concentrate 0.5-1.5 g; Epimedium 300-900 mg.
Label cautions: adults only; not for people with serious medical conditions; consult a physician if taking medication.
No proprietary blend was seen, but check the bottle for the Tribulus amount.
- **Dose versus trials (partial):** At 2 capsules a day:
- Tongkat ali 200 mg: within the tested 100-600 mg/day range (Tambi 2012, 200 mg/day; Chinnappan 2021, 100-200 mg/day) and Examine's 200-400 mg libido range [https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/21671978/; https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC8254464/; https://examine.com/supplements/tongkat-ali/]. Those trials used a standardized water extract (Physta), and this label names no extract [https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC9415500/].
- Maca: 500 mg of 6:1 concentrate is about 3 g root equivalent if the ratio holds. Trials used 1.5-3 g/day gelatinized maca for 12 weeks (desire up from week 8; Gonzales 2002) and 2.4 g/day dry extract in mild ED (IIEF-5 +1.6 vs +0.5; Zenico 2009) [https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/12472620/; https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/19260845/]. Treating a concentrate as equal to powder is an assumption.
- Epimedium 300 mg (at least 9 mg icariin): no human ED trial; the evidence is in vitro and in animals [https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/18778098/; https://www.mayoclinic.org/diseases-conditions/erectile-dysfunction/in-depth/erectile-dysfunction-herbs/art-20044394].
- Tribulus: amount unknown. The positive Kamenov 2017 trial used 1,500 mg/day Tribestan and the negative Santos 2014 trial 800 mg/day [https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/28364864/; https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/24630840/].
- **Certification:** None.
- NSF Certified for Sport: no NOW product among the 2,625 listings captured 8 Nov 2026 [https://www.nsfsport.com/certified-products/search-results.php?search=ashwagandha].
- Informed Sport: not in the 'tongkat', 'maca' or 'testosterone' search results, and the 'tribulus' search returned nothing (7-8 Nov 2026) [https://sport.wetestyoutrust.com/supplement-search?search=tongkat; https://sport.wetestyoutrust.com/supplement-search?search=maca; https://sport.wetestyoutrust.com/supplement-search?search=testosterone; https://sport.wetestyoutrust.com/supplement-search?search=tribulus]. NOW's Informed Sport listings are NOW Sports products such as Arginine & Citrulline, not this NOW Foods formula.
- Informed Choice: its 'tongkat' search lists only VH Nutrition [https://choice.wetestyoutrust.com/supplement-search?search=tongkat].
- BSCG, USP and ConsumerLab: not searched for this product in this pass. (certifier page: none; verified in the certifier's database: no; verifier marked the programme as screening for hidden drugs: no — see the challenge notes, which found PDE5-drug screening confirmed only for BSCG)
- **Manufacturer and regulatory record:** Company: NOW Foods (NOW Health Group), a large US manufacturer. Records found:
- FDA warning letter (Oct 2004) over possible contamination of one American ginseng lot with the pesticide quintozene; NOW said it had already begun testing its ginseng lots [https://www.supplysidesj.com/supplement-regulations/now-responds-to-fda-warning-letter; https://www.nutraingredients.com/Article/2004/11/18/NOW-Foods-makes-ginseng-changes-after-FDA-letter/].
- 2016 voluntary recall of six supplements (about 165 units) for mislabeling after a label supplier's printing error [https://www.Drugs.com/fda/now-health-group-dietary-supplements-recall-mislabeling-13846.html; https://community.kidswithfoodallergies.org/blog/soy-allergy-alert-now-health-group-dietary-supplements].
- A phosphatidyl serine product was recalled for not meeting its label claim (date not captured) [https://wholefoodsmagazine.com/keywords/12602-recall].
- 2025 food recalls, not supplements: nutritional yeast (Class III, terminated) and raw cashews (Class II) [https://recallcheck.net/brand/now-foods].
- None found: no FDA warning letter or supplement recall from 2024-2026 in an 8 Nov 2026 web search, and no hidden-drug notification for NOW or TestoJack (only the first page of FDA's 441 sexual-enhancement notifications was captured) [https://www.fda.gov/drugs/medication-health-fraud-notifications/sexual-enhancement-and-energy-product-notifications].
- Not checked directly: Health Canada, TGA and MHRA. Regulator sites were unreachable and the search budget was used up.
- **Evidence summary:** A drive-only, four-herb formula; no trial of the finished product was found.
- Tongkat ali modestly raises testosterone in trials of a standardized extract and did not improve erections in pooled RCTs [https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC9415500/; https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/26365449/].
- Maca: desire improved in healthy men with no hormone change, and a small IIEF-5 gain in mild ED [https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/12472620/; https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/12525260/; https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/19260845/].
- Tribulus results conflict: positive at 1,500 mg/day Tribestan, negative at 800 mg/day; a 2026 meta-analysis found IIEF-5 +3.23 but no testosterone change [https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/28364864/; https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/24630840/; https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/40360723/].
- Epimedium has no human ED trial [https://www.mayoclinic.org/diseases-conditions/erectile-dysfunction/in-depth/erectile-dysfunction-herbs/art-20044394].
There is no vascular ingredient.

**Safety risks**

- Tongkat ali: liver-injury case reports; EFSA (2021) could not establish its safety and flagged possible DNA damage in animals [https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/NBK609015/; https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/38646387/; https://efsa.onlinelibrary.wiley.com/doi/10.2903/j.efsa.2021.6937].
- Tribulus: liver and kidney injury case reports, e.g. a 46-year-old man after 2 months of daily use, and acute kidney injury needing dialysis; LiverTox says typical doses have not been convincingly linked to liver injury [https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/38328764/; https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42234880/; https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/NBK583201/].
- Epimedium: tachyarrhythmia and hypomania in a man with heart failure after 2 weeks of use, and a vasculitic rash linked to ginkgo and/or horny goat weed. MSKCC lists cardiovascular disease as a contraindication and advises avoidance with hormone-sensitive cancers [https://www.mskcc.org/cancer-care/integrative-medicine/herbs/epimedium].
- Maca: generally safe, but a manic episode in a 27-year-old man has been reported, and maca may interfere with testosterone immunoassays [https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC10910417/; https://www.mskcc.org/cancer-care/integrative-medicine/herbs/maca].
- No drug-screening certification in a category where tongkat products have been spiked with sildenafil analogues [https://www.opss.org/article/tongkat-ali-uses-and-safety-dietary-supplements; https://examine.com/supplements/tongkat-ali/]. NOW is a mainstream maker with no hidden-drug history found, which lowers but does not remove this risk (my inference).
- The label allows up to 3 servings a day, which triples every herb beyond any studied dose of this combination (my inference).
- No stimulant or hormone is listed.

**Interactions**

- Epimedium: prolonged use may induce CYP3A4 and may reduce the effect of aromatase inhibitors (clinical relevance unknown) [https://www.mskcc.org/cancer-care/integrative-medicine/herbs/epimedium]. Sildenafil and tadalafil are CYP3A4 drugs, so their levels could fall (my inference).
- Tongkat ali: propranolol (minor) and CYP1A2/CYP2A6 substrates [https://examine.com/supplements/tongkat-ali/].
- Nitrates: no documented direct interaction, but men on nitrates should avoid unscreened sexual-health blends because of hidden-drug risk [https://www.opss.org/article/tongkat-ali-uses-and-safety-dietary-supplements].
- Antihypertensives, anticoagulants and antiplatelets, diabetes drugs, thyroid drugs, SSRIs and MAOIs, and sedatives: no human interaction data found for these herbs in the sources reviewed. The label says to consult a physician if taking medication [https://www.netpharmacy.co.nz/products/now-foods-testojack-200].

**Who should avoid it**

- Men with heart disease or arrhythmia (Epimedium) [https://www.mskcc.org/cancer-care/integrative-medicine/herbs/epimedium]
- Men with hormone-sensitive cancers, or on aromatase inhibitors [https://www.mskcc.org/cancer-care/integrative-medicine/herbs/epimedium; https://www.mskcc.org/cancer-care/integrative-medicine/herbs/maca]
- Men with liver or kidney disease [https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/NBK609015/; https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/38328764/]
- Men with bipolar disorder or past mania (maca and Epimedium case reports) [https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC10910417/; https://www.mskcc.org/cancer-care/integrative-medicine/herbs/epimedium]
- Men taking nitrates [https://www.opss.org/article/tongkat-ali-uses-and-safety-dietary-supplements]
- Men on CYP3A4-metabolized drugs, including PDE5 inhibitors, unless a pharmacist agrees (my inference) [https://www.mskcc.org/cancer-care/integrative-medicine/herbs/epimedium]
- Drug-tested athletes [https://examine.com/supplements/tongkat-ali/]

**Stop signals**

- Palpitations, chest pain, shortness of breath or fainting: stop and seek care [https://www.mskcc.org/cancer-care/integrative-medicine/herbs/epimedium]
- Racing thoughts, agitation, reduced need for sleep or other mood change [https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC10910417/; https://www.mskcc.org/cancer-care/integrative-medicine/herbs/epimedium]
- Jaundice, dark urine, itching, nausea, upper-right abdominal pain or reduced urine output: stop and get liver and kidney tests [https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/38646387/; https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/38328764/]
- Flushing, headache or visual changes soon after a dose, which can suggest a hidden PDE5 drug (my inference) [https://www.fda.gov/drugs/medication-health-fraud-notifications/sexual-enhancement-and-energy-product-notifications]
- Rash, sweating or feeling hot [https://www.mskcc.org/cancer-care/integrative-medicine/herbs/epimedium]

### Avoid list: NOW Foods (NOW Health Group, Bloomingdale, Illinois) — Men's Virility Power, 60 or 120 veg capsules

- **Verifier verdict:** avoid — Every ingredient with human data is at 3-13% of the studied dose (ginkgo at a fraction of a dose that already failed), the main herb Epimedium has no human trial but has cardiac, muscle and liver case reports, and the product is not certified, so it cannot realistically replace Performer 8's drive role.
- **Product as verified:** NOW Foods (NOW Health Group, Bloomingdale, Illinois) — Men's Virility Power, 60 or 120 veg capsules
- **Product page:** https://www.nowfoods.com/supplements/mens-virility-power-capsules
- **Where sold:** US: NOW, Vitacost, iHerb [https://www.vitacost.com/products/now-foods-men-s-virility-power-120-veg-capsules-700; https://www.iherb.com/pr/Now-Foods-Men-s-Virility-Power-120-Veg-Capsules/700]. Gulf: Nahdi, Saudi Arabia [https://www.nahdionline.com/en-sa/now-foods-mens-virility-power-120-veg-capsules/pdp/102814718]. Also New Zealand and Bangladesh [https://www.netpharmacy.co.nz/products/now-foods-mens-virility-power; https://epharma.com.bd/en/sexual-wellbeing/men-performance-enhancers/now-foods-mens-virility-power-60-veg-capsules-usa-made-143982]. UK/EU: not verified.
- **Label verified:** no. Search-engine summaries (8 Nov 2026) of NOW's product page and of retailer and label-database copies [https://www.nowfoods.com/supplements/mens-virility-power-capsules; https://ingredientlist.com/products/mens-virility-power-60-veg-capsules; https://hellopharmacist.com/drug-supplement-interactions/supplements/men-s-virility-power-by-now-113701; https://www.nahdionline.com/en-sa/now-foods-mens-virility-power-120-veg-capsules/pdp/102814718]. No panel was opened directly: www.nowfoods.com and the NIH DSLD API (api.ods.od.nih.gov) are outside this session's allowed network and have to be allowed in the session's network settings, and the shared web-search budget for this run was used up.
- **Proprietary blend:** no
- **Directions and daily amounts:** Serving size 1 veg capsule; 60 servings per 60-count bottle (NOW label, via search summary) [https://www.nowfoods.com/supplements/mens-virility-power-capsules].
Per capsule:
- Epimedium extract, 3% icariin (about 11 mg icariin, calculated): 375 mg (one retailer lists 425 mg, possibly an older label)
- Maca root: 100 mg
- Muira puama 10:1 extract: 100 mg
- Tribulus extract: 75 mg
- Ginkgo extract (24% flavonglycosides): 30 mg (one source only)
[https://ingredientlist.com/products/mens-virility-power-60-veg-capsules; https://hellopharmacist.com/drug-supplement-interactions/supplements/men-s-virility-power-by-now-113701]
One label database lists Epimedium, ginkgo and cayenne instead, which suggests an older formula.
The suggested-use text (capsules per day) was not captured; at 1 capsule a day the amounts above are the daily amounts.
Every ingredient is itemised. No stimulant or hormone is listed.
- **Dose versus trials (underdosed):** - Maca: 100 mg vs 1,500-3,000 mg/day for desire (Gonzales 2002) and 2,400 mg/day in mild ED (Zenico 2009), i.e. 3-7% of the studied dose [https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/12472620/; https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/19260845/].
- Tribulus: 75 mg vs 1,500 mg/day of Tribestan in the positive Kamenov 2017 RCT (5%); 800 mg/day was negative in Santos 2014 [https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/28364864/; https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/24630840/].
- Ginkgo: 30 mg vs 240 mg/day, a dose that did no better than placebo (Wheatley 2004; Kang 2002 also negative) [https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/15378664/; https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/12404672/].
- Epimedium (about 11 mg icariin): no human ED or libido trial. The evidence is in-vitro PDE5 inhibition by icariin derivatives [https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/18778098/]; Mayo: not enough evidence that it helps ED [https://www.mayoclinic.org/diseases-conditions/erectile-dysfunction/in-depth/erectile-dysfunction-herbs/art-20044394].
- Muira puama: no controlled trial in men was found; the human data come from combination products, e.g. a muira puama-ginkgo formula in women [https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/11186145/].
Reaching the maca or Tribulus trial doses would take 15-30 capsules a day and multiply the Epimedium and ginkgo intake, so there is no realistic way to reach them (inference).
- **Certification:** None.
- NSF Certified for Sport: no NOW product appears in NSF's full certified-product list, read 8 Nov 2026 [https://www.nsfsport.com/certified-products/search-results.php?search=ashwagandha].
- Informed Sport: text searches for 'libido' and 'tribulus' returned no products on 8 Nov 2026; the product name itself was not searched [https://sport.wetestyoutrust.com/supplement-search?search=libido; https://sport.wetestyoutrust.com/supplement-search?search=tribulus].
- NOW does certify some sports products with Informed Sport, e.g. L-Arginine 1000 mg tablets (certified 26-Jan-2023), so the gap is product-specific [https://sport.wetestyoutrust.com/supplement-search/now-foods/l-arginine-1000mg-tabs]. (certifier page: none; verified in the certifier's database: no; verifier marked the programme as screening for hidden drugs: no — see the challenge notes, which found PDE5-drug screening confirmed only for BSCG)
- **Manufacturer and regulatory record:** Company: NOW Foods, Bloomingdale, Illinois [https://www.mdpi.com/2673-4389/4/1/8], a large mainstream US maker.
FDA history found (none involves this product):
- Oct 2005: FDA warning letter over possible quintozene (pesticide) contamination of one American ginseng lot [https://www.supplysidesj.com/supplement-regulations/now-responds-to-fda-warning-letter]
- 2016: voluntary recall of six supplements (about 165 units) after a label-printing error [https://www.Drugs.com/fda/now-health-group-dietary-supplements-recall-mislabeling-13846.html; https://community.kidswithfoodallergies.org/blog/soy-allergy-alert-now-health-group-dietary-supplements]
- a phosphatidylserine recall for not meeting its label claim [https://wholefoodsmagazine.com/keywords/12602-recall]
- 2025: food recalls (nutritional yeast, cashews) listed by recall aggregators [https://recallcheck.net/brand/now-foods]
No FDA hidden-drug notice, warning letter or recall naming Men's Virility Power was found.
Searched: one web search on 8 Nov 2026 for NOW recalls and warning letters, 2024-2026.
Not searched directly: FDA's warning-letter, recall and Health Fraud databases, Health Canada, TGA and MHRA. Those sites are blocked in this session and the shared web-search budget was used up; a follow-up run is needed to close this gap.
- **Evidence summary:** Essentially an Epimedium formula. Epimedium/icariin has no human ED or libido trial, only in-vitro data [https://www.mskcc.org/cancer-care/integrative-medicine/herbs/epimedium; https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/18778098/]. The ingredients that do have human data (maca, Tribulus) are at 3-7% of studied doses, and ginkgo failed even at 240 mg/day [https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/15378664/]. The formula itself has never been trialled. Expect no measurable effect on drive, testosterone or erections.

**Safety risks**

- Epimedium case reports: tachyarrhythmia and hypomania after two weeks (Partin & Pushkin 2004) [https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/15546831/]; severe muscle spasms with raised CK and creatinine (2025) [https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC12182873/]; acute hepatotoxicity (2025) [https://smrj.scholasticahq.com/article/144607-herb-induced-liver-injury-a-case-of-acute-hepatotoxicity-associated-with-horny-goat-weed-use]; new-onset psychosis in a man with Parkinson's disease (2026) [https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/40916762/]. LiverTox likelihood score E [https://www.mdpi.com/2673-4389/4/1/8].
- Mayo: horny goat weed can cause dizziness, dry mouth, nosebleed, thirst and vomiting and 'may affect heart or breathing functions' [https://www.mayoclinic.org/diseases-conditions/erectile-dysfunction/in-depth/erectile-dysfunction-herbs/art-20044394].
- MSKCC: icariin promotes estrogen biosynthesis and aromatase expression in vitro [https://www.mskcc.org/cancer-care/integrative-medicine/herbs/epimedium].
- Tribulus: rare severe liver and kidney injury (46-year-old man after 2 months of daily use) [https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/38328764/]; rhabdomyolysis and transaminitis 2 weeks after starting 500 mg/day in a man on atorvastatin [https://amjcaserep.com/abstract/full/idArt/943492]. LiverTox likelihood score E [https://www.mdpi.com/2673-4389/4/1/8].
- Ginkgo: rarely raises the risk of bleeding and irregular heartbeat [https://www.mayoclinic.org/diseases-conditions/erectile-dysfunction/in-depth/erectile-dysfunction-herbs/art-20044394].
- Maca: usually well tolerated (transient GI upset, headache, irritability); one manic-episode case report; it may interfere with testosterone immunoassays [https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC10910417/; https://www.mskcc.org/cancer-care/integrative-medicine/herbs/maca].
- Not batch-screened for hidden drugs (uncertified), although no hidden-drug history was found for NOW.

**Interactions**

- PDE5 inhibitors (sildenafil, tadalafil) and nitrates: icariin shows mild PDE5 inhibition in vitro, so an additive blood-pressure effect is theoretical; no clinical data (inference) [https://www.mskcc.org/cancer-care/integrative-medicine/herbs/epimedium; https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/18778098/]. Nitrates must never be combined with PDE5 drugs.
- Anticoagulants and antiplatelets (warfarin, DOACs, aspirin, clopidogrel): ginkgo bleeding risk [https://www.mayoclinic.org/diseases-conditions/erectile-dysfunction/in-depth/erectile-dysfunction-herbs/art-20044394].
- Statins: Tribulus-associated rhabdomyolysis in a man on atorvastatin [https://amjcaserep.com/abstract/full/idArt/943492].
- CYP3A4-metabolised drugs: maca raised mianserin levels in one case, possibly via CYP3A4 [https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC10910417/]; sildenafil and tadalafil are CYP3A4 substrates (background pharmacology, not verified this session).
- SSRIs, MAOIs and mood stabilisers: Epimedium is among herbs linked to hypomania or mania; no direct interaction study [https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC6043668/; https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/15546831/].
- Antihypertensives: no documented interaction; Mayo notes possible heart effects, so monitor for dizziness (inference) [https://www.mayoclinic.org/diseases-conditions/erectile-dysfunction/in-depth/erectile-dysfunction-herbs/art-20044394].
- Diabetes drugs, thyroid drugs and sedatives: no interaction documented in the sources reviewed.

**Who should avoid it**

- Men with heart-rhythm problems or cardiovascular disease [https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/15546831/; https://www.mayoclinic.org/diseases-conditions/erectile-dysfunction/in-depth/erectile-dysfunction-herbs/art-20044394]
- Men on anticoagulants or antiplatelets, or due for surgery (ginkgo) [https://www.mayoclinic.org/diseases-conditions/erectile-dysfunction/in-depth/erectile-dysfunction-herbs/art-20044394]
- Men with bipolar disorder or Parkinson's disease [https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC6043668/; https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/40916762/]
- Men with liver or kidney disease [https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/38328764/; https://smrj.scholasticahq.com/article/144607-herb-induced-liver-injury-a-case-of-acute-hepatotoxicity-associated-with-horny-goat-weed-use]
- Men taking statins [https://amjcaserep.com/abstract/full/idArt/943492]
- Men with a hormone-sensitive cancer [https://www.mskcc.org/cancer-care/integrative-medicine/herbs/epimedium; https://www.mskcc.org/cancer-care/integrative-medicine/herbs/maca]

**Stop signals**

- Palpitations, a racing or irregular heartbeat, chest discomfort or breathlessness [https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/15546831/; https://www.mayoclinic.org/diseases-conditions/erectile-dysfunction/in-depth/erectile-dysfunction-herbs/art-20044394]
- Agitation, insomnia, elevated mood or unusual thoughts [https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/15546831/; https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/40916762/]
- Muscle cramps or spasms, weakness or dark urine [https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC12182873/; https://amjcaserep.com/abstract/full/idArt/943492]
- Jaundice, itching or right-upper abdominal pain [https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/38328764/; https://smrj.scholasticahq.com/article/144607-herb-induced-liver-injury-a-case-of-acute-hepatotoxicity-associated-with-horny-goat-weed-use]
- Unusual bleeding, bruising or nosebleeds [https://www.mayoclinic.org/diseases-conditions/erectile-dysfunction/in-depth/erectile-dysfunction-herbs/art-20044394]
