### Avoid list: Designs for Health — LibidoStim-M, 60 vegetable capsules

- **Verifier verdict:** avoid — Tribulus, tongkat ali and ginkgo are at 20-50% of studied doses, it adds unstudied hormone-targeting DIM and chrysin plus three herbs with liver or cardiac case reports, the label differs by market, and nothing is certified.
- **Product as verified:** Designs for Health — LibidoStim-M, 60 vegetable capsules
- **Product page:** https://www.designsforhealth.com/products/libidostim-m
- **Where sold:** US and Canada through practitioner channels: Fullscript, Supplement First, Emerson Ecologics, Professional Supplement Center [https://fullscript.com/catalog/products/libidostim-m-60-vcaps; https://supplementfirst.com/products/designs-for-health-libidostim-m; https://ftp.emersonecologics.com/products/detail/Designs-for-Health/libidostim-m-60-vcaps/U3ByZWU6OlByb2R1Y3QtNzI0NTk=/LSM060; https://www.professionalsupplementcenter.com/libidostim-m-by-designs-for-health]. Canada has a different label (Fullscript Canada, Coal Harbour Pharmacy) [https://ca.fullscript.com/catalog/products/libidostim-m-60-capsules-canada/U3ByZWU6OlZhcmlhbnQtMTAyNTgwMjQwNg==; https://coalharbourpharmacy.com/collections/intimate-wellbeing/products/libidostim-m]. New Zealand: NetPharmacy [https://netpharmacy.co.nz/products/designs-for-health-libidostim-m]. UK/EU and Gulf: not verified.
- **Label verified:** no. Retailer transcriptions seen only through search-engine summaries on 8 Nov 2026: Supplement First (US) [https://supplementfirst.com/products/designs-for-health-libidostim-m], Vitabase [https://vitabase.com/product/libidostim-m], Sunridge Medical [https://sunridgemedical.com/vitamin-store/products/designs-for-health-libido-stim-m/] and Fullscript US/Canada. The brand's own page and patient-education PDF appeared in results but were not opened [https://www.designsforhealth.com/products/libidostim-m; https://designsforhealth.com/api/library-assets/patient-education---libidostim-m]. Three versions conflict.
- **Proprietary blend:** no
- **Directions and daily amounts:** Directions: 2 capsules per day, or as directed by a health-care practitioner [https://www.designsforhealth.com/products/libidostim-m].
US label, per 2 capsules (= daily dose) [https://supplementfirst.com/products/designs-for-health-libidostim-m]:
- Tribulus terrestris aerial extract (40% saponins): 300 mg
- Chrysin: 200 mg
- Tongkat ali (LJ100): 100 mg
- DIM (diindolylmethane): 100 mg
- Horny goat weed (Epimedium): 100 mg
- Ginkgo biloba leaf extract (24% flavonglycosides, 5.4-6% terpene lactones): 100 mg
- Boron (as Bororganic glycine): 6 mg
Canadian label: DIM 50 mg, chrysin (Passiflora caerulea) 100 mcg, Epimedium sagittatum (20% icariin) 50 mg, tongkat ali (1% eurycomanone) 50 mg [https://ca.fullscript.com/catalog/products/libidostim-m-60-capsules-canada/U3ByZWU6OlZhcmlhbnQtMTAyNTgwMjQwNg==].
A third listing shows chrysin 100 mg, Tribulus 170 mg and boron 3 mg [https://sunridgemedical.com/vitamin-store/products/designs-for-health-libido-stim-m/].
Other ingredients: cellulose capsule, silicon dioxide, vegetable stearate (one US listing adds sunflower lecithin).
All amounts are itemised (no proprietary blend), but the formula differs by market and date, so read the bottle.
- **Dose versus trials (underdosed):** - Tribulus: 300 mg (about 120 mg saponins, calculated) vs 1,500 mg/day of Tribestan (about 675 mg furostanol saponins) in the positive Kamenov 2017 RCT, i.e. 20% by extract weight; 800 mg/day was negative in Santos 2014 [https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/28364864/; https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/24630840/]. The pooled Tribulus benefit is small (IIEF-5 +3.2) and leans heavily on that trial (project inference) [https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/40360723/].
- Tongkat ali (LJ100): 100 mg vs 200-400 mg/day for libido (Examine) and 100-600 mg/day of the Physta extract in testosterone trials, i.e. 25-50% of the libido range [https://examine.com/supplements/tongkat-ali/; https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC9415500/]. The ED meta-analysis was null [https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/26365449/].
- Ginkgo: 100 mg vs 240 mg/day, a dose that did no better than placebo [https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/15378664/; https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/12404672/].
- Epimedium: 100 mg; no human trial [https://www.mayoclinic.org/diseases-conditions/erectile-dysfunction/in-depth/erectile-dysfunction-herbs/art-20044394; https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/18778098/].
- Chrysin 200 mg, DIM 100 mg, boron 6 mg: no male libido or ED trial in the sources reviewed; not researched this session.
Reaching the Tribulus or LJ100 trial doses would take 4-10 capsules a day, which would multiply the chrysin, DIM, Epimedium and ginkgo intake 2-5 times (inference).
- **Certification:** None found.
- NSF Certified for Sport: the only Designs for Health product in NSF's full list (read 8 Nov 2026) is Pro Greens + Reds, listing 1786415, not LibidoStim-M [https://www.nsfsport.com/certified-products/search-results.php?search=ashwagandha; https://www.nsfsport.com/certified-products/listing-detail.php?id=1786415].
- Informed Sport: text searches for 'libido' and 'tribulus' returned no products on 8 Nov 2026 [https://sport.wetestyoutrust.com/supplement-search?search=libido; https://sport.wetestyoutrust.com/supplement-search?search=tribulus]. (certifier page: none; verified in the certifier's database: no; verifier marked the programme as screening for hidden drugs: no — see the challenge notes, which found PDE5-drug screening confirmed only for BSCG)
- **Manufacturer and regulatory record:** Company: Designs for Health, a US practitioner-channel brand sold through Fullscript and Emerson Ecologics [https://fullscript.com/catalog/products/libidostim-m-60-vcaps; https://ftp.emersonecologics.com/products/detail/Designs-for-Health/libidostim-m-60-vcaps/U3ByZWU6OlByb2R1Y3QtNzI0NTk=/LSM060].
The brand advises users on prescription medication, with a health condition or worried about herb-drug interactions to consult a practitioner first [https://www.designsforhealth.com/products/libidostim-m].
None found, but not searched: no FDA warning-letter, recall or Health Fraud search, and no Health Canada, TGA or MHRA search, was run for Designs for Health in this workflow, and the project notes hold no record. fda.gov and the other regulator sites are blocked in this session and the shared web-search budget was used up. Treat the record as unknown, not clean.
- **Evidence summary:** A multi-herb drive formula whose evidence-bearing ingredients (Tribulus, tongkat ali) are at 20-50% of studied doses. Ginkgo failed even at 240 mg/day [https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/15378664/], Epimedium has no human trial [https://www.mayoclinic.org/diseases-conditions/erectile-dysfunction/in-depth/erectile-dysfunction-herbs/art-20044394], and tongkat ali has no erection benefit in its meta-analysis [https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/26365449/]. The discovery sweep described DIM and chrysin as estrogen- and aromatase-modulating additions; this was not verified, and no libido trial of either was found. The formula itself has never been trialled.

**Safety risks**

- Tribulus: rare severe liver and kidney injury [https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/38328764/]; rhabdomyolysis with transaminitis in a man on atorvastatin [https://amjcaserep.com/abstract/full/idArt/943492].
- Tongkat ali: isolated liver-injury reports (LiverTox; a 47-year-old man with jaundice) [https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/NBK609015/; https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/38646387/]. EFSA (2021) could not establish safety of a tongkat root extract, citing liver and kidney effects in a chronic toxicity study and possible DNA damage in animals [https://efsa.onlinelibrary.wiley.com/doi/10.2903/j.efsa.2021.6937; https://www.opss.org/article/tongkat-ali-uses-and-safety-dietary-supplements].
- Epimedium: tachyarrhythmia and hypomania, muscle spasms with raised CK, and acute hepatotoxicity case reports [https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/15546831/; https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC12182873/; https://smrj.scholasticahq.com/article/144607-herb-induced-liver-injury-a-case-of-acute-hepatotoxicity-associated-with-horny-goat-weed-use].
- Ginkgo 100 mg: rarely bleeding and irregular heartbeat [https://www.mayoclinic.org/diseases-conditions/erectile-dysfunction/in-depth/erectile-dysfunction-herbs/art-20044394].
- DIM and chrysin are hormone-targeting ingredients a buyer may not expect in a libido product; their effects in men were not researched this session (gap).
- The label differs by country and date, so the bottle may not match any listing.
- Not certified, so no batch screening for hidden drugs or contaminants.

**Interactions**

- Anticoagulants and antiplatelets: ginkgo bleeding risk [https://www.mayoclinic.org/diseases-conditions/erectile-dysfunction/in-depth/erectile-dysfunction-herbs/art-20044394].
- PDE5 inhibitors and nitrates: icariin's mild in-vitro PDE5 activity makes an additive effect theoretical; no clinical data (inference) [https://www.mskcc.org/cancer-care/integrative-medicine/herbs/epimedium; https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/18778098/].
- Statins: Tribulus-associated rhabdomyolysis case [https://amjcaserep.com/abstract/full/idArt/943492].
- Propranolol and CYP1A2 or CYP2A6 substrates: tongkat ali may lower propranolol levels (minor) and is flagged for these enzymes [https://examine.com/supplements/tongkat-ali/].
- Hormone therapies (testosterone, finasteride, anti-androgens, tamoxifen, aromatase inhibitors): DIM, chrysin and tongkat ali are hormone-targeting; no interaction data, so do not combine (inference).
- SSRIs, MAOIs and mood stabilisers: Epimedium is linked to hypomania; no direct study [https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC6043668/; https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/15546831/].
- Antihypertensives, diabetes drugs, thyroid drugs and sedatives: no interaction documented in the sources reviewed.

**Who should avoid it**

- Men with liver or kidney disease [https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/38328764/; https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/NBK609015/]
- Men on anticoagulants or antiplatelets, or due for surgery [https://www.mayoclinic.org/diseases-conditions/erectile-dysfunction/in-depth/erectile-dysfunction-herbs/art-20044394]
- Men taking statins [https://amjcaserep.com/abstract/full/idArt/943492]
- Men with prostate cancer or another hormone-sensitive condition, or on hormone therapy (inference from the hormone-targeting ingredients)
- Men with heart-rhythm problems or bipolar disorder [https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/15546831/; https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC6043668/]
- Drug-tested athletes (tongkat ali WADA caution) [https://examine.com/supplements/tongkat-ali/]

**Stop signals**

- Jaundice, dark urine, itching or right-upper abdominal pain [https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/38646387/; https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/38328764/]
- Muscle pain or weakness with dark urine [https://amjcaserep.com/abstract/full/idArt/943492; https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC12182873/]
- Palpitations or an irregular heartbeat [https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/15546831/; https://www.mayoclinic.org/diseases-conditions/erectile-dysfunction/in-depth/erectile-dysfunction-herbs/art-20044394]
- Unusual bleeding or bruising [https://www.mayoclinic.org/diseases-conditions/erectile-dysfunction/in-depth/erectile-dysfunction-herbs/art-20044394]
- Breast tenderness or swelling, or marked mood change (possible hormonal effect, inference)

### Avoid list: Puresport (UK) — Ultra Ashwagandha KSM-66, capsules

- **Verifier verdict:** avoid — Its main advantage, Informed Sport batch testing, was withdrawn on 10 Oct 2026 and its dose could not be verified, so a currently NSF-listed KSM-66 600 mg (for example Core Med Science, NSF listing 1778870) is the better pick until Puresport is relisted and its label confirmed [https://www.nsfsport.com/certified-products/listing-detail.php?id=1778870].
- **Product as verified:** Puresport (UK) — Ultra Ashwagandha KSM-66, capsules
- **Product page:** https://puresport.co/products/ultra-ashwagandha
- **Where sold:** UK brand site and an Australian storefront [https://puresport.co/products/ultra-ashwagandha; https://puresport.co/en-au/products/ultra-ashwagandha]. UK retailer XMiles, which has EU-locale storefronts, listed it in its 'Informed Sport certified' collection in a search result seen on 8 Nov 2026 [https://xmiles.co.uk/en-se/collections/informed-sport-certified/support_stress]; that label is now out of date. While certified, Informed Sport listed availability in Africa, Asia, Europe, North America, Oceania and South America [https://sport.wetestyoutrust.com/supplement-search/puresport/ultra-ashwagandha-ksm-66]. Not legal in Danish food supplements [https://nutraingredients.com/Article/2023/05/17/Growing-concerns-over-safety-of-ashwagandha-in-EU-member-states]. Gulf: not verified.
- **Label verified:** no. Not retrieved. No agent in this workflow read the Supplement Facts: puresport.co is outside this session's allowed network, the shared web-search budget was used up, and the Informed Sport listing shows only jar and box images [https://sport.wetestyoutrust.com/supplement-search/puresport/ultra-ashwagandha-ksm-66].
- **Proprietary blend:** no
- **Directions and daily amounts:** NOT VERIFIED. The name says KSM-66, a root-only ashwagandha extract, in capsule form [https://sport.wetestyoutrust.com/supplement-search/puresport/ultra-ashwagandha-ksm-66]. The mg per capsule, serving size, directions and any added ingredients (for example black pepper) are unknown. Read the label before use; the proprietary-blend flag below is unconfirmed.
- **Dose versus trials (unknown):** KSM-66 trials used 300 mg twice daily (600 mg/day) for 8 weeks: Chauhan 2022 in men with low desire (DISF-M improved, testosterone rose) and Khanna 2026 in healthy men [https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/35873404/; https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/41766918/]. The hormone meta-analysis found testosterone +57 ng/dL in men, with high heterogeneity [https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/41740946/]. This product's daily amount is unknown, so no comparison is possible.
- **Certification:** Informed Sport (drug-screening tier), now WITHDRAWN. Read from Informed Sport's own database on 8 Nov 2026 (live page, not cache): 'This product is no longer certified. Please contact the brand for further information.' Date certified 29-Jun-2023; date withdrawn 10-Oct-2026; 'No batches are currently associated with this product' [https://sport.wetestyoutrust.com/supplement-search/puresport/ultra-ashwagandha-ksm-66].
- The discovery note of current batches (batch BN6647 to 31-Dec-2026) came from an older search-index snapshot and is out of date.
- Withdrawn products still show in Informed Sport's search results, so appearing in a search is not proof of certification [https://sport.wetestyoutrust.com/supplement-search?search=ashwagandha].
- Puresport's Tongkat Ali was withdrawn the same day, which points to a brand-level change rather than a failed test (inference; no reason is given) [https://sport.wetestyoutrust.com/supplement-search/puresport/tongkat-ali].
- While certified, Informed Sport tested every batch for 285+ banned substances before release [https://sport.wetestyoutrust.com/about/sport-vs-choice]. (certifier page: https://sport.wetestyoutrust.com/supplement-search/puresport/ultra-ashwagandha-ksm-66; verified in the certifier's database: no; verifier marked the programme as screening for hidden drugs: no — see the challenge notes, which found PDE5-drug screening confirmed only for BSCG)
- **Manufacturer and regulatory record:** Company: Puresport, a UK sports-nutrition brand [https://puresport.co/products/ultra-ashwagandha].
Key record point: both of its Informed Sport certifications were withdrawn on 10 Oct 2026 [https://sport.wetestyoutrust.com/supplement-search/puresport/ultra-ashwagandha-ksm-66; https://sport.wetestyoutrust.com/supplement-search/puresport/tongkat-ali].
None found, but not searched: no FDA, MHRA, UK FSA, TGA or Health Canada search was run for Puresport in this workflow, and the project notes hold no record. Those sites are blocked in this session and the shared web-search budget was used up.
- **Evidence summary:** KSM-66 at 600 mg/day is the trial-matched form for Performer 8's drive role: two 8-week RCTs in men showed better desire scores and higher testosterone [https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/35873404/; https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/41766918/]. NCCIH calls the testosterone and sperm-quality evidence limited, with some evidence for stress and sleep [https://www.nccih.nih.gov/health/ashwagandha]. Whether this product delivers 600 mg/day is unknown.

**Safety risks**

- Liver: LiverTox recognises ashwagandha as a cause of clinically apparent liver injury [https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/NBK548536/]. In the Bjornsson 2020 five-case series, jaundice, itching and nausea began 2-12 weeks after starting; injury was cholestatic or mixed and liver tests normalised in 1-5 months [https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/31991029/]. NCCIH: rare, but a number of cases link liver injury to ashwagandha [https://www.nccih.nih.gov/health/ashwagandha].
- Thyroid: pooled RCTs show a small T4 rise (+0.61 µg/dL) without TSH change [https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/41740946/]; NCCIH advises against use with thyroid or autoimmune disorders [https://www.nccih.nih.gov/health/ashwagandha].
- Drowsiness, stomach upset, diarrhoea and vomiting can occur; safety is shown only for short-term use (up to 3 months) [https://www.nccih.nih.gov/health/ashwagandha].
- Hormonal: it may raise testosterone, so NCCIH says men with hormone-sensitive prostate cancer should avoid it [https://www.nccih.nih.gov/health/ashwagandha].
- Regulatory: banned in Danish food supplements since 2023 over possible thyroid and sex-hormone effects [https://nutraingredients.com/Article/2023/05/17/Growing-concerns-over-safety-of-ashwagandha-in-EU-member-states]; the Netherlands declared a ban in early 2026, with the EU standstill period running to 7 Dec 2026 [https://www.nutraingredients.com/Article/2026/07/06/timeline-will-authorities-de-throne-the-king-of-herbs/].
- Do not stack with Performer 8 (it already contains KSM-66 ashwagandha) or any other ashwagandha product [https://www.usatoday.com/press-release/story/43980/performer-8-reviews-wolfson-brands-announces-u-s-launch-of-current-11-ingredient-mens-wellness-formula/].
- No current batch screening: the Informed Sport certification lapsed on 10 Oct 2026, so bottles from later batches are untested.

**Interactions**

- Thyroid hormone (levothyroxine): possible interaction per NCCIH [https://www.nccih.nih.gov/health/ashwagandha].
- Sedatives (benzodiazepines, sleep drugs, alcohol) and anticonvulsants: possible interaction per NCCIH [https://www.nccih.nih.gov/health/ashwagandha].
- Antihypertensives: possible interaction per NCCIH; watch for dizziness [https://www.nccih.nih.gov/health/ashwagandha].
- Diabetes drugs: possible interaction per NCCIH; monitor glucose [https://www.nccih.nih.gov/health/ashwagandha].
- Immunosuppressants: possible interaction per NCCIH [https://www.nccih.nih.gov/health/ashwagandha].
- Nitrates and PDE5 inhibitors: no documented interaction and none studied; because NCCIH lists blood-pressure medicines, watch for light-headedness (inference) [https://www.nccih.nih.gov/health/ashwagandha].
- Anticoagulants/antiplatelets and SSRIs/MAOIs: no interaction documented in the sources reviewed [https://www.nccih.nih.gov/health/ashwagandha].

**Who should avoid it**

- Men with thyroid disease or on thyroid medication [https://www.nccih.nih.gov/health/ashwagandha]
- Men with autoimmune disease or on immunosuppressants [https://www.nccih.nih.gov/health/ashwagandha]
- Men with hormone-sensitive prostate cancer [https://www.nccih.nih.gov/health/ashwagandha]
- Anyone with liver disease or an earlier herb-induced liver injury [https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/NBK548536/]
- Anyone due for surgery, or taking sedatives or anticonvulsants [https://www.nccih.nih.gov/health/ashwagandha]
- Anyone still taking Performer 8 or another ashwagandha product [https://www.usatoday.com/press-release/story/43980/performer-8-reviews-wolfson-brands-announces-u-s-launch-of-current-11-ingredient-mens-wellness-formula/]

**Stop signals**

- Jaundice, dark urine, pale stools, itching, nausea or right-upper abdominal pain, especially in weeks 2-12: stop and get liver tests [https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/31991029/]
- Palpitations, tremor, heat intolerance or weight loss, which could be a thyroid effect (inference) [https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/41740946/]
- Marked drowsiness, vomiting or diarrhoea [https://www.nccih.nih.gov/health/ashwagandha]
- No benefit after 8 weeks (the trial length): stop rather than continue indefinitely [https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/35873404/]
